# Symptom index

One line per symptom in the [subsystem references](../reference/): what you see (an assertion, a `[RED]` line, an abort or crash site, a visible result) and the entry that explains it. Search this page for the text in your log first (`grep -i` the function name, the `[RED]` text or the address form), then read the entry and the game notes it names. Generated by `dm/tools/docs_symptoms.py` from the entries' Symptom bullets: change the entry, then run it.

## Packing

- `no base game, only update packages: pack the base game and its update into one NUP`, even when the update carries the complete game's files (issue #1195). → [A NUP with complete standalone updates stops at "no base game"](../reference/packing.md#a-nup-with-complete-standalone-updates-stops-at-no-base-game)
- the game reloads one asset for ever: a background loader takes about a core and the process several (Ocarina of Time: 412% CPU on the Mac, `OptimizeChangeBanc` and `ParallelDecompThread` busy all session, one model and its texture opened about 22 times a second). Or, from the packing script: `vN: K file(s) whose bytes do not start as their names promise (a .zs file that is not a zstd stream), so the game files are damaged`, then the files. → [A damaged file in a NUP or a dump has no hash to fail](../reference/packing.md#a-damaged-file-in-a-nup-or-a-dump-has-no-hash-to-fail)
- `pack_nup.py`, or the packing script on a NUP, ends in a Python traceback (`RecursionError` in `tree_bytes`) instead of a `[RED]` line. → [A NUP whose folder contains itself](../reference/packing.md#a-nup-whose-folder-contains-itself)
- Finder shows `Version 1.0` for a Mac app whose game is at another version (this game's NUP holds update v131072, 1.1.1), and the game asks the host for its version (`nn::oe::GetDisplayVersion`) and gets `v131072`. → [An app packed without control data says version 1.0](../reference/packing.md#an-app-packed-without-control-data-says-version-10)
- after the packing script was stopped in Spyder (its interrupt), or by a front end's SIGTERM, the tool it was running went on: a shader build kept the CPU busy and wrote into the build folder that the next build reads, and the script could wait for it with no end. A regression run (`regress.py`) stopped during a shader build whose direct child ignored SIGTERM for 10 s left the build's workers running. A tool's child still writing after the tool's own exit was taken for a finished step. → [Stopping a build left its tools running](../reference/packing.md#stopping-a-build-left-its-tools-running)
- after the terminal running the packing script or `regress.py` was closed, the tool the script was running (a shader build, a game under the harness) went on running with no parent, writing into the build folder; the script itself had gone without its cleanup. → [Closing the terminal left a build's tools running](../reference/packing.md#closing-the-terminal-left-a-builds-tools-running)
- none seen on a game. An interrupt, a SIGTERM or a failed delete while the packing script replaced one of its outputs (the rewritten modules, the shader package) could leave that output half deleted at its real path, and a reuse check that looks for a few files could take it for a finished output on the next run. → [Stopping a build while it replaced an output left that output half deleted](../reference/packing.md#stopping-a-build-while-it-replaced-an-output-left-that-output-half-deleted)
- in nxnative, before running the packing script, a player had to install the .NET SDK 10, and builds then failed in ways that were about .NET, not the game: a translator that finds no runtime of its architecture (GitHub issue #54, #248, The shader step stops with an empty `xlate_source`), a Homebrew runtime the apphost cannot find, a dll tied to one architecture (#803). GitHub issue #1853 asked for the tools to be rewritten in Python or Objective-C. → [Building needed .NET, and finding its runtime kept failing](../reference/packing.md#building-needed-net-and-finding-its-runtime-kept-failing)
- on macOS 26 the packing script stops before unpacking anything with `[RED] the shader translator does not run (exit code -11)`, and `dmsh_xlate --source-sha` exits 139: `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` at address 0 with pc 0, called from `dmsh_xlate +0x5adc`. On macOS 27, and under `arch -x86_64`, it runs. → [The committed translator crashes at address 0 on macOS 26](../reference/packing.md#the-committed-translator-crashes-at-address-0-on-macos-26)
- on a Mac without Nix (GitHub's runners, a player's), the packing script stops before unpacking anything with `[RED] the shader translator does not run (exit code -6)`, and `dmsh_xlate` aborts in dyld: `Library not loaded: /nix/store/…-brotli-1.2.0-lib/lib/libbrotlienc.1.dylib`. The tool tests that run the translator fail with the same message, and `test_native_tools.py` reads an empty `--source-sha`. Under `arch -x86_64`, and on the Mac that built it, it runs. → [The committed translator needs a `/nix/store` brotli](../reference/packing.md#the-committed-translator-needs-a-nixstore-brotli)
- the game unpacks, then the shader step stops with `translation chain tools do not match the lock file: xlate_source: lock … / actual` and nothing after `actual`, which reads like a stale translator. → [The shader step stops with an empty `xlate_source`](../reference/packing.md#the-shader-step-stops-with-an-empty-xlate_source)
- none at the time: after an edit under `dm/third_party/ryujinx` without rebuilding `dmsh_xlate`, `tool_lock.py` and `test_native_tools.py` passed, and the shaders were translated by the old code. → [A translator built from older Ryujinx sources passed the tool check](../reference/packing.md#a-translator-built-from-older-ryujinx-sources-passed-the-tool-check)
- the shader split stops with `the split shader tables differ from game profile <id>'s`, on the game version the profile registers. → [The split shader tables differ from a game profile's](../reference/packing.md#the-split-shader-tables-differ-from-a-game-profiles)
- `dmsh_build.py` stops with `[RED] <package> holds no record of shader <key>, which the shader map names: the package and the map come from different shader_split.py runs`, with `(its records carry no keys: it was split before they did)` for a package from before chain `3df44a5a4e0c`. Before that chain: wrong shaders wherever the game drew, such as a black screen with sound. → [The shader package and the shader map come from different splits](../reference/packing.md#the-shader-package-and-the-shader-map-come-from-different-splits)
- a build that restores every shader from the compile cache still spends about a minute and a half after the last shader, with the progress line at 99%, before `dmsh_build.py` prints `c1_full.tsv: …` and `cb_extent.tsv: …`: 95 s for Ocarina of Time's 42,848 shaders on an M3 Max. → [A reuse build spends a minute and a half on the c1 and uniform extent tables](../reference/packing.md#a-reuse-build-spends-a-minute-and-a-half-on-the-c1-and-uniform-extent-tables)
- a build's last stage shows `Mac app · game files` without `(reused, signed)` and signs for three minutes, though the game files did not change and the build before it showed `game files (reused, signed)`; a build that changed nothing still shows `Mac app · pipeline archive` and signs for half a minute. Ocarina of Time (a 36 GB app) on an M3 Max: `Mac app` took 3:33 in the build after one where it took 1:11, and four packs that each changed one small file took 8 s, 220 s, 8 s and 220 s. → [Every second build signs the whole Mac app again](../reference/packing.md#every-second-build-signs-the-whole-mac-app-again)
- a build whose every stage says `reused (inputs unchanged)` still takes 1:45 (Ocarina of Time on an M3 Max): 22 s between `unpack` and `rewrite`, 12 s in `Mac shaders · checking the inputs`, 26 s in `Pipeline archive`, 39 s in `Mac app`. → [A build that changes nothing takes a minute and three quarters](../reference/packing.md#a-build-that-changes-nothing-takes-a-minute-and-three-quarters)
- after a full shader build that restored every shader, the app step packs `c1_full.tsv` and `cb_extent.tsv` again though their rows are the same. → [A full shader build that changed no shader gives the app new tables](../reference/packing.md#a-full-shader-build-that-changed-no-shader-gives-the-app-new-tables)
- a build after a chain change that left the MSL as it was (`Metal cache: mac 55482 reused, 0 compiled`) spends about a minute in `writing the library image` and a minute and a half in the c1 and uniform extent tables, and its app step half a minute in `shader libraries` (Ocarina of Time, 42,848 shaders: 0:56, 1:26 and 0:25 on an M3 Max). → [After a chain change the steps behind the last shader take three minutes](../reference/packing.md#after-a-chain-change-the-steps-behind-the-last-shader-take-three-minutes)
- the shader step's progress stops just short of its total (42,811 of 42,848 for Ocarina of Time in nxnative, every shader from the cache) and stays there through the library image and the two tables, which looks like a hang. → [The shader step stands at 99% after the last shader](../reference/packing.md#the-shader-step-stands-at-99-after-the-last-shader)
- a shader build stops with an uncaught `KeyError` in `Store._reader` while the damaged compile cache is being recreated. → [Cache recovery races a parallel shader reader](../reference/packing.md#cache-recovery-races-a-parallel-shader-reader)
- replacing a shader-library image with different bytes of the same size while preserving its modification time leaves the old pipeline archive reused without reading the changed libraries. → [A changed shader library reuses an old pipeline archive](../reference/packing.md#a-changed-shader-library-reuses-an-old-pipeline-archive)
- the packing script stops with `[RED] less than N GB free on the volume of <build folder>` before it unpacks the game, while Finder and the Storage settings show far more available. The Mac app step can stop with `the app needs … GB on the volume of …`, and `pso_offline.py` with `GB free on the volume of …, metal-tt needs about …` or `GB free before merging`. → [The free-space check stops a build on a Mac that has the space](../reference/packing.md#the-free-space-check-stops-a-build-on-a-mac-that-has-the-space)
- in the NativeNavi app, the steps stop at "Installing the Rust compiler" (ticked), the line under the bar keeps rustup's last message (`info: you may opt out with RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0 …`), and Details shows nothing of the packing script, while `build_app.py` runs and writes its logs in the build folder (`logs/dmsh_build.log`, `shader/progress.json` counting). A few minutes into the shader step the script's progress thread blocks in `write()` on its standard output, holding the progress lock, and the build stops for good when the stage ends. → [NativeNavi stays on Installing the Rust compiler while the game builds](../reference/packing.md#nativenavi-stays-on-installing-the-rust-compiler-while-the-game-builds)
- the first build after restarting the Mac spends minutes in `unpack` (`[1/5] unpack v131072: 5%, 1.2/~24.4 GB`) although the game, the packer and the image are as they were: 8 minutes for a 24.4 GB image from a NUP on a network share. → [A reboot packs a RomFS image again](../reference/packing.md#a-reboot-packs-a-romfs-image-again)
- the first build after restarting the Mac runs `Mac shaders` again (a cache check of every shader, the library image written again) though nothing changed. → [A reboot runs the shader step again](../reference/packing.md#a-reboot-runs-the-shader-step-again)
- the next build spends minutes unpacking a NUP after a packer update even though its complete `RomFS.img` is still present. → [An old NUP cache stamp makes the build unpack again](../reference/packing.md#an-old-nup-cache-stamp-makes-the-build-unpack-again)
- a build after `git checkout` to another commit and back, or from another worktree of the same repository, with the translator's bytes as they were, translates every shader again: `building the whole shader map, 42,848 shaders` and 46 minutes for Ocarina of Time's Mac shaders, all from the compile cache, where `reused (inputs unchanged)` was expected. → [A commit switched and back, or another worktree, rebuilds every shader](../reference/packing.md#a-commit-switched-and-back-or-another-worktree-rebuilds-every-shader)
- a direct `dmhost` run logs `fs mount rom: → <path> (read-only)` for a path that does not exist, then crashes in the game's code a fraction of a second after `nnMain` (Echoes of Wisdom: `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x88` 0.3 s in). The host now stops at the mount instead with `FATAL the game files <path> (rom:) are missing: neither a folder nor a RomFS image`. → [A stale `DM_ROMFS` path crashes the game soon after `nnMain`](../reference/packing.md#a-stale-dm_romfs-path-crashes-the-game-soon-after-nnmain)
- packing a game's RomFS as one image still created thousands of resource files on the Mac first, and the build's scanners required the expanded folder. → [Building creates a full RomFS tree before packing its image](../reference/packing.md#building-creates-a-full-romfs-tree-before-packing-its-image)
- a build that runs the shader step writes the compile cache's files back and removes them again, on Skyward Sword HD 24,131 files and 10,150 folders each time, Tears of the Kingdom over 900,000 entries. Removing old entries took up to half an hour on an external drive. → [The compile cache was unpacked for each build](../reference/packing.md#the-compile-cache-was-unpacked-for-each-build)
- Ocarina of Time's `shader/cache/cas.db` occupies 40.8 GB: 30.4 GB of unique Mac and iOS Metal compilations, two complete generations of 42,848 per-shader records, variants and old staged outputs. Some results are useful for regression tests, but pruning old regression chain libraries does not reclaim their cache entries. → [Regression shader compilations grow the normal build cache](../reference/packing.md#regression-shader-compilations-grow-the-normal-build-cache)
- Ocarina of Time's `run/pso_archive` held 13.9 GB in 99 files: 64 indexed by library file names (5.3 GB, about 33,700 pipelines, against a prewarm of under 10,000) and 35 with no index (8.4 GB) from the first week. What a build or a run no longer used was deleted in place by some steps, kept by others, and could be told apart from what was in use only by a tool. → [Unused files had no one place in the build folder](../reference/packing.md#unused-files-had-no-one-place-in-the-build-folder)
- Ocarina of Time's `shader/cache/cas.db` grew to 52 GB, of which its current Mac library used 10.3 GB: about seven generations of Mac Metal compiles (31.4 GB), one of iOS (5.3 GB), three sets of 42,848 per-shader entries (one of them in use), the variants nothing reads since c8bfa161 (2.1 GB) and stopped builds' staging (1.0 GB). What a development build of another chain left could not be told apart or removed without a tool. → [Old chains' compiles stayed in the one compile cache file](../reference/packing.md#old-chains-compiles-stayed-in-the-one-compile-cache-file)
- the first build of a game whose build folder is on an exFAT drive stops at its shader step, before any shader is compiled: `dmsh_build.py` fails opening `shader/cache/cas.db` with `sqlite3.OperationalError: attempt to write a readonly database` at `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` in `shader_cache.Store._open`, and the step ends with `[RED] own_compilers.py exit code 1`. Running the build again goes past it, as the failed run has left the file 16 KB long. → [A new compile cache on an exFAT drive is "a readonly database"](../reference/packing.md#a-new-compile-cache-on-an-exfat-drive-is-a-readonly-database)
- in a new build folder, a `--seen-shaders` build works, then the next full build stops in the shader step with `NotADirectoryError: … shader/cache/b-<chain>.img`. → [A full build after a partial one stops with NotADirectoryError](../reference/packing.md#a-full-build-after-a-partial-one-stops-with-notadirectoryerror)
- a game's shader libraries are slow to copy or delete: Skyward Sword HD's `b-<chain>/` held 12,064 files for 2,470 shaders, a third of its build folder's files, and each `regress.py` chain build held another set. A tar would not do: the host reads these files at every run. → [Shader libraries take a file per shader variant](../reference/packing.md#shader-libraries-take-a-file-per-shader-variant)
- the Mac app logs `[RED] shader library …/Contents/Resources/msl/b-<chain> (a folder or a .img file) does not exist` and then `failed to load shader`. Its `msl/` holds `b-<chain>.img.img`, and its `Info.plist` records `DMChain` as `<chain>.img`. → [A Mac app packed with the library's image names it `b-<chain>.img.img`](../reference/packing.md#a-mac-app-packed-with-the-librarys-image-names-it-b-imgimg)
- a game's build folder holds tens of thousands of small files while its shaders build, and keeps them when the build stops: Octopath Traveler 0's `_build`, stopped in its compile, held 68,230 files (38,376 in `tmp/dmsh_*`, 29,757 in `shader/cache/b-<chain>/`), against 105 once a build finished. Every later build made another `tmp/dmsh_*` beside the old one. → [A shader build leaves a file per shader output while it runs or after a stop](../reference/packing.md#a-shader-build-leaves-a-file-per-shader-output-while-it-runs-or-after-a-stop)
- after a shader build on a nearly full drive (one `[RED] the shader compile cache … cannot be written` line), the library image lacks some outputs of a shader that built: the host finds no reflection or library for it. → [A full drive during a shader build drops finished outputs from the library](../reference/packing.md#a-full-drive-during-a-shader-build-drops-finished-outputs-from-the-library)
- a game's `<id>-work` folder is too slow to count: Tears of the Kingdom's held 8,893,028 files and 509 GB, and its count ran past ten minutes. → [A work folder holds millions of small files](../reference/packing.md#a-work-folder-holds-millions-of-small-files)
- the packing script stops at the shader split of a registered profile with `[RED] 13744 file(s) could not be read, their shaders are left out (first: AI/FileEntry/FileEntry.Product.100.byml.zs: CalledProcessError: Command ['zstd', '-d', '-c', '-q', '…/extract/v0/RomFS.img/AI/…'] returned non-zero exit status 1.)`. → [The zstd command given a path inside the image](../reference/packing.md#the-zstd-command-given-a-path-inside-the-image)
- a game version the profile registers, packed with fewer DLC (or none, or other ones) than the profile was split with, stops at the shader split with `the split shader tables differ from game profile <id>'s`, though the packing script should derive a DLC-only profile for it (`the installed DLC shader content differs from the registered tables: using a shader-only derived profile`). → [A registered build without the profile's DLC stops at the shader split](../reference/packing.md#a-registered-build-without-the-profiles-dlc-stops-at-the-shader-split)
- the game shows `?` where it shows its version (Super Mario Bros. Wonder's title screen: `Ver. ?`), and the build's lines name the version as `v327680 (?)`. → [A game packed from a NUP or a dump shows "Ver. ?" for a build its profile registers](../reference/packing.md#a-game-packed-from-a-nup-or-a-dump-shows-ver--for-a-build-its-profile-registers)
- the settings of a Mac app packed from a NUP have no Game language row, while `DM_LANG` in the environment changes the language. Reported on Donkey Kong Bananza (NX2Apple#619, #659; the reporters' packed files were not available). → [No Game language row in an app packed from a NUP or a dump](../reference/packing.md#no-game-language-row-in-an-app-packed-from-a-nup-or-a-dump)
- starting a Mac app through a front end with unchanged settings enables the encode thread, and can disable Metal 4 pipelines and the pipeline archive; starting the same app from Finder keeps the host's defaults. → [A Mac front end's default settings change Metal execution paths](../reference/packing.md#a-mac-front-ends-default-settings-change-metal-execution-paths)
- in a Mac app, the settings window stays on screen after **Play**, not responding (a spinning cursor over it), until the game ends. → [The Mac settings window stays on screen after Play](../reference/packing.md#the-mac-settings-window-stays-on-screen-after-play)
- with the build folder on an exFAT or FAT drive, the shader step compiles every library and then stops in `c1_guard.py` with `UnicodeDecodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't decode byte …` (dmsh_build.py: `[RED] c1_guard.py failed`), reading a `._<key>_b.metal`. → [The c1 guard stops on an AppleDouble file after the shader build](../reference/packing.md#the-c1-guard-stops-on-an-appledouble-file-after-the-shader-build)
- with the build folder or the app on an exFAT or FAT drive, the Mac app step stops in `pack_app.py`. The first form it takes is `no input file at <build>/shader/cache/<chain>/._<key>_b.metallib`, or `no rewriter fixups for <build>/<version>/._main.patched.bin` when the modules come first. When the inputs get past it, the copies stop with `chflags` or `Permission denied`, and the signing step stops with `codesign failed (1): code object is not signed at all` and `In subcomponent: …/Contents/MacOS/._nativenavi`, which reads like a broken launcher build. → [The Mac app step stops on AppleDouble files in its inputs, its copies and its signature](../reference/packing.md#the-mac-app-step-stops-on-appledouble-files-in-its-inputs-its-copies-and-its-signature)
- with the app on a Mac OS Extended, exFAT or FAT drive, the first build into a folder works and the next one that changes anything stops in `pack_app.py` `replace_app` with `OSError: [Errno 45] Operation not supported: '<app>'`. The old app stays in place. → [A second Mac app build stops with `[Errno 45]` when it replaces the old app](../reference/packing.md#a-second-mac-app-build-stops-with-errno-45-when-it-replaces-the-old-app)
- on an exFAT or FAT drive, shader splitting stops with `ValueError: not a version 2 shader code archive` or `[RED] /glslc_capture/._…: no GLSLC output`. → [External shader directories contain AppleDouble metadata](../reference/packing.md#external-shader-directories-contain-appledouble-metadata)
- the packing script or any module tool stops on `main`: `compressed with zstd (NSO flag bit 7), which this loader does not decode yet`; with the decoder but without its package, `ZBIC (zstd) segments need the zbic package (python3 -m pip install zbic)`. → [ZBIC-compressed NSO segments](../reference/packing.md#zbic-compressed-nso-segments)
- `FATAL open <folder>/subsdk1.fixups.tsv` right after the host starts, for a version that has no such module. → [A version without one of the profile's modules stops the host at boot](../reference/packing.md#a-version-without-one-of-the-profiles-modules-stops-the-host-at-boot)
- the build stops with `no NUP (a .nup file) in <folder>` for a folder holding `Game.NUP`, on any Mac volume; renaming it to `.nup` makes the build go on. → [A game file whose extension is in capitals is not found](../reference/packing.md#a-game-file-whose-extension-is-in-capitals-is-not-found)
- a version a profile was not brought up on crashes at its first movie: `UNIMPL _ZN5movie9ExtractorC1ENS_13ContainerTypeE`, then `CRASH sig=11` (issue #1211). The derived profile's `modules` has only `main sdk`. → [A derived profile leaves out a module that moved to another slot](../reference/packing.md#a-derived-profile-leaves-out-a-module-that-moved-to-another-slot)
- the packing script stops at its rewrite stage with `FileNotFoundError: … extract/v0/ExeFS/subsdk1` while packing a version the profile was not brought up on; the extraction has every other module. → [A derived profile loads only the modules its version ships](../reference/packing.md#a-derived-profile-loads-only-the-modules-its-version-ships)
- an app of a version the profile was not brought up on prepares far fewer shaders before its first frame (the progress screen's Preparing shaders, the log's `pipeline prewarm list … lines` and `shader keys not in the shader map (another game version)`) and compiles more of them while it plays, from the first scenes on. → [A derived profile's prewarm list names another version's shaders](../reference/packing.md#a-derived-profiles-prewarm-list-names-another-versions-shaders)
- an app of Ocarina of Time 1.0.0 has no Practice section in its settings window; the packing script said `[RED] practice tools: … is not in this build: the host's check logged 4 red line(s), the first: [dm] [RED] forced takeover entry no module references: …`. → [The practice tools' check failed on the game's own red lines](../reference/packing.md#the-practice-tools-check-failed-on-the-games-own-red-lines)
- every build with `--allow-damaged` runs the shader split and restores every shader from the compile cache, though nothing changed: about 56 s of split and 9½ minutes of shaders (42,848, about 75 a second) for Ocarina of Time on an M3 Max, each build. → [A build that allows damaged files splits and restores every shader again](../reference/packing.md#a-build-that-allows-damaged-files-splits-and-restores-every-shader-again)

## Rewriter

- `NameError: name 'CS_ARCH_ARM64' is not defined` at the start of `patch_nso.py`; now `[RED] Capstone 6.… is unsupported` from the packing script or `patch_nso.py`. → [Capstone API version](../reference/rewriter.md#capstone-api-version)
- a build made from a checkout or folder under a name such as `Game [0400486024DEC000][v131072]` silently loses the repository's per-game cuts, observation gates and frame-step stubs, although its build ID is registered; no error, as the SDK patch file, named directly, still loads. Under the same kind of folder, `nso_match.py --registered` finds no cuts, and `nso_syms.host_hooks` reads no host hooks, so a derived profile's module list (`main_modules` in `scripts/build_app.py`) is worked out without them. → [Repository patches missing under bracketed paths](../reference/rewriter.md#repository-patches-missing-under-bracketed-paths)
- an updated game loses a needed cut because its NSO build ID changed, although the relevant code still matches a verified version; the rewriter reports `[RED] function patch ... skipped` when that match cannot be established, and the packing script ends with `[RED] main: no function patch carries the registered builds' fixes to this build` or `[RED] main: function patches not applied` (Super Smash Bros. Ultimate 13.0.1: a static-initialiser abort in `nninitStartup`, GitHub issue #375). → [Function-matched cuts for unregistered builds](../reference/rewriter.md#function-matched-cuts-for-unregistered-builds)
- a game that steps its time by a fixed amount per frame needs the host's frame step (the frame step), but its stubs could only be keyed by the main's build ID: every update needed a new port, and the binding page listed each such patch as `build` bound, blocked for want of a function-matched form. → [Frame-step stubs placed by their function](../reference/rewriter.md#frame-step-stubs-placed-by-their-function)
- a local timing correction needs extra instructions, but declaring a frame-step state page would also change the host's pacing for a game with a native engine frame-rate setting. → [Function-matched ordinary stubs](../reference/rewriter.md#function-matched-ordinary-stubs)
- the rewrite of a main that gets frame step stubs (`FRAME_STEP` or a `MATCH_FRAME_STEP` match) stops with `AssertionError: frame step state page`; the same main rewrites without the stubs. → [A frame step state page where the patch area starts](../reference/rewriter.md#a-frame-step-state-page-where-the-patch-area-starts)
- a crash (`sig=11`) in the patch area a few seconds into a run, at an `x18` site's load, with an address a little above a small stale value (`addr=0x33220`, the thread's `K18` slot at `0x33200`), shortly after the game read `tpidr_el0` into `x18`. → [System register accesses through x18](../reference/rewriter.md#system-register-accesses-through-x18)
- `jump table <addr>: <reason>` assertion in `patch_nso.py`, the reason being what could not be proved (`the index is not bounded`, `the table base is not a constant`, `a target is not an instruction of the function`, `not a table`, …), or `jump table <addr>: an entry from another function is not proved`. → [Jump tables proved over control flow](../reference/rewriter.md#jump-tables-proved-over-control-flow)
- `jump table 2c0a220: the index reaches 0xffffffffffffffff` stops the rewrite. → [A guard on a wrapping 32-bit jump-table index](../reference/rewriter.md#a-guard-on-a-wrapping-32-bit-jump-table-index)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py`, in a Unity / IL2CPP `main`. → [Offset jump tables through x18 read at rewrite time](../reference/rewriter.md#offset-jump-tables-through-x18-read-at-rewrite-time)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py`, in a `subsdk`. → [Offset jump tables with the bounds check on another register](../reference/rewriter.md#offset-jump-tables-with-the-bounds-check-on-another-register)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion where the index is a copy made before the check (`mov w0,w18`, then 41 instructions holding two other jump tables on `x0`, then `cmp w18,#4; b.hi; ldrsw x18,[x11,x0,lsl #2]; add x18,x18,x11; br x18`). → [Index copied before the bounds check](../reference/rewriter.md#index-copied-before-the-bounds-check)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py` on an offset table whose index is checked with a 64-bit compare (`cmp x22, #0x10; …; b.hi; adrp x18 + add x18; mov x0, x18; ldrsw x18, [x0, x22, lsl #2]; add x18, x18, x0; br x18`). → [64-bit bounds check of the index](../reference/rewriter.md#64-bit-bounds-check-of-the-index)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py` where the index is made with a 64-bit `and` by `0xffffffff` after the check (`cmp w8, #4; b.hi; and x18, x8, #0xffffffff; ldrsw x18, [x16, x18, lsl #2]; add x18, x18, x16; br x18`). → [Index zero-extended after a 32-bit bounds check](../reference/rewriter.md#index-zero-extended-after-a-32-bit-bounds-check)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py` on an offset table whose bound comes from a bit test, a complemented or zero-extended mask, a mask whose highest value branches away (`and w18, w17, #7; cmp w18, #7; b.eq`), or a `cmp` more than 16 instructions before the `br`. → [Guarded dispatch tails](../reference/rewriter.md#guarded-dispatch-tails)
- `jump table 590ee8` assertion in `patch_nso.py` on ULTRA STREET FIGHTER II: The Final Challengers 1.1.1 (SDK 1.3.1). → [Bit-test bounded masked jump-table index](../reference/rewriter.md#bit-test-bounded-masked-jump-table-index)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py` on an offset table whose index is masked before a bit test of the mask's source, with no compare (`and w13, w17, #7; tbnz w17, #2, exit; ldrsw x13, [x14, x13, lsl #2]; add x18, x13, x14; …; br x18`). → [Bit test after the mask](../reference/rewriter.md#bit-test-after-the-mask)
- `jump table 63607c` assertion in `patch_nso.py` on ULTRA STREET FIGHTER II: The Final Challengers 1.1.1. → [Bounds check further back than 16 instructions](../reference/rewriter.md#bounds-check-further-back-than-16-instructions)
- `jump table 6eea88` or `jump table 6eeac0` assertion in `patch_nso.py` on Return of the Obra Dinn 1.0.1. → [Zero-guarded complemented jump-table index](../reference/rewriter.md#zero-guarded-complemented-jump-table-index)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion on an offset table whose base was set far back and moved through other registers (`ldrb w18,[x14]; sub w18,w18,#1; cmp w18,#6; b.hi; ldrsw x18,[x7,x18,lsl #2]; add x18,x18,x7; br x18`). → [Far table base passed through other registers](../reference/rewriter.md#far-table-base-passed-through-other-registers)
- `jump table ae95f8` assertion in `patch_nso.py` while rewriting Blasphemous 1.0.8. → [Offset table base initialized after the dispatch](../reference/rewriter.md#offset-table-base-initialized-after-the-dispatch)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py` where a guarded dispatch's table base is set later in address order and other loops reuse that register for their own tables. → [Masked dispatches sharing bases across control-flow paths](../reference/rewriter.md#masked-dispatches-sharing-bases-across-control-flow-paths)
- `AssertionError: jump table 59dc90` in `patch_nso.py` on Cuphead 1.3.7 `main`, a build its profile names, since e5620b1a. → [A table base register that another loop holds a small constant in](../reference/rewriter.md#a-table-base-register-that-another-loop-holds-a-small-constant-in)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion, entries relative to an `adr`. → [Offset tables relative to an adr](../reference/rewriter.md#offset-tables-relative-to-an-adr)
- `AssertionError: jump table 30ee50c` while rewriting an ADR-relative dispatch with `cmp x9,#4`. → [ADR-relative jump tables with 64-bit bounds](../reference/rewriter.md#adr-relative-jump-tables-with-64-bit-bounds)
- `AssertionError: jump table 3ce6c` while rewriting `main`, on an ADR-relative dispatch that the existing form describes: `ldrb w0, [x18, #5]; cmp w0, #5; b.hi; ldr s0, [sp, #8]; ldr s1, [x18, #0xc]; adr x18, #L; ldrsw x1, [x17, x0, lsl #2]; add x18, x18, x1; br x18`. → [Vector registers read as writes of the jump-table index](../reference/rewriter.md#vector-registers-read-as-writes-of-the-jump-table-index)
- no failure seen on a game. The rewriter could read a jump table with the wrong size or base, so a case's target or landing pad would be missing or wrong. In the other direction, a halfword table whose base is in x30 got all 65,536 landing pads (`J` rows with 65536 in `main.sites.tsv`), which only makes the patch area larger. → [Overwritten jump-table indices and bases taken as unchanged](../reference/rewriter.md#overwritten-jump-table-indices-and-bases-taken-as-unchanged)
- no failure seen on a game. `jump_offsets` and `jump_adr_offsets` could read a table with the wrong size and report nothing: on a synthetic site with a second compare between the real check and the load (`cmp w18,#4; b.hi exit; cmp w18,#1; ldrsw x18,[x4,x18,lsl #2]`) they returned 2 targets where the check allows 5, so three cases had no landing pad. → [A compare that guards nothing taken as a jump table's bound](../reference/rewriter.md#a-compare-that-guards-nothing-taken-as-a-jump-tables-bound)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion on a byte table indexed by x30 (`adr x18, #L; ldrb w30, [x14, x12]; add x18, x18, x30, lsl #2; br x18`). → [Byte tables indexed by x30 (capstone register names)](../reference/rewriter.md#byte-tables-indexed-by-x30-capstone-register-names)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion on a byte table whose `add` is far from the `br` (`adr x18,#L; ldrb w0,[x17,x15]; add x18,x18,x0,lsl #2`, then 17 register moves before `br x18`). → [Byte tables whose add is far from the br](../reference/rewriter.md#byte-tables-whose-add-is-far-from-the-br)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py`, in a Unity / IL2CPP `main`, at a `blr x18`. → [Indirect calls through x18](../reference/rewriter.md#indirect-calls-through-x18)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion on a `br x18` with no table in front of it. → [Indirect tail calls through x18 in IL2CPP thunks](../reference/rewriter.md#indirect-tail-calls-through-x18-in-il2cpp-thunks)
- `RuntimeError: subst fail <addr> caspal x18, x19, x4, x5, [x16]` in `patch_nso.py` (`subst`, from `site_plain`). → [Compare and swap of a register pair that is x18, x19](../reference/rewriter.md#compare-and-swap-of-a-register-pair-that-is-x18-x19)
- `AssertionError: jump table d29e68` while rewriting Moving Out 2 1.3.315, or `jump table 478a7b4` on Hello Kitty Island Adventure 2.17.2 (`4a521d4` in 3.0.0). → [Unframed pointer tail calls followed by compiler padding](../reference/rewriter.md#unframed-pointer-tail-calls-followed-by-compiler-padding)
- `jump table 53e9c` while rewriting Warm Snow 2.1.4's Unity IL2CPP `main`, or `jump table 108bb70` on Ori and the Blind Forest: Definitive Edition. → [Loaded method-pointer tail call before an error block](../reference/rewriter.md#loaded-method-pointer-tail-call-before-an-error-block)
- `AssertionError: retry block overlaps a window 34b790` while rewriting Trails in the Sky 2nd Chapter 1.0.4 `main`. → [Branches within an exclusive window](../reference/rewriter.md#branches-within-an-exclusive-window)
- job system hangs; a queue claims thousands of tasks but every slot is empty. → [Exit stub order in exclusive windows](../reference/rewriter.md#exit-stub-order-in-exclusive-windows)
- `branch into window <addr>` assertion. → [Out-of-line retry blocks of atomic updates](../reference/rewriter.md#out-of-line-retry-blocks-of-atomic-updates)
- branches to an `ldxr … stxr` loop's head landed mid-window. → [A window never spans a second exclusive load](../reference/rewriter.md#a-window-never-spans-a-second-exclusive-load)
- `AssertionError: 0x…` in `site_window` on an `adrp` inside an exclusive window (`ldaxr x9,[x9]; cmp x9,x25; b.ne out; adrp x10 + add x10` (the same address again, for the store); `mov x18,x26; stlxr w9,x8,[x10]`, then loads and `cbnz w9` back). → [adrp inside an exclusive window](../reference/rewriter.md#adrp-inside-an-exclusive-window)
- `B()` assertion at a spin wait: a branch into the patch area beyond the ±128 MB a `b` reaches. → [Halfword jump table landing pads sized to the largest entry](../reference/rewriter.md#halfword-jump-table-landing-pads-sized-to-the-largest-entry)
- `B()` range assertion in `patch_nso.py` (`site_plain`), only for one module. → [Patch area before the module base for a large bss](../reference/rewriter.md#patch-area-before-the-module-base-for-a-large-bss)
- `frame step state page` assertion when a `FRAME_STEP` profile is added to a module with a large BSS. → [Frame-step state with a front patch area](../reference/rewriter.md#frame-step-state-with-a-front-patch-area)
- `B()` range assertion, then `patch area of … bytes does not fit in the … before the base`. → [Patch area right before the base for a long text](../reference/rewriter.md#patch-area-right-before-the-base-for-a-long-text)
- `main: patch area of 0x6b9d38 bytes does not fit in -0x39c0000 before the base; rewriting again with 0x6c0000`, then a `B()` range assertion in `site_window`: the gap before the base came out negative. → [Two patch areas for a text longer than a branch reaches](../reference/rewriter.md#two-patch-areas-for-a-text-longer-than-a-branch-reaches)
- a `B()` range assertion in `site_plain` on the branch back to the site, in a module that takes two patch areas. → [Split point within the back area's reach](../reference/rewriter.md#split-point-within-the-back-areas-reach)
- `AssertionError: no split reaches: the back patch area reaches no site below 0x9480000, too close to the front area's reach` in `patch_nso.py`, before any site is rewritten. → [Hops for sites neither patch area reaches](../reference/rewriter.md#hops-for-sites-neither-patch-area-reaches)
- `no padding word for a hop` on a branch back to a site in a large module. → [Distributing branch hops between patch areas](../reference/rewriter.md#distributing-branch-hops-between-patch-areas)
- `observation gate neither at a function entry nor after a bl`; `observation gate neither at a function entry, after a bl nor before a call` on an epilogue. → [Observation gates on any relocatable instruction](../reference/rewriter.md#observation-gates-on-any-relocatable-instruction)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion on an ordinary byte table near the start of the text (`adr x18; ldrb w0,[x17,x16]; add x18,x18,x0,lsl #2; br x18`). → [Code before the first unwind entry is one function](../reference/rewriter.md#code-before-the-first-unwind-entry-is-one-function)
- `KeyError: 0` in `patch_nso.py`, at the parse of the eh_frame_hdr's table (`rd(o, penc)`). → [A module without unwind entries](../reference/rewriter.md#a-module-without-unwind-entries)
- the rewrite never finishes, the Mac swaps, the rewriter's footprint reaches 100 GB. → [Long functions analysed in pieces](../reference/rewriter.md#long-functions-analysed-in-pieces)
- `KeyError` at `site_br` for a `br x18` that is an ordinary byte jump table. → [Disassembly goes on after undecodable words](../reference/rewriter.md#disassembly-goes-on-after-undecodable-words)
- `RuntimeError: 788 ldr x18, #0xd1eb0` in `patch_nso.py`, or any rewrite site in data. → [Data blocks at the start of the text](../reference/rewriter.md#data-blocks-at-the-start-of-the-text)
- crackle in Super Mario Sunshine's 3D file-select menu and gameplay, with both stereo and six-channel output. The crackle is already in the PCM Hagi submits to AudioOut. → [An x18 scratch spill overwrites a live stack temporary](../reference/rewriter.md#an-x18-scratch-spill-overwrites-a-live-stack-temporary)
- `jump table <addr>` assertion in `patch_nso.py`, in a Unity / IL2CPP `main`, at an `alloca` (`mov sp, x18`). → [Stack pointer writes through x18](../reference/rewriter.md#stack-pointer-writes-through-x18)
- `CRASH sig=10 addr=0` on an `stp` relative to `sp`, with `sp` not a multiple of 16 (`main+0x2bbba4`, `sub sp, sp, #0xc0` then `stp d15, d14, [sp, #0x60]` with sp ending in `…9dc`). → [Stack arguments reserved with sub sp, sp, xN](../reference/rewriter.md#stack-arguments-reserved-with-sub-sp-sp-xn)
- `CRASH sig=10 addr=0 pc=main+0x4e8a18` on `str x1, [sp]` right after `sub sp, sp, #0x98`, with `sp` ending in `…8`; the game runs until its UI changes screen for the second time (Civilization VII's first-run options: Continue, then Continue or Back). → [Stack frames of an odd size: sub sp, sp, #imm](../reference/rewriter.md#stack-frames-of-an-odd-size-sub-sp-sp-imm)
- SIGILL / trap on a system register read. → [System register reads that macOS traps](../reference/rewriter.md#system-register-reads-that-macos-traps)
- a module's timers run 1.3 times too fast: what it measures with `cntpct_el0` and converts with `cntfrq_el0`. → [The counter frequency read raw](../reference/rewriter.md#the-counter-frequency-read-raw)
- every pointer in `.data` / the GOT is a module offset, crashes everywhere. → [Packed relative relocations (DT_RELR)](../reference/rewriter.md#packed-relative-relocations-dt_relr)
- `regress.py check` stops before gameplay with `CUTS of build ... in both ...`, naming a revision profile and its copied harness profile. → [Regression rewrites register copied profiles twice](../reference/rewriter.md#regression-rewrites-register-copied-profiles-twice)
- packing spends most of the rewrite step on the x18 scan and the spin-wait search, using one core per module. Cancelling a parallel rewrite can leave its workers running; `DM_REWRITE_J=0` or a negative value can silently omit spin gates, and the packing script can ignore an explicit worker limit. → [Parallel scans preserve rewrite output](../reference/rewriter.md#parallel-scans-preserve-rewrite-output)
- the rewrite step's memory grows through `find_spin_regions`; on a large `main` the step swaps or does not finish (EA SPORTS FC 27 1.8e, 185 MB of text: more than 24 GB in nxnative). → [Spin-wait search memory bounded](../reference/rewriter.md#spin-wait-search-memory-bounded)
- a rewrite never ends, using no CPU: `patch_nso.py` waits in `wait4` and one forked worker of the same command in `sem_wait`, in `multiprocessing/queues.py` `get`, having run no job. It is rare and needs small jobs (about 1 in 6,000 pools of tiny jobs under load). Separately, a SIGTERM could be ignored until the rewrite ended, or leave workers behind. → [A pool's shutdown hangs on a worker that has just started](../reference/rewriter.md#a-pools-shutdown-hangs-on-a-worker-that-has-just-started)
- `[RED] mods: <mod>: <file> is not applied: … cannot be made in the running image, the first at main+0x…, which uses x18, which Apple's kernel owns` (or `… replaces an instruction the rewriter already moved out of line`): a mod's `.pchtxt` or `.ips` does nothing. → [Mod code patches the host cannot make at load time](../reference/rewriter.md#mod-code-patches-the-host-cannot-make-at-load-time)

## Memory and address space

- a sanitizer build reports `global-buffer-overflow` in `t_time.c`'s `section_fn`, immediately after `hle_lib_memcpy`; other linker-collected registration tables can fail the same way. → [Registration section layout under ASan](../reference/memory.md#registration-section-layout-under-asan)
- on the Mac, a game crashes once its address regions pass the profile's alias region: at launch (Trails in the Sky 1st Chapter: `sig=11 addr=0x1` at `sdk+0x15576c`, after an 11.27 GB region at alias +1.00 GB) or a little later (Just Dance: a null allocation past 4.375 GB after about 40 s, then a crash at `main+0x639ab0`), though the build's address region check reports a larger reach. Tears of the Kingdom's long-session memory-shortage Blood Moon (`[game] MSF / R / Rf` after about 87 minutes at +2.25 of 2.38 GB, issue #639) may be this too: not confirmed by a long session, and a mark near the top alone is not exhaustion. → [The Mac's alias region is the Switch's 64 GB](../reference/memory.md#the-macs-alias-region-is-the-switchs-64-gb)
- large resources silently skipped, later a null deref where the resource is used; also visible as `SetHeapSize` asking for much less than the pool. → [Partitioning the guest block between alias region, stacks and heap](../reference/memory.md#partitioning-the-guest-block-between-alias-region-stacks-and-heap)
- an `nn::diag` abort with Result 0 under `nn::os::detail::ThreadManager::CreateThread` → `ThreadManagerImplByHorizon::MapAliasStack`, on some boots and not on others, with no `MapMemory refused` line. → [Thread stacks placed at random in the stack region](../reference/memory.md#thread-stacks-placed-at-random-in-the-stack-region)
- `nnMain` returns right after `nv::InitializeGraphics`, with no error (Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity); `[mem] SetHeapSize … + modules … > app pool` (Monster Hunter Rise), `[mem] SetHeapSize … + modules 0x5c00000 > app pool` (Catherine: Full Body), an `nn::diag` abort in `nninitStartup` after `[mem] SetHeapSize 0xc9400000 + modules 0x4400000 > app pool 0xcd500000` (MIO: Memories in Orbit; the host now logs `[mem] SetHeapSize … > app pool … less used memory …`); `SetHeapSize` asks for almost the whole application pool and fails at boot (Metroid Dread: 0xc9400000, larger than the heap region); the memory pool for the game's 96 MB GPU heap has no storage (`MemoryPoolInitialize` red line), then a null read at `main+0x2eb954` (Catherine: Full Body); heap allocations fail late in a busy scene. → [Heap sized from the application pool less used memory](../reference/memory.md#heap-sized-from-the-application-pool-less-used-memory)
- `SetHeapSize OOM: req 0x120000000 > cap 0xd0000000`, then `Break#1 reason=0` in `nninitStartup`, with a backtrace through `nn::os::SetMemoryHeapSize` (`sdk+0x967a4`). → [A heap request beyond the heap region returns ResultOutOfMemory](../reference/memory.md#a-heap-request-beyond-the-heap-region-returns-resultoutofmemory)
- `StandardAllocator::Initialize` aborts in `nninitStartup` (Fire Emblem Engage); `nn::diag` abort in `StandardAllocator::Initialize(void *, size_t, bool)` from `nninitStartup` (`sdk+0x2f98e4` ← `main+0x5b69e0`, Big Brain Academy: Brain vs. Brain). → [Virtual address memory follows the NPDM's system resource](../reference/memory.md#virtual-address-memory-follows-the-npdms-system-resource)
- the first `AllocateAddressRegion` fails with 0x1803; `StandardAllocator::Initialize` aborts in `nninitStartup` (an `nn::diag` abort); the alias region fills in minutes; regions a game reserves later fail (Size-aligned regions exhaust the alias address space). → [The SDK's address-region node area](../reference/memory.md#the-sdks-address-region-node-area)
- a null-based write minutes into play, at the pool size minus 0x20 (Engage: a write to `NULL + 0x1fffe0`, `addr=0x1fffe0` at `main+0x9a4664`); NULL index pointers written on several job threads at once (`main+0xdb0ad4`, `0xdaea58`, `0xdadb5c`), with 32-bit mesh indices turning up inside texture objects. → [Size-aligned regions exhaust the alias address space](../reference/memory.md#size-aligned-regions-exhaust-the-alias-address-space)
- the `[mem]` high-water mark climbs close to the end of the alias region while far less is mapped (SUPER ROBOT WARS Y: +4.27 GB of 4.38 GB with 1.82 GB of live regions), and it looks like the address space is about to run out. → [An alias high-water mark near the top is not exhaustion](../reference/memory.md#an-alias-high-water-mark-near-the-top-is-not-exhaustion)
- `StandardAllocator::Initialize` aborts in `nninitStartup` (Pokémon Legends: Z-A: `main+0x1b2e690`); `nn::diag` abort at `sdk+0x11ff34` (`StandardAllocator::Initialize`) in `nninitStartup`, SDK 15.3.2, called from `main+0x31370c` (Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door). → [StandardAllocators over 12 to 62 GB of address space](../reference/memory.md#standardallocators-over-12-to-62-gb-of-address-space)
- `nn::diag` abort at `sdk+0x12dad0` in `nninitStartup`, called from `main+0x756310` (1.1.1: `main+0x7562d0`). → [.NET NativeAOT malloc and the GC region range](../reference/memory.md#net-nativeaot-malloc-and-the-gc-region-range)
- C++ exception aborts in `nn::ro::detail::GetExceptionInfo`. → [`svcQueryMemory` must report each module's real layout](../reference/memory.md#svcquerymemory-must-report-each-modules-real-layout)
- the practice tools' levitation says `levitate: Link's position not found, try again`, and their log `scanning 0 heap regions for Link's record` (the game's note). → [A walk of the address space from 0 took 4 KiB steps through host memory](../reference/memory.md#a-walk-of-the-address-space-from-0-took-4-kib-steps-through-host-memory)
- `MapPhysicalMemory` faults on part of a page. → [`MapPhysicalMemory` in 16 KB host pages](../reference/memory.md#mapphysicalmemory-in-16-kb-host-pages)
- the alias region's mapped total runs past the application pool in the first minute. → [Maps past the application pool fail as on Horizon](../reference/memory.md#maps-past-the-application-pool-fail-as-on-horizon)
- after a large `UnmapPhysicalMemory` or heap shrink, RAM does not come back. `phys_footprint` may still fall, so it hides this: the `guest` class in `DM_MEMSTAT` shows the real figure. → [Unmapped guest memory stays resident](../reference/memory.md#unmapped-guest-memory-stays-resident)
- the GPU hangs or stalls with no failing draw: compute shaders that read counts from a memory pool loop forever (on the Mac a silent stall right after the title screen; on an iPhone "Caused GPU Hang Error"), and text or other data in that pool comes out scrambled. → [Device-shared memory is not unmapped](../reference/memory.md#device-shared-memory-is-not-unmapped)
- a write through a null pointer at +0x1c0 during startup. → [`GetInfo(UserExceptionContextAddress)` returns a real buffer](../reference/memory.md#getinfouserexceptioncontextaddress-returns-a-real-buffer)
- a null-pool buffer even with virtual pools working (Virtual memory pools mapped onto one physical pool). → [A null-pool buffer from a game heap that ran out](../reference/memory.md#a-null-pool-buffer-from-a-game-heap-that-ran-out)
- a model load exhausts its resource heap: a null `operator new` at the world load, or `BufferInitialize: pool has no storage` followed by a null-pool crash. A display-list heap that runs out shows as `AddCommandMemory` with a null pool outside a recording, then host-lent segments growing until `FATAL NVN command memory` (Host-lent command memory segments). → [Model heaps sized by the host's command record sizes](../reference/memory.md#model-heaps-sized-by-the-hosts-command-record-sizes)
- SIGBUS inside a host hook (`snprintf` under `mount_ex` / `h_fs_MountSave`), then the process hangs (Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury); SIGBUS inside Metal (`AGCDeserializedReply`, an AGX compiler block) at an address in the guest stack region (Breath of the Wild). → [Host calls on small guest stacks](../reference/memory.md#host-calls-on-small-guest-stacks)
- SIGBUS in `__sfvwrite` / `__vfprintf` under `fprintf`, from a hook that only logs (`focus_sync` under `h_hid_ShowCtrl`, the controller applet), at an address a few bytes below sp and just under a page of the guest thread's stack; then the process hangs in the crash handler. → [Log lines on small guest stacks](../reference/memory.md#log-lines-on-small-guest-stacks)
- resident memory grows steadily (about 0.2 MB a frame) while the residency set and the texture counts stay flat; in nxnative, an iPhone app killed for memory. → [Deferred-release drain without an autorelease pool](../reference/memory.md#deferred-release-drain-without-an-autorelease-pool)
- out of focus, with the picture still and the sound on, GPU allocated climbs by hundreds of MB a second while the residency set stays flat, and swap follows it. → [Retired pool buffers pile up while a game submits nothing](../reference/memory.md#retired-pool-buffers-pile-up-while-a-game-submits-nothing)
- `check_t_image_alias` fails `the last texture hands its heap to the recycled heaps, resident while it waits` and `every family heap is released after its textures and views go` in most runs, with direct and deferred encoding. The family textures still alive at the check (41 to 88 of the main family's 129 in five runs) are gone 100 ms later. → [A kicked drain still releases when a test checks](../reference/memory.md#a-kicked-drain-still-releases-when-a-test-checks)
- in an area load GPU allocated jumps by 6-10 GB between two stats windows (Ocarina of Time: 3.3 GB at frame 900, 15.8 GB at frame 1200 on a 16 GB M5, issue #2068; 3.5 to 13.7 GB inside the load on an M5 Pro) while the residency set grows by about 4 GB, and stays there. The memory census's "not counted" line is 8 GB, and the pool buffers made sum to twice the pools' size. → [The pool walk's statistics made chunks of a large pool](../reference/memory.md#the-pool-walks-statistics-made-chunks-of-a-large-pool)
- command buffers fail with `Insufficient Memory (00000008:kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorOutOfMemory)` once a game has played a while, though the game's own memory is far from the Mac's. The residency census (`DM_CTL` `restop`, or the one printed at the first out-of-memory error) is mostly shared buffers of 80 to 96 MB each, several times the size of the one large pool they lie in. → [Unknown-length lookups made a chunk at every stride](../reference/memory.md#unknown-length-lookups-made-a-chunk-at-every-stride)
- travelling produces a brief multi-GB footprint spike, then memory returns to its earlier level; periodic memory lines can miss the peak. Upload diagnostics show many swapped-out blocks still alive in one batch. → [Upload staging grows within one rendering submission](../reference/memory.md#upload-staging-grows-within-one-rendering-submission)
- up to 192 MiB of private GPU memory stays in upload slots after a scene load, even when steady play needs little staging. → [Upload staging keeps its largest blocks after a load](../reference/memory.md#upload-staging-keeps-its-largest-blocks-after-a-load)
- the first frame of a scene that draws hundreds of new packaged textures takes a quarter to half a second, nearly all of it in `submit+present` (`[longframe] … texture uploads 855 first …, 908.5 MB`), and the `residency set up to frame` line of that window counts about twice as many shared buffers from `up_big_src` as packaged textures (2,866 and 1,433). → [Two source buffers for each staged upload from a large pool](../reference/memory.md#two-source-buffers-for-each-staged-upload-from-a-large-pool)
- in a frame that makes many new textures (a scene's first), `res_add` on the submit thread takes tens of milliseconds in all, a few milliseconds at a time, though each is a hash-table insert. → [Residency changes waited for another thread's commit](../reference/memory.md#residency-changes-waited-for-another-threads-commit)
- graphics memory stays above the earlier steady state after frame-wide command allocator sharing, with many mostly empty 4 MB arena blocks retained. → [Per-frame arena blocks made for every submission in flight](../reference/memory.md#per-frame-arena-blocks-made-for-every-submission-in-flight)
- GPU memory grows with repeated `nvnTextureReadTexels` calls on a long-lived game thread, even after each readback completes. → [GPU readback without an autorelease pool](../reference/memory.md#gpu-readback-without-an-autorelease-pool)
- process footprint and Metal allocation grow during stationary gameplay even after readback fences complete; the deferred-release queue and command allocator sizes remain bounded. → [Completion readbacks survive a guest waiter's pool](../reference/memory.md#completion-readbacks-survive-a-guest-waiters-pool)
- `footprint` shows `Malloc Large` about the size of `pso_archive/` (the log's `pipeline archive …: N files, … MB`), resident or swapped for the whole run. `MallocStackLogging=1` with `malloc_history <pid> -allBySize` gives `newArchiveWithURL` → `MTL4ArchiveImpl::loadFromURL` → `LoaderGlobalState::loadFile` → `-[NSData initWithContentsOfFile:options:maxLength:error:]` → malloc. → [A pipeline archive on an external drive is read into memory](../reference/memory.md#a-pipeline-archive-on-an-external-drive-is-read-into-memory)
- a save load aborts in `pthread_create` or a host `realloc` with the footprint far below the memory limit; turning off Metal 4 pipelines lets the save load. The crash's address-space census, rather than the footprint alone, distinguishes this from the physical-memory limit. → [Pipeline archives exhaust a restricted task map](../reference/memory.md#pipeline-archives-exhaust-a-restricted-task-map)
- the periodic `residency set up to frame` line shows hundreds of allocations added and removed per 300 frames in a scene where nothing loads: Ocarina of Time, 1,500 (`pool_gpu` 900 of 180 KB, `mrbuf_get` 600 of 0.5 MB), and 650 commits. → [Pool tables and readback buffers made anew each frame](../reference/memory.md#pool-tables-and-readback-buffers-made-anew-each-frame)
- with a profile that has `tex_dirty_upload`, a texture in a sparse pool that the game draws into or writes as an image logs `CPU-written texture ser=…` at its first use, and takes its content from its storage. `t_sparse_tiles` crashed at `and unmapped, not drawn: its heap tile goes back` when built with such a profile, and passed with one without the key. → [A texture in a sparse pool came out CPU-written at its first use](../reference/memory.md#a-texture-in-a-sparse-pool-came-out-cpu-written-at-its-first-use)
- in nxnative, on an 8 GB iPhone 17 walking from Hyrule Field into Kakariko Village, Ocarina of Time's residency set reached 3.9 GB (wired memory 5.7 GB of 7.67 GB, 147 MB free). `DM_CTL restop` showed its 305 MB physical render-target pool (`0x18c`) wired whole in the residency set even though the GPU only reads its render targets through Metal textures. → [GPU-only render-target physical pool wired whole by texture addresses and small lookups](../reference/memory.md#gpu-only-render-target-physical-pool-wired-whole-by-texture-addresses-and-small-lookups)
- Metal copies of the textures of areas the player has left stay resident, and memory pressure goes unanswered. In nxnative, on an 8 GB iPhone 17 walking across area transitions (Hyrule Field into Kakariko Village), Ocarina of Time accumulated 0.6–1.0 GB of Metal copies of uploaded textures from areas the player had left and up to 123 MB of re-upload spares, triggering OS memory-pressure warnings and `proc-thrashing` termination at 67 MB free / 5.56 GB wired while `os_proc_available_memory` still reported 2.7 GB. → [Idle uploaded textures, re-upload spares, and memory-pressure trims](../reference/memory.md#idle-uploaded-textures-re-upload-spares-and-memory-pressure-trims)
- a Switch 2 game goes past Metal's recommended working set while its first 3D scene loads: `Insufficient Memory` command buffer errors, then a hung Mac. Ocarina of Time on a 16 GB Mac: 12 GB and more wired, once a kernel panic in `IOGPUGroupMemory.cpp` at 14.7 GB wired. → [Memory pools nothing gave an address to stay out of the raw pool table](../reference/memory.md#memory-pools-nothing-gave-an-address-to-stay-out-of-the-raw-pool-table)
- a game whose shaders read texture handles from memory wires far past the working set while a scene loads, with `Insufficient Memory` errors or a panic, and stutters when new textures stream in. With a limit on how many it makes, surfaces in shadow go near black instead. → [Shaders report which textures they read through a pool](../reference/memory.md#shaders-report-which-textures-they-read-through-a-pool)
- memory pools that hold only shader code are in the residency set, though no shader reads them. In The Great Ace Attorney Chronicles, three shared buffers, about 5 MB: chunks of its 1,000 MB pool (`DM_CTL` `restop`: shared buffers 241 MB at 30 s, 236 MB with the fix). → [Pools that hold shader code made resident to key the shaders](../reference/memory.md#pools-that-hold-shader-code-made-resident-to-key-the-shaders)
- hundreds of megabytes of memory pools that hold only shader code stay in the residency set, their pages wired, though no shader reads them. Ocarina of Time on an 8 GB iPhone 17 in nxnative: 223 such pools, 317 MB, starving the guest's file-backed pages (14–17 fps in Kokiri Forest, 6,000–15,600 page-ins every 10 s). On the Mac the same pools stay resident and count in the game's footprint. → [Shader-code pools made resident by the raw pool table](../reference/memory.md#shader-code-pools-made-resident-by-the-raw-pool-table)
- the residency set stays high after an area load that finalizes a memory pool, and at the next load that makes the pool's textures again it holds them twice. On the Mac with the iPhone's settings, Ocarina of Time's set stayed about 360 MB higher through Hyrule Field than in Kakariko Village before it, and a large recycled heap was let go only about 20,000 submissions after its pool's Finalize. On the iPhone 17 the game was ended for memory (`JetsamEvent`, `vm-pageshortage`) entering the castle grounds, with two 300 MB heaps in the set. → [A texture object finalized after its pool kept the pool's memory](../reference/memory.md#a-texture-object-finalized-after-its-pool-kept-the-pools-memory)
- the Ocarina of Time residency census in PR #2566 found a 296 MB shadow target in a 333 MB Metal heap. → [Large render targets over-allocate recycled heaps](../reference/memory.md#large-render-targets-over-allocate-recycled-heaps)
- the shared zero buffer stayed at 64 MB in the Ocarina of Time iPhone 17 residency census in PR #2566. → [A full colour texture grew the shared zero buffer](../reference/memory.md#a-full-colour-texture-grew-the-shared-zero-buffer)
- Ocarina of Time's field load on iPhone 17 added 732 MB of zero-copy upload sources to Metal residency within a 300-frame window in PR #2270. → [Guest texture source buffers accumulate within an upload batch](../reference/memory.md#guest-texture-source-buffers-accumulate-within-an-upload-batch)
- the Mac grows unresponsive for hours, then panics, with the host still submitting work that fails. → [Repeated GPU out-of-memory failures end the game](../reference/memory.md#repeated-gpu-out-of-memory-failures-end-the-game)
- a scene load logs `upload command buffer execution error: … Insufficient Memory (00000008:kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorOutOfMemory)` right after a `large texture … format 0x32 … 296 MB` line, then `upload command buffer dropped`, then the game's own command buffers fail one after another, up to the 32-failures stop (Repeated GPU out-of-memory failures end the game), with most of the GPU's working set free. → [A clear pass on a large depth texture fails with `Insufficient Memory`](../reference/memory.md#a-clear-pass-on-a-large-depth-texture-fails-with-insufficient-memory)

## Threads

- TSan reports races when guest threads first yield, sleep briefly or enter a rewritten spin wait together; a thread could see `DM_YIELD_BACKOFF`'s default cap before its own value was parsed. → [Concurrent first use of scheduler settings](../reference/threads.md#concurrent-first-use-of-scheduler-settings)
- TSan reports `thread_main` writing `host_work` while `thr_runnable` reads it during a same-core yield (`t_notify_sched`, issue #411). → [Publishing a new thread's scheduler fields](../reference/threads.md#publishing-a-new-threads-scheduler-fields)
- a parent returns from `StartThread` before a newly runnable higher-priority child pinned to its core finishes its work (deterministic regression, not attributed to a game failure). → [Starting a higher-priority thread on the caller's core](../reference/threads.md#starting-a-higher-priority-thread-on-the-callers-core)
- per-core job queues never drained. → [Core mask changes move the ideal core into the new mask](../reference/threads.md#core-mask-changes-move-the-ideal-core-into-the-new-mask)
- crash indexing a per-core table with a huge offset (index −2). → [Ideal core −2 resolves to the process default core](../reference/threads.md#ideal-core-2-resolves-to-the-process-default-core)
- ASan reports a heap-use-after-free in `svc_thread_impl` reading `t->id`; TSan reports a race between `GetThreadId` and `CloseHandle`, or between `SetThreadCoreMask` and the target's `GetCurrentProcessorNumber`. → [Thread handle queries hold the scheduler lock](../reference/threads.md#thread-handle-queries-hold-the-scheduler-lock)
- the main thread waits forever on an event right after `nnMain` starts, with only three or four threads created; `DM_THR=1` shows no `CreateThread` for the thread it waits on. → [Free thread count (`svcGetInfo` type 24)](../reference/threads.md#free-thread-count-svcgetinfo-type-24)
- each `CreateThread` whose handle is closed without `StartThread` leaves a host pthread (1 MB stack) waiting for ever and lowers the free thread count for good. → [A thread closed before it starts ends](../reference/threads.md#a-thread-closed-before-it-starts-ends)
- in garbage-collected titles (Unity / IL2CPP), live objects freed, with their finalizers releasing native resources. → [Pausing threads and reading their context for garbage collectors](../reference/threads.md#pausing-threads-and-reading-their-context-for-garbage-collectors)
- in an IL2CPP title, about once an hour, a crash on a thread that keeps working while the collector has the world stopped: an indirect call to a non-code address with `0x30` in the top byte (Aokana: NVorbis's `BufferedReadStream.Seek` on FMOD's stream thread). → [A pause acknowledged past its resume](../reference/threads.md#a-pause-acknowledged-past-its-resume)
- ASan reports a heap-use-after-free in `svc_thread_impl` (`SetThreadActivity`) reading `paused` or waiting on `pcv` of a thread that exited during the pause. → [A pause holds its target until it returns](../reference/threads.md#a-pause-holds-its-target-until-it-returns)
- in a garbage-collected title whose collector stops threads cooperatively, live objects freed while their thread sleeps, and their memory reused: a crash in the collected runtime's code on a pointer made of another object's bytes (Streets of Rage 4: `CRASH sig=11` at `main+0x46a4`, BRUTE's `SyncBlock::GetSyncBlock`, on `0x5b005b00000002`, a string's length 2 and "[["), in about two boots of three, during the first seconds of loading. → [SVCs leave the stack below sp untouched](../reference/threads.md#svcs-leave-the-stack-below-sp-untouched)
- crashes around movie changes in the game's own player threads, about one run in two: `SignalEvent` → `MultiWaitImpl::NotifyAndWakeupThread` on a null MultiWait at address 0x20; `WaitAny` walking freed holders; a worker reading a freed object at `main+0x225338`. The other way round, a handoff that waits too widely: a black screen at boot that never loads a texture (`packaged textures: first uploads 0`), or a load that never ends behind an animated loading screen, with the thread dump's `same-core handoffs N, total T ms` larger than the run itself. → [Same-core wake handoffs](../reference/threads.md#same-core-wake-handoffs)
- the picture stops for good while sound goes on; the main thread spins in the game's own wait loop (a `sample` of the process shows it in guest code, no SVC), the other guest threads wait, and the job workers it waits for sit in `svc_thread` → `dm_yield_to_core_peers` → `nanosleep`. → [A same-core handoff holds only a waker pinned to that core](../reference/threads.md#a-same-core-handoff-holds-only-a-waker-pinned-to-that-core)
- frequent same-core handoffs raise CPU wakeups and power even at a steady frame rate. → [A same-core handoff waits without a timer per handoff](../reference/threads.md#a-same-core-handoff-waits-without-a-timer-per-handoff)
- the picture stops and stays (sound goes on) at random places, about one run in two: every task worker waits on its own event, and the task a waiter waits for counts a subtask that is marked queued but is in no queue (the ring's head equals its tail). → [Lock-free queues published without barriers](../reference/threads.md#lock-free-queues-published-without-barriers)
- the game freezes when a movie starts playing beside the game (a guide movie in a corner of the screen): the last frame stays, the process idles at a few percent of a core, and the thread dump shows a lower-priority job thread `running/not waiting` with its backtrace in the movie player (`ReleaseOutputBufferIndex`), the main thread waiting for it, and the movie's input thread `running/not waiting` too. A `sample` of the process shows the job thread in `md_pump` → `md_event` → `dm_yield_to_core_peers`, and the input thread in `h_movie_SendIn` → `md_pump` waiting for a pthread mutex. → [A host lock held across a same-core handoff](../reference/threads.md#a-host-lock-held-across-a-same-core-handoff)
- boot stops before the first frame or GLSLC capture. The main thread waits for the game's audio mutex in `GetMemoryPoolState`'s caller. The host render thread is in `render_main` → `dm_yield_to_core_peers`, and the game's sound thread is waiting on a pthread mutex in `h_ars_Update`. → [A host lock held across a same-core handoff](../reference/threads.md#a-host-lock-held-across-a-same-core-handoff)
- several threads crash at once and the dumps interleave, then the process hangs instead of exiting. → [Simultaneous crashes of several threads](../reference/threads.md#simultaneous-crashes-of-several-threads)
- `watchdog: sampling failed` and no thread stacks when `DM_WATCH_SAMPLE` contains an apostrophe, such as `/tmp/John's Mac/hang.txt`; shell syntax in the path can also run commands, and long paths are truncated. → [Watchdog sample output paths are literal arguments](../reference/threads.md#watchdog-sample-output-paths-are-literal-arguments)
- a busy guest thread spends 5–7% of its time in `_pthread_mutex_firstfit_lock_slow` under `pthread_cond_wait` (Breath of the Wild's main thread in a `sample` of the Mac host); guest CPU and power above what the work needs. → [Wakes signalled after the scheduler lock is let go](../reference/threads.md#wakes-signalled-after-the-scheduler-lock-is-let-go)
- none seen; found while investigating Fire Emblem Engage's demo crash, which it did not change (that was the address-space shortage, Size-aligned regions exhaust the alias address space). → [`SignalToAddress` type 2 with count ≤ 0](../reference/threads.md#signaltoaddress-type-2-with-count--0)
- a 64-bit wait could return at once or block when it should not (not met in a game). → [`WaitForAddress` type 3: 64-bit wait if equal](../reference/threads.md#waitforaddress-type-3-64-bit-wait-if-equal)
- a game that runs on the Switch crashes on the Mac 10 to 60 s into a scene, at a different place each run, with garbage in a list the game just built (Breath of the Wild 1.6.0: `h_nvnBufferGetAddress` called with a row's metadata word as the buffer pointer, `main+0x14ef6e0`; also faults in `main+0x12970dc`, `+0x13615f4`, `+0x1445d48`, `+0x1472470`). A trace of the draw-list builders shows two threads inside the same view at once, running the same job descriptor. → [Lock acquire without acquire ordering runs a queue item twice](../reference/threads.md#lock-acquire-without-acquire-ordering-runs-a-queue-item-twice)
- a game's lock-free list or job chain uses data another thread wrote just before publishing it, and reads it as it was before (a job chain runs the previous round's callback, and the thread waiting for its signal waits for good); or a lock taken with `ldxr` lets two threads into the same queue item (Lock acquire without acquire ordering runs a queue item twice). Rare, at random points, with no `[RED]` line. → [Exclusive accesses without ordering](../reference/threads.md#exclusive-accesses-without-ordering)
- `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0, call site sdk+0x153c34` during `main`'s static initialisers, `bt: sdk+0x153c34 sdk+0x137eb4 sdk+0x13dc50 sdk+0x13fa90 sdk+0xa4d64 main+0x134 run_module_inits+0xa8 guest_main+0x15c main+0xfc start+0x1a20`, and no game code on the stack at all. → [The thread ID at ThreadLocalRegion+0x110 (SDK 22)](../reference/threads.md#the-thread-id-at-threadlocalregion0x110-sdk-22)
- a 30 Hz game thread runs at 27 Hz, and whatever it paces comes out slow (cutscenes at 36 ms per frame instead of 33.3; gameplay was fine at 60). → [Timer slack in guest sleeps (`sleep_precise`)](../reference/threads.md#timer-slack-in-guest-sleeps-sleep_precise)
- a game stops presenting just after the deterministic clock takes over. Mario Kart World v0 stops at frame 2 with its main thread and workers in `SleepThread`. → [Relative thread waits can hold back the replay clock](../reference/threads.md#relative-thread-waits-can-hold-back-the-replay-clock)
- a guest thread keeps a core at 100% with billions of `SleepThread` calls. → [Sleeps under 20 µs take the yield path](../reference/threads.md#sleeps-under-20-µs-take-the-yield-path)
- a thread polls at full speed although the power line's yields (if any) show no back-off sleeps; a `sample` of the host shows it in `svc_thread` → `block` → `pthread_cond_wait` with timeouts of nanoseconds, between plain `cthread_yield`s. → [Short timed waits are polls](../reference/threads.md#short-timed-waits-are-polls)
- one guest thread takes a whole host core for the whole run with no other sign of trouble (about 4–6.5 W of CPU on a static scene). Its thread dump (`DM_THREAD_DUMP`) shows millions of SVCs a second from one `lr`, and `DM_THR=1` logs them as `svc 0x34 ... x3=0` (`WaitForAddress` with a timeout of 0), or `svc 0x18` / `svc 0x1c` with `x3=0`. → [Zero-timeout waits are polls](../reference/threads.md#zero-timeout-waits-are-polls)
- an idle game keeps a performance core awake: the power line shows tens of thousands of wakeups a second, most of the CPU time on performance cores, and a Unity job worker (`Job.Worker 0`) at the top of its yielders, while the frame needs little work. → [Short sleeps are polls](../reference/threads.md#short-sleeps-are-polls)
- a loading screen sits for a minute with the CPU nearly idle; one thread yields thousands of times a second, every yield a back-off sleep (the power line's top yielder, with as many back-off sleeps). → [Yields that make progress return at once (`thr_yield`)](../reference/threads.md#yields-that-make-progress-return-at-once-thr_yield)
- one guest thread keeps a host core at 100% with about a billion yields a minute (`SVC 0xb` in `DM_HLECOUNT`), none of them a back-off sleep, while the game itself plays normally. → [A wait at two yields is not progress](../reference/threads.md#a-wait-at-two-yields-is-not-progress)
- a game's frame limiter that yields and reads the clock holds 59.4–59.5 fps instead of 60 (frame time p50 about 16.8 ms instead of 16.68; the HUD rounds it to 60), and uses more CPU energy than the same game sleeping: the power line's top yielder is the main thread with thousands of yields a frame. → [Clock waits sleep to their predicted end](../reference/threads.md#clock-waits-sleep-to-their-predicted-end)
- a wait times out after its event was signaled and cleared; closing and reusing a handle redirects a blocked wait to an unrelated event, or a wait on an exiting thread never completes. A signaled first entry also hides an invalid later handle. `ResetSignal` on a thread handle returns `0xfa01` (invalid state) instead of `0xe401` (invalid handle). → [Synchronization waits retain objects and completed notifications](../reference/threads.md#synchronization-waits-retain-objects-and-completed-notifications)
- a host helper thread aborts when signalling a destroyed `SystemEvent`; repeated `vi::GetDisplayVsyncEvent` calls double the event's tick rate. Rapid replacement can abort with `too many vsync events`, or a retired vsync worker can signal another service's event at reused storage. → [Service-event producers follow the binding lifetime](../reference/threads.md#service-event-producers-follow-the-binding-lifetime)
- the main thread waits forever on a job's done event at boot while every worker sleeps, two of them on one event (`main+0x1426c` waiting on `Job::m_doneEvent`); 4 of 6 Mac runs. → [One auto-clear event shared by a pool's workers](../reference/threads.md#one-auto-clear-event-shared-by-a-pools-workers)
- abort in `os::WaitSystemEvent` under `oe::PopNotificationMessage`. → [The blocking `oe::PopNotificationMessage`](../reference/threads.md#the-blocking-oepopnotificationmessage)
- Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door 1.0.1 stays black before its first frame. The main thread stops at `main+0x32796c`, after starting `SYS_Notification`; the priority-28 notifier is in `oe::PopNotificationMessage`, while the priority-44 main thread waits for its same-core wake handoff. → [Native HLE waits release the guest core](../reference/threads.md#native-hle-waits-release-the-guest-core)
- every save slot shows NO DATA and `Play Time 0 mins`, right after "Save was successful" and with a save imported from elsewhere, though the files are there and a commit is logged. Reopening the save screen reads no save file (`DM_FS=1`). The terms of service come back at every boot, since the system data save is refused too. → [File system calls are service requests](../reference/threads.md#file-system-calls-are-service-requests)
- Breath of the Wild 1.6.0 shows frames of 100–600 ms every few seconds while the GPU's work stays at 8–17 ms; in every stall the main thread and both workers are in one job queue poll (`main+0x12c0e48`). It is the starvation 1.9.0 had in the same poll (CPU hitches): the workers take and release the queue's lock at full speed, and the main thread, whose item they wait for, loses nearly every exclusive store to them. → [Queue polls with exclusive retries and no clrex](../reference/threads.md#queue-polls-with-exclusive-retries-and-no-clrex)
- going back to the title as a Switch does it (Home, close the software, start it again) took quitting the app and opening it again: the settings window, then about 14 s of the host's start on a 16 GB M2 Pro (three pipeline archives opened, 0.4-4.9 s; the prepare step's 61,141 lines, 5.8-12.1 s) before the game's first frame, and the game's own boot after that. → [A reset ends every guest thread and starts the game again](../reference/threads.md#a-reset-ends-every-guest-thread-and-starts-the-game-again)

## Services and SDK calls
