- `sm::Initialize` aborts right after `ConnectToNamedPort` (SVC 0x1F) and `SendSyncRequest` (0x21); abort inside `nn::sm::Initialize`, the log showing `SVC 0x1f` (ConnectToNamedPort, port name `sm:`) and `SVC 0x21` answered by placeholders, then an `nn::diag` abort; abort with no message at start-up in a service call the host has no hook for (`Break` from `sdk+…` with an `nn::diag` backtrace). → [SDK calls that open a system service through sm](../reference/services.md#sdk-calls-that-open-a-system-service-through-sm)
- abort in `sm::Initialize` under `sf::hipc::InitializeHipcServiceResolution`, right after the first frames; since that fix, `[RED] sf: the game connects to service <name> itself, which the host does not serve`, then an abort with `Result=0xe15` (`caps:su` in Super Smash Bros. Ultimate's single-player mode, `notif:a` when Fit Boxing 3 opens its Settings). → [Game-linked HIPC clients of system services](../reference/services.md#game-linked-hipc-clients-of-system-services)
- the host crashes in `h_fs_OpenDirectory` when its folder-backed directory handle allocation fails. → [Directory handle allocation failure](../reference/services.md#directory-handle-allocation-failure)
- `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0x7802` (`MountNameAlreadyExists`) when a game mounts a RAM disk under a name it has just unmounted. → [A mount in the SDK's own table stays until the SDK releases it](../reference/services.md#a-mount-in-the-sdks-own-table-stays-until-the-sdk-releases-it)
- `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0x202` (path not found) right after `fs path not found: <name>:/...` lines (`DM_FS=1`) for a mount name no `fs mount` line ever named. → [File calls on a mount in the SDK's own table](../reference/services.md#file-calls-on-a-mount-in-the-sdks-own-table)
- a 10-byte read of the wrong RomFS file, then a null pointer at boot; files named `rom:\StartupUI.pak` are not found. → [File handles with low bits set, and backslash paths](../reference/services.md#file-handles-with-low-bits-set-and-backslash-paths)
- a fault inside `sdk` at boot, right after the save data is mounted: `ldr w8, [x0, #0x24]` with `x0` holding a host file handle (SD GUNDAM G GENERATION CROSS RAYS: `sdk+0x62a78`, `x0 = 0x4000000000000390`), while `GetFileSize` on the same handle answered just before. → [An unhooked `fs::ReadFile` overload falls through to the SDK's own](../reference/services.md#an-unhooked-fsreadfile-overload-falls-through-to-the-sdks-own)
- Addressables never start (no `StreamingAssets/aa/settings.json` in the `DM_FS=1` output at all), then a null asset crash (`main+0x31100ec`). → [C library path checks ask the file system's limits](../reference/services.md#c-library-path-checks-ask-the-file-systems-limits)
- an Atk task aborts in a `switch` default (`UnexpectedDefaultImpl`, then `AbortImpl`) while a stream sound loads. → [Paths with `.` and `..` components](../reference/services.md#paths-with--and--components)
- a long black screen at boot with nothing in the log (Engage: about 17 s on the Mac, 32 s on an iPhone); sampling the process (`sample <pid> 3`) shows `getxattr` under `path_component` at the top of stack. → [RomFS lookups on large directories](../reference/services.md#romfs-lookups-on-large-directories)
- `[RED] fs ReadFile into … failed at +0 (EFAULT): VM region … protection 1`, now and then. → [ReadFile into write-protected texture pool pages](../reference/services.md#readfile-into-write-protected-texture-pool-pages)
- `fs … not found` lines with `DM_FS=1`. → [File-not-found noise from optional paths](../reference/services.md#file-not-found-noise-from-optional-paths)
- a missing `fs::DeleteDirectory`; undefined `nn::fs::` imports with no hook, found from the import list (Listing a game's unhooked imports before they are met) before they were met. → [Directory delete and rename calls](../reference/services.md#directory-delete-and-rename-calls)
- a failed write in one independent save can make another save's next `Commit` fail and discard its successful changes; the damaged save may then commit its truncated working file. → [Save-write failures stay with their backing transaction](../reference/services.md#save-write-failures-stay-with-their-backing-transaction)
- after an unexpected host error while creating, opening for writing, deleting or renaming a save entry, `Commit` could publish the other changes in the working directory without the failed change. → [A failed save path operation still allowed the save to commit](../reference/services.md#a-failed-save-path-operation-still-allowed-the-save-to-commit)
- `fs unlink <path> failed errno=1` in the log, then `[RED] save commit refused: ... errno=1` at the game's next `Commit`: that commit's changes are discarded (the committed version stays, the front end is told), and every commit after another such call is refused too. The game's `DeleteFile` call returned a generic failure. → [`DeleteFile` on a directory refused the save's next commit](../reference/services.md#deletefile-on-a-directory-refused-the-saves-next-commit)
- `[RED] save transaction: cloning the work directory failed (…, errno=45)` when the save folder (`DM_SAVE`) is on an external drive, then a read-only save mount and every save write failing (South Park: `profile.sys`); now the line reads `the save folder's filesystem cannot clone directories (save transactions need APFS …)`. → [A save folder that cannot clone directories](../reference/services.md#a-save-folder-that-cannot-clone-directories)
- `RenameFile("save:/a", "rom:/a")` succeeded and wrote into the read-only RomFS folder; a rename between two writable mounts, or between two names of one host save, moved the file. → [A rename between two mounts](../reference/services.md#a-rename-between-two-mounts)
- after a read error on a folder (`EIO`, an external drive going to sleep), files of a read-only mount are not found for the rest of the run; a recursive delete succeeds with files left; a listing or count is short with no error. → [A folder that cannot be read is listed short](../reference/services.md#a-folder-that-cannot-be-read-is-listed-short)
- with `DM_ROMFS_OVERLAY` set, `OpenDirectory` on a RomFS folder of tens of thousands of entries takes seconds, during which every other path call waits for the path lock. → [Opening a large folder with an overlay set takes seconds](../reference/services.md#opening-a-large-folder-with-an-overlay-set-takes-seconds)
- `OpenDirectory` on a large `DMROMFS1` RomFS image directory allocates 784 bytes (`sizeof(DirectoryEntry)`) per child up front (28.4 MiB for a 38,000-entry directory) and calls `die` if the allocation fails, even when the next `ReadDirectory` asks for one entry. → [Opening a large RomFS image directory allocates a full snapshot per handle](../reference/services.md#opening-a-large-romfs-image-directory-allocates-a-full-snapshot-per-handle)
- DLC the player owns is not unlocked. → [Add-on content from `DM_AOC_DIR`](../reference/services.md#add-on-content-from-dm_aoc_dir)
- some owned DLC is missing, and `aoc: reporting` stops at 64 add-on contents. → [Owned add-on content lists beyond 64 entries](../reference/services.md#owned-add-on-content-lists-beyond-64-entries)
- SIGBUS in `__vfprintf` under `aoc_load`, from `h_aoc_Count` (a store to `0x…67ff0`, right below a page of the guest thread's stack). → [Add-on content queries on a small guest stack](../reference/services.md#add-on-content-queries-on-a-small-guest-stack)
- clang reports a negative shift in `fill` or `vib_targets` (`hle_hid.c`). → [Controller disconnect between style and player lookup](../reference/services.md#controller-disconnect-between-style-and-player-lookup)
- buttons and menus work, the sticks do nothing in play (Super Mario Odyssey: its first hint, "Look Around", waited forever). → [History reads fill every requested entry](../reference/services.md#history-reads-fill-every-requested-entry)
- motion moves never happen; shaking does nothing (Super Mario Odyssey: cap throws by shaking). → [Six-axis sensors](../reference/services.md#six-axis-sensors)
- a Joy-Con 2 or Pro Controller 2 does not appear in the Mac's Bluetooth menu, or as a GameController or HID device; the game sees no controller. → [Switch 2 controllers over Bluetooth LE](../reference/services.md#switch-2-controllers-over-bluetooth-le)
- a game that turns a feature off while a stick is in use keeps it off after the stick is let go, some of the time. Breath of the Wild with a Pro Controller 2: motion aiming did nothing on about half the aims, and from the first use of the right stick on. → [A Switch 2 controller's stick at rest reads as in use](../reference/services.md#a-switch-2-controllers-stick-at-rest-reads-as-in-use)
- with a Pro Controller 2, tilting the controller forward turns the game's view sideways, as a roll. → [A Pro Controller 2's motion turned a quarter turn](../reference/services.md#a-pro-controller-2s-motion-turned-a-quarter-turn)
- a wired Xbox One controller that is not Microsoft's, such as a PDP Wired Controller for Xbox One (`0e6f:02a8`), is listed on USB (System Information) but its light stays off, GameController does not list it and the game sees no controller. The same pad works on Linux. → [Xbox One controllers from other makers over USB](../reference/services.md#xbox-one-controllers-from-other-makers-over-usb)
- a pair of Joy-Cons is one Pro-style controller: the game cannot see a dual controller with two sensors, one Joy-Con cannot be a sideways player with its rail buttons; a game that wants two players from one pair (its controller screen) gets one. → [Joy-Cons as dual or single npads](../reference/services.md#joy-cons-as-dual-or-single-npads)
- docked, the game opens the controller applet again every frame and never leaves the screen behind it. The log repeats `oe library applet: controller support`, `hid controller applet: 1 to 1 players wanted, 1 connected` and focus 2, then 1, thousands of times (7,162 in 3 minutes), after `hid supported controller styles 0x6`. → [A controller in a style the game does not support](../reference/services.md#a-controller-in-a-style-the-game-does-not-support)
- motion controls do nothing with a controller that has a gyroscope (Breath of the Wild: aiming with its Motion Controls option on); the six-axis sensor always lies flat. → [Live controller motion](../reference/services.md#live-controller-motion)
- a non-finite accelerometer or gyro sample corrupts the six-axis fields; later valid samples do not restore the previous fusion state. → [Non-finite motion samples](../reference/services.md#non-finite-motion-samples)
- a Pro Controller's buttons work on the Mac, but its gyro pointer never moves; `GCMotion` callbacks arrive with acceleration and rotation all zero. → [Switch Pro Controller advertises motion but sends zeroes](../reference/services.md#switch-pro-controller-advertises-motion-but-sends-zeroes)
- on the Mac, a third-party Switch Pro-compatible controller (`057e:2009`, `Switch Pro Controller`) has working buttons and motion, but one or both thumbsticks stay at `(0, 0)` in GameController, both in `GCExtendedGamepad` and in `GCController.input.unmappedInput`. → [Switch Pro Controller stick stays zero in GameController](../reference/services.md#switch-pro-controller-stick-stays-zero-in-gamecontroller)
- a DualShock 4 over Bluetooth is logged `with motion (gyro)` and the game reads the six-axis sensor every frame, but motion aiming does nothing; over USB it works (issue #1770). With `DM_MOTION_LOG=1` no `[hid] motion player 1` line appears. → [DualShock 4 over Bluetooth advertises motion but sends zeroes](../reference/services.md#dualshock-4-over-bluetooth-advertises-motion-but-sends-zeroes)
- an 8BitDo Ultimate 2 Wireless (`2DC8:6012`) over Bluetooth plays its buttons and sticks, but motion aiming does nothing, and `DM_MOTION_LOG=1` logs no motion sample. → [8BitDo Ultimate 2 Wireless over Bluetooth has no motion on the Mac](../reference/services.md#8bitdo-ultimate-2-wireless-over-bluetooth-has-no-motion-on-the-mac)
- on the Mac, an original (Switch 1) Joy-Con's buttons and sticks work through GameController but its motion does not: the six-axis sensor lies flat, so a game that steers or aims with it (Nintendo Switch Sports Resort: Archery, Boxing; Splatoon Raiders: motion aiming) does not follow the controller. A lone Joy-Con does not play at all, and a pair plays as one full-key controller whatever the game asks for. → [An original Joy-Con's sensors are read by the host](../reference/services.md#an-original-joy-cons-sensors-are-read-by-the-host)
- two Switch Pro Controllers connected over USB to a Mac play as one player: GameController lists one controller and reads one of the two pads, the other does nothing, and a two-player game refuses to start (issue #1218). Over Bluetooth both are listed. → [Identical controllers that GameController lists as one](../reference/services.md#identical-controllers-that-gamecontroller-lists-as-one)
- with one Pro Controller over Bluetooth, the log shows `GameController lists 1 of 2 pads of this model` twice for the same USB location, then players 1 and 2 for that pad; a one-player game opens the controller applet every few frames (`hid controller applet: 1 to 1 players wanted, 2 connected`, focus state 2 then 1, thousands of times). A game that suspends its music while an applet has focus plays none: sound effects and positional music still play, while area and cutscene music never start. → [One Pro Controller read as two players](../reference/services.md#one-pro-controller-read-as-two-players)
- one Pro Controller over Bluetooth comes back from a long disconnect (20 minutes or more) as two players: `GameController lists 1 of 2 pads of this model: read here` for two USB locations, one of them `0x0`, then `controller 'Pro Controller' (HID) connected` as players 1 and 2. The location-0 entry logs `cannot ask its stick calibration (IOReturn 0x10000003)`. The `connected` then `disconnected ... is free` lines of the GameController controller just before are the host releasing it to the twin path, not a Bluetooth drop. A short drop of about 20 s reconnects as one player. → [A HID entry that sends nothing counted as a second pad](../reference/services.md#a-hid-entry-that-sends-nothing-counted-as-a-second-pad)
- on the Mac, with an Xbox controller, ZL stays held without the trigger being touched; in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, Z-targeting stays on and the camera stays locked behind Link. → [An analog trigger at rest read as ZL held](../reference/services.md#an-analog-trigger-at-rest-read-as-zl-held)
- on the Mac through Steam Link / Remote Play, a virtual `045e:028e` controller named `GamePad-N` gives a game no input and no controller-connected log line. `+[GCController supportsHIDDevice:]` answers YES, but `GCController.controllers` is empty (issue #2465). → [A virtual controller is trusted but never published by GameController](../reference/services.md#a-virtual-controller-is-trusted-but-never-published-by-gamecontroller)
- on the Mac, a PDP Wired Fight Pad Pro for Nintendo Switch (USB, `0e6f:0185`, "Wired Fight Pad Pro for Nintendo Switch") is read as a raw HID pad, and the log says `buttons use 8BitDo's numbering`. Its buttons reach the game in the wrong places: A, L and R do nothing, and L acts as X. → [A PDP Wired Fight Pad Pro gets 8BitDo's button map](../reference/services.md#a-pdp-wired-fight-pad-pro-gets-8bitdos-button-map)
- on the Mac, with a keyboard and mouse receiver plugged in, the log shows `controller '<receiver>' (HID) connected: player 1` and the real controller as player 2; a one-player game takes no input from it and opens the controller applet again and again (`hid controller applet: 1 to 1 players wanted, 2 connected`). → [A keyboard receiver that declares a gamepad takes player 1](../reference/services.md#a-keyboard-receiver-that-declares-a-gamepad-takes-player-1)
- on the Mac, a Pro Controller clone that GameController does not list (a PowerA NANO pairs over Bluetooth as `Lic Pro Controller`, HID vendor and product 0) is read as a raw HID pad with its buttons in the wrong places, a full stick push reads 61 to 74% so the character does not run, and its four lights keep running in a chase. → [Pro Controller clone read as raw HID](../reference/services.md#pro-controller-clone-read-as-raw-hid)
- on the Mac, pressing left on a raw HID controller's d-pad registers up-left, and pressing up-left registers no direction. → [A raw HID d-pad reads left as up-left](../reference/services.md#a-raw-hid-d-pad-reads-left-as-up-left)
- at boot, `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0xe15` at `sdk+0x6d6948` (under `hid::InitializeSensorFusion`, with `[RED] sf: the game connects to service hid itself`). Later, motion controls that go through the SDK's sensor fusion (`hid::UpdateSensorFusion`) do nothing: the controller's orientation stays still. → [Switch 2 hid calls that open the hid service](../reference/services.md#switch-2-hid-calls-that-open-the-hid-service)
- a Switch 2 game's motion controls stay still and its motion calibration fails: Nintendo Switch Sports Resort's "Calibrar los sensores de movimiento de los mandos Joy-Con" ("place the Joy-Con on a flat surface") ends after 30 s with "No se ha podido completar la calibracion" every time, though `GetNpadImuSamples` is called thousands of times; `StoreSixAxisSensorCalibrationParameter` is never called (`DM_HLECOUNT`). → [The SDK's IMU handles are the console's six-axis handles](../reference/services.md#the-sdks-imu-handles-are-the-consoles-six-axis-handles)
- a Switch 2 game's motion calibration never completes and, with a real controller, what it moves turns by itself in circles while the controller lies still: Nintendo Switch Sports Resort's "place the Joy-Con on a flat surface" ends after 30 s with "No se ha podido completar la calibración" every time, and its paddle spins. A probe on the fusion's output sample (`DM_PROBE=sdk:6d7118@x22+0x40`: `GetUnbiasedAcceleration` z, then `GetUnbiasedAngularVelocity` in deg/s) reads an acceleration of 0 G and an angular velocity of about +560 deg/s about z with a flat sensor and no controller. → [The Npad IMU sample holds the acceleration first](../reference/services.md#the-npad-imu-sample-holds-the-acceleration-first)
- with IMU samples flowing from the host's own flat sensor, the fusion reset itself several times a second (its initialization, which asks `GetSixAxisSensorIcInformation`, ran 612 times in one Nintendo Switch Sports Resort session) and never took the controller for still; `StoreSixAxisSensorCalibrationParameter` was never called. With a DualSense its orientation drifted. → [The SDK's sensor fusion needs sensor noise and a stored calibration](../reference/services.md#the-sdks-sensor-fusion-needs-sensor-noise-and-a-stored-calibration)
- abort in `nn::sm::Initialize` under `nn::hid::VibrationNode::Update`. → [Vibration values flushed by the SDK's vibration graph](../reference/services.md#vibration-values-flushed-by-the-sdks-vibration-graph)
- with rumble on, `[RED] sf: the game connects to service hid itself`, then `CRASH sig=11 addr=0` in the SDK's mounted check (`hid::detail::IsVibrationDeviceMounted`, `sdk+0x24254c` in SDK 7, under `hid::IsVibrationDeviceMounted`; a Switch 2 SDK's form is `hid::IsOunceVibrationDeviceMounted`). With rumble off in the host's settings the game never asks. → [Whether a vibration device is mounted](../reference/services.md#whether-a-vibration-device-is-mounted)
- `DM_RUMBLE_LOG=1` shows the game's startup handle request answered with none, then every vibration value sent to handle 0 and reaching no output for the whole session, although a controller with haptics (a DualSense on the Mac) is connected. A game that rumbles would stay silent. → [Vibration handles before a controller is reported](../reference/services.md#vibration-handles-before-a-controller-is-reported)
- with rumble on, a game asks for vibration handles at boot and gets two, then sends one idle value and none for the rest of the session; nothing vibrates although a controller connects a few seconds later. `DM_RUMBLE_LOG=1` shows `GetVibrationDeviceInfo` answering mounted 0 (and the mounted check, when asked, false) before the pad connects. → [A vibration device before its controller connects](../reference/services.md#a-vibration-device-before-its-controller-connects)
- a controller (an Xbox Wireless Controller, a GameSir Super Nova, a GameSir Cyclone) never vibrates in a game; the log repeats `[RED] haptics: controller left handle create player: Couldn’t communicate with a helper application. (NSCocoaErrorDomain 4097)`. The engine itself starts. → [Controller haptic engines without advanced players](../reference/services.md#controller-haptic-engines-without-advanced-players)
- a paired Bluetooth `Joy-Con (L/R)` rumbles both Joy-Cons together for every effect—including when a game splits the pair into sideways `JoyLeft` and `JoyRight` npads so player 1's rumble shakes player 2's Joy-Con too—and logs `localities All, Default, JoyCon (L), JoyCon (R), 1 channels`. → [Paired Joy-Cons rumble both halves on one channel](../reference/services.md#paired-joy-cons-rumble-both-halves-on-one-channel)
- the app dies with `*** Terminating app due to uncaught exception 'com.apple.CoreHaptics', reason: '-[CHHapticEngine(CHHapticEngineInternal) checkEngineRunning:]: self.running'`, then `[dm] CRASH sig=6` in `__pthread_kill`; no `[RED] haptics` line comes first. Seen after about 85 s of a Mario Kart World race on an iPhone, a few seconds after the log's `oe focus state` changed twice (2, then 1). → [A haptic engine that stops under a call raises, and ended the app](../reference/services.md#a-haptic-engine-that-stops-under-a-call-raises-and-ended-the-app)
- on the Mac, a DualShock 4 or a controller that presents itself as one (a GameSir Cyclone over Bluetooth: HID vendor `054C`, product `05C4`, `productCategory` "DualShock 4") does not rumble; Core Haptics failed on every locality with NSCocoaErrorDomain 4097 (issue #363, also in a minimal Swift app), while the same controller rumbles in an SDL-based emulator. → [DualShock 4 rumble through its effects report](../reference/services.md#dualshock-4-rumble-through-its-effects-report)
- with two controllers that present themselves as DualShock 4s on the Mac, the one turned on first rumbles normally and the one turned on later is barely felt. The first logs `rumble through the DualShock 4 effects report`; the later one logs `haptics: player 2 controller …, localities …`, then `has no advanced players (NSCocoaErrorDomain 4097): plain players` and `plays both motors on one engine (Handles)`. → [A DualShock 4 that connects after the first stays on Core Haptics](../reference/services.md#a-dualshock-4-that-connects-after-the-first-stays-on-core-haptics)
- in fullscreen, the menu bar shows at the top of the game's screen whenever the game is the active app (a click on it, a controller in use), and goes away when another app on another screen is active. The system setting is "In Full Screen Only". → [Mac menu bar over the fullscreen game](../reference/services.md#mac-menu-bar-over-the-fullscreen-game)
- on the Mac, a Switch Pro Controller or a controller that presents itself as one (an 8BitDo pad, GameSir Nova, or Xtreme XTRE041 in Switch mode: HID vendor `057E`, product `2009`, `productCategory` "Switch Pro Controller") does not rumble through Core Haptics, or on a genuine Switch Pro Controller feels thin and loses stereo and dual-band texture. The log shows no error: `haptics: player 1 controller Pro Controller, localities All, Default, Handles, Left Handle, Right Handle`, then `has no advanced players (NSCocoaErrorDomain 4097): plain players` and `plays both motors on one engine (Handles)`, and the plain players start and play. → [Pro Controller rumble through its rumble report](../reference/services.md#pro-controller-rumble-through-its-rumble-report)
- an 8BitDo Ultimate 2 Wireless (`2DC8:6012`, firmware 1.03 or later) over Bluetooth LE plays its buttons and sticks but never rumbles. The log says `haptics: player 1 controller 8BitDo Ultimate 2 Wireless, localities none, 0 channels`. → [An 8BitDo Ultimate 2 Wireless with no rumble on the Mac](../reference/services.md#an-8bitdo-ultimate-2-wireless-with-no-rumble-on-the-mac)
- with a controller connected (a DualShock 4 here), `DM_KEYS` and `DM_CTL` presses are logged (`DM_KEYS: … press …`) but the game does not react to them, or only now and then; without the controller the same script works. → [Scripted presses on a path of their own](../reference/services.md#scripted-presses-on-a-path-of-their-own)
- on the Mac, the mouse pointer stays over the game picture while playing with a controller or the keyboard, in a window and in fullscreen (issue #675). → [Mac pointer stays over the game](../reference/services.md#mac-pointer-stays-over-the-game)
- the Mac dims or turns off its display and can reach the lock screen while the player is using a controller, in a window or in fullscreen. → [Mac display sleep during controller play](../reference/services.md#mac-display-sleep-during-controller-play)
- the first boot aborted in `hid::GetMouseState` (the SDK's mouse resource was never initialized). → [Mouse and keyboard](../reference/services.md#mouse-and-keyboard)
- undefined `irsensor` imports found from the import list (Listing a game's unhooked imports before they are met). → [IR camera reported unconnected](../reference/services.md#ir-camera-reported-unconnected)
- null read in `sdk` under `nn::applet::CreateLibraryApplet` when a game calls a `Show…` function that opens a system applet (Engage: title menu → DLC, its shop page); `Show…` overloads found in the imports before they were met. → [System applets opened by `Show…` calls](../reference/services.md#system-applets-opened-by-show-calls)
- entering 2P stops the game on its 2P screen: the same frame repeats, the music plays, no controller does anything, until the window loses and regains focus (Mario Kart 8 Deluxe, every launch). With one controller, once that was fixed: the 2P character select ignores input while the log shows the controller applet opened again every few frames. → [Library applets take the application's focus](../reference/services.md#library-applets-take-the-applications-focus)
- with Press a button to join on, a game's player screen closes when the game asks for more players than have pressed a button. The log shows `hid controller applet: 2 to 2 players wanted, 1 connected: too few, closed as the player would (canceled)` (Mario Kart World 1.8.0, pull request #1596). → [The controller applet waits for players](../reference/services.md#the-controller-applet-waits-for-players)
- with more controllers than a game takes, the game gets no input and the log repeats `oe library applet: controller support`, `hid controller applet: 1 to 1 players wanted, 2 connected` and `focus state → 1 (library applet closed)` every few frames (1,671 times in one run). A game that suspends its music while an applet has focus plays none. → [The controller applet leaves out the players past the maximum](../reference/services.md#the-controller-applet-leaves-out-the-players-past-the-maximum)
- crash in `lmem::AllocateFromExpHeap` under `ngc::ProfanityFilter::MaskProfanityWordsInText` right after the character name is entered (DARK SOULS: REMASTERED); filter methods among the undefined imports with no hook, found from the import list before they were met (The Great Ace Attorney Chronicles). → [`nn::ngc::ProfanityFilter` overloads taken over by name](../reference/services.md#nnngcprofanityfilter-overloads-taken-over-by-name)
- SIGSEGV in a `vi` layer or display call (The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening, which tears its display down on exit). → [vi layer and display calls without vi service objects](../reference/services.md#vi-layer-and-display-calls-without-vi-service-objects)
- `CRASH sig=11 addr=0 pc=0` with the link register just after a call to a `vi::SetLayerNextHdr…` function. → [Layer HDR metadata](../reference/services.md#layer-hdr-metadata)
- a null read in `sdk` in an `oe` call that reaches am, at boot or later; other such calls found in the imports before they were met. → [oe calls answered without an applet proxy](../reference/services.md#oe-calls-answered-without-an-applet-proxy)
- a Switch 2 game renders smaller docked than handheld, or smaller than console footage: Star Fox's scene was 768x432 docked (DLSS network resized to it at boot, its window 1920x1080) and 960x540 handheld; Ocarina of Time drew 2048x1152 docked, its 3D at no more than 1536x864; Donkey Kong Bananza's docked frame buffer was 2304x1296 on a 2880x1620 swap chain. → [The graphics scale does not follow the operation mode](../reference/services.md#the-graphics-scale-does-not-follow-the-operation-mode)
- abort in `nn::sm::Initialize` under `nn::oe::FinishStartupLogo`, right after the first frames. → [The startup logo](../reference/services.md#the-startup-logo)
- SDK 20's `OpenAudioRenderer` aborts on an ARUID of 0. → [Applet resource user ID](../reference/services.md#applet-resource-user-id)
- the game freezes after switching apps and back, picture still, sound on; the main loop waits in its pause handshake (`main+0x5d10d4` / `main+0x5cea68`). Mac repro `DM_OE_SCRIPT=bg=1@20,bg=0@25`. → [Focus handling mode decides whether focus changes are notified](../reference/services.md#focus-handling-mode-decides-whether-focus-changes-are-notified)
- undefined `nn::account::` / `nn::friends::` imports with no hook, found from the import list (Listing a game's unhooked imports before they are met) before they were met. → [Account and friends calls found in the imports](../reference/services.md#account-and-friends-calls-found-in-the-imports)
- a second controller ends the game with `FATAL account::GetUserId: the handle was not issued by the host (0x2)`, at boot with both connected or when the second joins during play (EA SPORTS FC 27 1.8d.6523; the FC 26 patch reports `(0x8)`). → [`account::GetUserId` copies the Uid of any handle](../reference/services.md#accountgetuserid-copies-the-uid-of-any-handle)
- Minecraft Dungeons II's second local player repeatedly receives player 1's user and cannot join (PR #2846). → [Local users, sessions and separate saves](../reference/services.md#local-users-sessions-and-separate-saves)
- `CRASH sig=11` at `main+0x5b64f8`, `lr=main+0x3f1b8cc` (`nn.account.AsyncContext$$Finalize`), on an IL2CPP thread-pool worker a few seconds after the player confirms a name and birthday (Pokémon Friends 1.1.5). The object's first word, its vtable, holds stray bytes (`0xffffd256` in one run, `0x15600000128` in another). The game had started `account::EnsureNetworkServiceAccountIdTokenCacheAsync`. → [An account AsyncContext needs its SDK constructor](../reference/services.md#an-account-asynccontext-needs-its-sdk-constructor)
- a "Transmitting" loading screen with a hint stays for ever before the main menu while the game keeps drawing at 60 fps and its music plays (Taiko no Tatsujin: Rhythm Festival). No red line, no crash; `DM_SF_TRACE=1` shows nothing after `nsd:u`. `DM_HLECOUNT` shows `account::EnsureNetworkServiceAccountAvailable` called thousands of times (4,647 in under three minutes, about once per frame). → [The network account check is cancelled offline](../reference/services.md#the-network-account-check-is-cancelled-offline)
- the game ends right after `nnMain`, with a crash in its own cleanup on an object it had not created yet (DRAGON QUEST BUILDERS 2: `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x60db0` at `main+0x2676c8`, x0 = 0, the callers `main+0x85210` and `main+0x7fabf8`). No red line. → [Creating an ssl context needs no network](../reference/services.md#creating-an-ssl-context-needs-no-network)
- `abort` under `grpc_init` at startup, with no red line: `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0, call site sdk+0xb8fe8`, backtrace `sdk+0xba138 sdk+0x7bd670 sdk+0x777f30 main+0x21f0c80`, just after `fs mount _npln:` (Ocarina of Time). → [`EventFd` needs no network](../reference/services.md#eventfd-needs-no-network)
- `CRASH sig=10` (SIGBUS) in a network thread right after start (Minecraft's `NetherNet Network`, `main+0x18b2ae8`, `x0=0x4000`), at a load from its own stack. → [Event descriptors in an `fd_set`](../reference/services.md#event-descriptors-in-an-fd_set)
- New Game stays on the loading artwork with an empty progress bar while rendering and loading tips continue (Divinity: Original Sin 2). Both `nn::socket::Socket(AF_INET, SOCK_DGRAM, 0)` calls fail, so RakNet never completes initialization. → [UDP loopback works offline](../reference/services.md#udp-loopback-works-offline)
- `[RED] sf: the game connects to service mii:e … mii:u itself`, then an abort, from `nn::mii::Database`; once the host answers it, a game finds none of the console's Miis. → [A console's own Mii database](../reference/services.md#a-consoles-own-mii-database)
- shop service accessor calls among a game's imports; the first one met was an abort with no message (`SVC 0x15` / `0x1f` / `0x21` placeholders, then `Break` with `sm::Finalize` under `ShopServiceAccessServer::Proxy::Initialize`) at the start of `nnMain`. → [Shop service accessors](../reference/services.md#shop-service-accessors)
- abort in `nn::sm::Initialize` under a `nn::pctl` permission query (Engage, entering its first battle map). → [Parental control permissions](../reference/services.md#parental-control-permissions)
- play report calls met entering the save select (3D World); undefined `nn::socket::` imports with no hook, found from the import list before they were met (Big Brain Academy). → [Socket and play report calls found in the imports](../reference/services.md#socket-and-play-report-calls-found-in-the-imports)
- `nn::socket` imports in forms the host had not taken over, at a game's first socket calls (Star Fox). → [SDK 21 socket overloads](../reference/services.md#sdk-21-socket-overloads)
- `[RED] sf: the game connects to service nsd:a itself, which the host does not serve` (also `nsd:u`), then `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0xe8d` and exit 134 during startup. → [Server environment queries without nsd](../reference/services.md#server-environment-queries-without-nsd)
- one thread at 100% CPU from boot, with one SVC in 99 s (`NexusRevolution Socket`, `main+0x92c7c4`, the game's network thread). → [Socket `Poll` and `Select` with nothing to wait on](../reference/services.md#socket-poll-and-select-with-nothing-to-wait-on)
- a game that ships Polish or Thai text could not be given either language: the packing script stopped at `unknown game language 'pl'`, and the Game language row did not offer them. → [Polish and Thai, and a Switch 2 SDK's own language list](../reference/services.md#polish-and-thai-and-a-switch-2-sdks-own-language-list)
- the font engine runs on garbage; crash after the shared fonts load, looking up the "?" glyph (`main+0x6c73c0`, a font cache lookup returning null), with SDK 10's `ScalableFontEngine::LoadFont` failing with error 403 and `SetFont` with 301 on both stand-in fonts; or Japanese, Korean or Chinese text draws nothing in a Unity game while Latin text is fine (Patrick's Parabox's language menu showed three blank lines). → [Shared font address: BFTTF header or bare TTF](../reference/services.md#shared-font-address-bfttf-header-or-bare-ttf)
- a crash in the font engine at boot with the null glyph, from a code point no message uses (`main+0x62d20` on 1.0.0, `main+0x63780` on 1.1.4); `?` in text for an icon the stand-in does not draw. → [Every undrawn extension code point is a ring](../reference/services.md#every-undrawn-extension-code-point-is-a-ring)
- controller-button icons in text show as `?`. → [Nintendo-extension glyphs for button icons](../reference/services.md#nintendo-extension-glyphs-for-button-icons)
- a crash after the shared fonts load, looking up the "?" glyph (`nro0+0xaef8a8`, a null pointer from nn::font's texture cache), with `ScalableFontEngine::LoadFont` returning 201 on one engine and 0 on another, and `SetFont` 301. → [Shared font stand-ins sized for the game's font engines](../reference/services.md#shared-font-stand-ins-sized-for-the-games-font-engines)
- saves dated 18:00:02 31/12/1969 (`ToCalendarTime: first conversion 2 → 1969-12-31 18:00`), and Continue, which takes the newest save, loads an older one. → [Time zone rules, calendar conversion and the network clock](../reference/services.md#time-zone-rules-calendar-conversion-and-the-network-clock)
- times a game shows through the C library are off by the host's UTC offset: Hades II's load screen dated a save made at 01:44 KST (UTC+9) 16:44 the day before, its UTC time. The save file itself holds the right time. → [The C library's local time is UTC, as on a Switch](../reference/services.md#the-c-librarys-local-time-is-utc-as-on-a-switch)
- SDK calls that reach the time service, each aborting in `sm::Initialize` (or found in the imports before they were met). → [Steady clock, clock snapshots and user clocks](../reference/services.md#steady-clock-clock-snapshots-and-user-clocks)
- `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0x74, call site sdk+…` within the first frames, the backtrace's first `sdk` frame right past `time::AdjustableNetworkSystemClock::Adjust` (its last instruction is the call of `diag::detail::AbortImpl`, so the return address names the next function, `GetSystemClockContext` of the same class). Any other reader of an adjustable network clock, or the adjustable user clock's `now()`, aborts in `diag::detail::AbortImpl` under `time::detail::MonotonicSystemClock`. → [The adjustable network clock aborts in `Adjust`](../reference/services.md#the-adjustable-network-clock-aborts-in-adjust)
- `Break#1 reason=0 Result=0 svc at sdk+0x370f24` right after the first save at the title, with `bt: sdk+0x175f00 sdk+0x175fc4 sdk+0x176060 sdk+0x375b88 main+0x6ea74 …` (`diag::detail::OnAssertionFailure` called from `time::ClaimPeriodicBenefitWithUserSystemClock`). → [A periodic benefit claim without the time service session](../reference/services.md#a-periodic-benefit-claim-without-the-time-service-session)
- a call the host answers reads as failed when it succeeded, or reads leftover register bits. Super Smash Bros. Ultimate refills the Spirit Board at every visit, since the elapsed time between two snapshots never reads as known. → [Hooks whose return type did not match the SDK](../reference/services.md#hooks-whose-return-type-did-not-match-the-sdk)
- `std::terminate` (`Break` from `sdk+0x5bfa48`) at the first real-time read (right after the first-boot dialog, `main+0x3f3f68` → `system_clock::now()`). → [`clock_gettime(CLOCK_REALTIME)` through the time source pointer](../reference/services.md#clock_gettimeclock_realtime-through-the-time-source-pointer)
- after switching apps and back, one guest thread at 100% in a tight loop (`main+0x9abd0`, `x10 = 0`); the game freezes, picture still, sound on. Mac repro `DM_OE_SCRIPT=bg=1@20,bg=0@25`. → [Guest clock reads wait while the app is in the background](../reference/services.md#guest-clock-reads-wait-while-the-app-is-in-the-background)
- abort in `sm::Initialize` under `nn::crypto::GenerateCryptographicallyRandomBytes` (Unicorn Overlord: `std::random_device` → the `sdk`'s own `getentropy` → `InitializeCsrng`; Z-A: the first call in `nnMain`, `main+0x1734b4`). → [Cryptographically random bytes](../reference/services.md#cryptographically-random-bytes)
- `libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::__1::system_error` (the arguments came out as rubbish, the format named the failure) once the game's own code ran, with `sdk+0x5f2e60` (`std::terminate`) and `sdk+0x66ad2c`/`sdk+0x66acc8` (`get_terminate`/`terminate`) in the backtrace. → [`clock_gettime` is `nn::time`'s steady clock (SDK 22)](../reference/services.md#clock_gettime-is-nntimes-steady-clock-sdk-22)
- relocation repeatedly scans all HLE and forced-takeover entries, and named-stub reuse becomes quadratic as more imports are missing. → [Host symbol indexes avoid repeated name scans](../reference/services.md#host-symbol-indexes-avoid-repeated-name-scans)
- a library's `free` rejects a pointer (`CentralHeap::Free` → `NotNMallocPtrError`). → [Global symbol resolution in load order](../reference/services.md#global-symbol-resolution-in-load-order)
- a crash during `main`'s static initializers, before `nnMain`: `CRASH sig=11 addr=0xffffffffffffffe8 pc=sdk+0x46d880 lr=main+0x164410` — a null-vptr read inside the sdk module's `std::ostream::write`, called from a game log helper that `std::cout.write`s its first line. → [Module initializers run dependencies first](../reference/services.md#module-initializers-run-dependencies-first)
- a weakly imported function the game calls although nothing defines it: an `UNIMPL` line, then an abort. → [Undefined weak imports bind to 0](../reference/services.md#undefined-weak-imports-bind-to-0)
- an abort connecting to `pl:u` at boot; a failed `nn::audio` call at boot. → [Built-in fonts and the audio device notification of SDK 21](../reference/services.md#built-in-fonts-and-the-audio-device-notification-of-sdk-21)
- an abort in `nn::hid` at boot, in a Switch 2 game that sets up the Joy-Con's mouse mode. → [The Joy-Con mouse pointer](../reference/services.md#the-joy-con-mouse-pointer)
- `nn::diag abort` in `gs::sdk::CheckGraphicsSystemInitialized` at boot. → [The graphics system check](../reference/services.md#the-graphics-system-check)
- a null read in `nn::os::SdkMutexType::Lock`, at `sdk+0x151fb0`, called from `nn::gs::sdk::QueryApplicationGpuTimeSpan` after the first rendered frame. → [Application GPU scheduling time](../reference/services.md#application-gpu-scheduling-time)
- a null read at boot in `nn::os::SdkMutexType` under `nn::gs::sdk::SetGraphicsFrameLengthMode`, called from `nn::oe::SetGraphicsTargetFrameRate`. → [The target frame rate (graphics frame length)](../reference/services.md#the-target-frame-rate-graphics-frame-length)
- a mod folder as Ryujinx, the yuzu family or Atmosphère take it (`romfs/`, `exefs/`) had no place to go: only `DM_ROMFS_OVERLAY` replaced RomFS files, by hand. With the mods folder: `[RED] mods: … is not applied` for a code patch, `mods: warning: … differ only in case` for a file a Switch would not have matched. → [Mods made for emulators do nothing, or only half of them does](../reference/services.md#mods-made-for-emulators-do-nothing-or-only-half-of-them-does)
- the practice tools' `subsdk9` read no controller (its `nn::hid::detail::GetNpadStates` ran the SDK's own reader, whose HID client the host never sets up) and could not keep its settings (`nn::fs::MountSdCard` needs fsp-srv, which the host does not serve). → [An add-on module calls SDK functions the game never makes](../reference/services.md#an-add-on-module-calls-sdk-functions-the-game-never-makes)
- none seen: a patch that writes an `svc`, an `x18` instruction or a timer register read into the padding between a module's text and its read-only data would have been applied, and run raw against Apple's kernel. → [A mod's code written past the end of the text was not checked](../reference/services.md#a-mods-code-written-past-the-end-of-the-text-was-not-checked)
- a game's amiibo screen waits for a figure forever; nothing in the host can put one on the reader. → [A game that asks for an amiibo never finds one](../reference/services.md#a-game-that-asks-for-an-amiibo-never-finds-one)

## Movies

- a Switch 2 game crashes when a movie starts: `nn::codec::video::Initialize` faults in the SDK, and before that `nv::InitCheck` aborts (`hle_nv.c`). Later, when a second movie starts: `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0` in `nn::os::ClearSystemEvent` or `TryWaitSystemEvent` on the decoder's event. → [`nn::codec` AV1 decoder taken over](../reference/movies.md#nncodec-av1-decoder-taken-over)
- `game called nn::diag abort` with Result 0x1c286f (nn::codec 3604) on `VideoDecoderThread` when a movie starts, now and then: in nxnative about 1 time in 5 after a race in Mario Kart World 1.0.0. Not seen in this game's logs. → [Registered decoder buffers signalled before a picture was decoded](../reference/movies.md#registered-decoder-buffers-signalled-before-a-picture-was-decoded)
- a crash in `nn::codec::video::InitializeAv1DecoderByTransferMemory` when a movie starts. → [AV1 movies through SDK 22's decoder](../reference/movies.md#av1-movies-through-sdk-22s-decoder)
- a movie stays black with the game waiting behind it, the decoder made and no `first picture` line (`Submit…InputBuffer` never called). Or `[RED] nn::codec VP9: Corrupt frame detected`, then `Keyframe / intra-only frame required`, each time about 6 s into a movie or after skipping in one. → [The submission-ready event counted as packets](../reference/movies.md#the-submission-ready-event-counted-as-packets)
- a movie stutters in small regular jerks (a picture stays for two or three frames, then the movie jumps on) while the frame rate reads a steady 60 and the frame times are flat: the game's frames are on time, the movie's pictures are not. Worst where the host decodes in software. → [`nn::codec` packets decoded inside the submission](../reference/movies.md#nncodec-packets-decoded-inside-the-submission)
- on a chip without a hardware AV1 decoder (M1, M2), a 1080p 60 fps AV1 movie shows a picture twice now and then in its heavy parts, with the decoder already on its own thread, and the log has `[codec] AV1 decoder 1: the decoder is behind the movie: of the last 600 packets decoded in … ms each, … ms the longest; … pictures came after their buffer was released`. → [dav1d decoded one frame at a time](../reference/movies.md#dav1d-decoded-one-frame-at-a-time)
- where VideoToolbox decodes AV1 and its session is invalidated during a movie (found on an iPhone after a background/foreground round trip), `[RED] movie: AV1 decode submission failed (-12903, first case)` appears and the movie ends within a few seconds, with many fewer pictures than input packets. → [VideoToolbox session invalidation consumes the rest of a movie](../reference/movies.md#videotoolbox-session-invalidation-consumes-the-rest-of-a-movie)
- `CRASH sig=11 addr=0 pc=sdk+0x40ca1c lr=subsdk0+0x32455c` after `StoryEndOfMovieCallback`, while opening another MP4. → [Movie extractor allocations need the SDK graphics allocator](../reference/movies.md#movie-extractor-allocations-need-the-sdk-graphics-allocator)
- a movie's sound runs ahead of its picture and ends before it, so the movie's later scenes play in silence: the log's `audren: renderer 0 rendered N frames, M of them not silent` shows the mix silent for up to a minute or more, with no underrun and no red line, and the movie's decoder reaches `end of stream` long after the movie's length. Seen where frames take two vsyncs during a 60-picture movie, which the frame step's movie rate cannot prevent: a GPU that does not hold 60 there (a slower or a busy Mac) and the thermal cap, which holds movies at 30; before that rule, every time with the frame rate set to 30. The game's speed reads right. → [A player limited to one picture a frame falls behind its sound](../reference/movies.md#a-player-limited-to-one-picture-a-frame-falls-behind-its-sound)
- a movie's subtitles, its voice lines and the overlays its event shows (Ocarina of Time's title logo) fall behind its picture and sound, about twice as late when frames take two vsyncs; one due after the movie's last picture never shows. No red line. Seen only once the player takes more than one picture a frame (A player limited to one picture a frame falls behind its sound); before that, the picture was as late as they were. → [A movie's frame counts frames drawn, not pictures](../reference/movies.md#a-movies-frame-counts-frames-drawn-not-pictures)

## Audio

- abort with Result 0x499 in `audio::GetAudioRendererWorkBufferSize`. → [SDK 8's longer `AudioRendererParameter`](../reference/audio.md#sdk-8s-longer-audiorendererparameter)
- the game is silent; only the game's updates render (catch-up frames that nothing plays). The log has `audren: OpenAudioRenderer (no event)` and no `render thread started`. → [A renderer opened without an event](../reference/audio.md#a-renderer-opened-without-an-event)
- `[RED] audren: a second renderer (the host drives only one)` with different parameters from the first open; later, `catch-up renders so far` in the thousands (7,625 in 6 minutes), about 10% of the first renderer's frames. → [A game with more than one renderer](../reference/audio.md#a-game-with-more-than-one-renderer)
- with two renderers open, one plays single 5 ms frames between silences and its sound effects are missing, while the other has an occasional all-zero 5 ms block; no underrun and no `[RED]` line, and the AudioOut `appended` and `released` counts stay balanced. Cross-correlating the outputs finds one renderer's waveform on the other. → [Two renderers overwriting each other's queued blocks](../reference/audio.md#two-renderers-overwriting-each-others-queued-blocks)
- `audren: OpenAudioRenderer` logged twice right after the first open, with the same work buffer and "original device 1 mode 1", and a second render thread (until 2026-11-04 the second log line was `[RED] audren: a second renderer`, which now means more than one renderer). → [The SDK's open with an event calls the open without one (`t_in_open`)](../reference/audio.md#the-sdks-open-with-an-event-calls-the-open-without-one-t_in_open)
- the frame loop stops for good as a level loads while audio keeps rendering. The main thread waits in `os::WaitSystemEvent` under `CAudioMemoryPool::Shutdown`, and a sound thread of higher priority waits on the same condition variable. → [The renderer event as the service gives it: a kernel event](../reference/audio.md#the-renderer-event-as-the-service-gives-it-a-kernel-event)
- the game exits with 42 on its first rendered audio frame: `Break#1 reason=0` in `os::SignalSystemEvent`, called from the public `ExecuteAudioRendererRendering` on the host's render thread (`render_main`). → [The renderer event belongs to the renderer, not the game](../reference/audio.md#the-renderer-event-belongs-to-the-renderer-not-the-game)
- none of its own; command generation races the game's `Update` on SDKs whose lock is not at SDK 16's offset. → [The renderer's update lock (`update_lock`)](../reference/audio.md#the-renderers-update-lock-update_lock)
- `CommandListProcessor::Process` crashes now and then (SIGSEGV under `render_main`, once at 60 s and once at 134 s in the shrine); every `RequestUpdate` renders a catch-up frame. → [The render thread's route through the public `ExecuteAudioRendererRendering` (SDK 20)](../reference/audio.md#the-render-threads-route-through-the-public-executeaudiorendererrendering-sdk-20)
- SIGSEGV at address 0x18 in `UpsamplerManager::Allocate` on the render thread. → [A renderer below 48 kHz with no upsampler on the CPU](../reference/audio.md#a-renderer-below-48-khz-with-no-upsampler-on-the-cpu)
- the main thread spins at start-up in a resource wait (a `YieldThread` loop, no crash), right after `OpenAudioRenderer`. → [A sound engine's heap too small for its renderer and players](../reference/audio.md#a-sound-engines-heap-too-small-for-its-renderer-and-players)
- no sound after boot; `audren: no DeviceSink (ID 0x13) in the command list` and a malformed-command error persist even when later command lists are valid. → [Incomplete startup lists decide nothing](../reference/audio.md#incomplete-startup-lists-decide-nothing)
- ASan reports a stack-buffer-overflow in `capture` while logging the first full command histogram; a malformed list can also consume sink discovery or return PCM before its invalid tail is checked. → [Malformed later command lists and bounded histogram logging](../reference/audio.md#malformed-later-command-lists-and-bounded-histogram-logging)
- the CPU processor drops the final mix. → [The final mix is taken from the DeviceSink's mix buffers](../reference/audio.md#the-final-mix-is-taken-from-the-devicesinks-mix-buffers)
- none seen: no SDK writes such a list. A DeviceSink input index outside the renderer's mix buffers, or a command with the sink's ID too short for the sink's fields, read memory past the mix buffers or past the command list; now `[RED] audren: DeviceSink input N names mix buffer M, of K: no sound`, `[RED] audren: the DeviceSink command (ID 0x14) is 0x40 bytes, too short for its inputs: no sound` or `[RED] audren: the command list's K mix buffers of N samples at 0x... cannot be read: no sound`, once each, and silent frames. → [DeviceSink fields read without a bound](../reference/audio.md#devicesink-fields-read-without-a-bound)
- UBSan reports a misaligned `uint32_t` load in `cmd_ok` while `t_audren_layout` probes SDK 4/6 lists (issue #409). → [Unaligned command headers during layout probing](../reference/audio.md#unaligned-command-headers-during-layout-probing)
- no music and no sound effects, only the movies' sound (the log repeats `audren: first-frame commands …` every frame and never prints `DeviceSink inputs`). → [SDK 8: command IDs numbered differently](../reference/audio.md#sdk-8-command-ids-numbered-differently)
- `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x20` at `nn::audio::server::AddressInfo::GetReference` (`sdk+0x249874`) on the render thread, under `CommandGenerator::GenerateDataSourceCommand` and `render_once`, minutes into a movie. The `AddressInfo` the crash register points at is all zeros, though the function had just read a non-null memory pool pointer from it. Or `[RED] audren: no update lock read off AudioRenderSystem::Update or UpdateImpl`, on an SDK whose code the scan does not recognize. → [The update lock read off `UpdateImpl`](../reference/audio.md#the-update-lock-read-off-updateimpl)
- no sound; red `audren: command 0 in the command list is malformed (first word 0x181901 size 0)`. → [SDK 21 DSP command header without a magic](../reference/audio.md#sdk-21-dsp-command-header-without-a-magic)
- the game quits before its first frame with `FATAL AudioOut sample rate 44100 not supported (48 kHz only)`, `CRASH sig=6` in `abort` under `au_open_at`, exit 134. Nothing is drawn. → [An AudioOut opened at a rate other than 48 kHz](../reference/audio.md#an-audioout-opened-at-a-rate-other-than-48-khz)
- `[RED] AudioOut channel count N: audout takes 1, 2 or 6, the open fails with 0x1499`, or `[RED] OutputStream channel count N: not supported (1, 2 or 6), the open fails`. Before the refusal, an output opened with more than eight channels wrote past a stack block on the silent sink's thread (`null_sink`) once it started. → [An AudioOut or OutputStream opened with an unsupported channel count](../reference/audio.md#an-audioout-or-outputstream-opened-with-an-unsupported-channel-count)
- RealtimeSanitizer reports stdio locking in `au_render` at its 2,000-callback diagnostic boundary. A busy log writer can block that callback even when its PCM queue is full. → [Periodic diagnostics outside the AudioUnit callback](../reference/audio.md#periodic-diagnostics-outside-the-audiounit-callback)
- PCM is consumed correctly and its buffer reaches the released queue, but the guest release event never arrives. The notifier is asleep with `notify_pending=1` and `notify_armed=0`. → [AudioOut release notification can sleep with pending work](../reference/audio.md#audioout-release-notification-can-sleep-with-pending-work)
- FMOD waits for buffer release after stopping an AudioOut, hanging the game during a scene transition. → [Stopping an AudioOut releases its pending buffers](../reference/audio.md#stopping-an-audioout-releases-its-pending-buffers)
- clicks and dropouts on the Mac, heard as clipping; the log's `audio #N device callbacks` lines show hundreds of underruns per 2000 callbacks. Closing an output can also log `[RED] audio device buffer: ... (status 560947818)` (`!obj`). → [The Mac's default I/O buffer](../reference/audio.md#the-macs-default-io-buffer)
- a windowless Mac run logs intermittent audio underruns. A long frame can occur around the same time. → [Underruns in a run without a window](../reference/audio.md#underruns-in-a-run-without-a-window)
- sound stays silent after the output device stops calling the AudioUnit back without stopping it; a game whose audio thread waits for its buffers back (Wwise's poll-only output, FMOD) then waits forever. → [An output device that stops calling back](../reference/audio.md#an-output-device-that-stops-calling-back)
- a game stops advancing while the Mac's selected output refuses to play. A process sample shows `AudioOutputUnitStart` waiting in the HAL, with the game's audio renderer waiting for a released PCM buffer. → [A blocked audio device start freezes the game](../reference/audio.md#a-blocked-audio-device-start-freezes-the-game)
- TSan reports `au_create` writing `a->au` while `au_watchdog` reads it under `g_aos_lock` when a second real output opens (issue #410). → [AudioUnit construction before output publication](../reference/audio.md#audiounit-construction-before-output-publication)
- combined numbers mislead: one fully fed output plus one fed by nobody look like "every other callback underruns, 2.0 blocks queued". → [Several outputs at once (`audio #N`)](../reference/audio.md#several-outputs-at-once-audio-n)
- an abort in `sm::Initialize` under `audio::GetAudioDeviceOutputVolume` (the game thread, `main+0xee8edc`). → [Audio device output volume and names](../reference/audio.md#audio-device-output-volume-and-names)
- an `nn::diag` abort with Result 0xe15 on an audio thread at start, in `audio::CreateUserClientSessionByHipc` under `audio::AcquireAudioOutputDeviceNotification`, after the line `sf: the game connects to service aud:u itself`. → [The output device notification under its SDK 21 and 22 name](../reference/audio.md#the-output-device-notification-under-its-sdk-21-and-22-name)
- SDL3's audio driver lists the input devices (AudioIn) when its audio starts. `[RED] sf: the game connects to service audren:u itself`, then `nn::diag abort: Result 0xe15` under `audio::AcquireAudioDeviceNotificationForInput` (`sdk+0x5633c`). → [No audio input devices](../reference/audio.md#no-audio-input-devices)
- `[RED] sf: the game connects to service auddmg:u itself, which the host does not serve`, then `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0xa99` at `sdk+0x1f61ac` under `audio::ListVirtualInputDevices`. On main before real input support, the safe empty list also kept the game from hearing a microphone. → [Virtual audio input streams (SDK 21/22)](../reference/audio.md#virtual-audio-input-streams-sdk-2122)
- with a DualSense over Bluetooth and its microphone asked to stream, the game reads buttons and sticks no one touched (upstream counted about 1,300 presses and 300 sticks past half in 5 s with the pad lying still). Over Bluetooth the controller is in no input-device list. Over USB it is one, but its mic may stay silent: muted, or in power saving. → [A DualSense's microphone over Bluetooth](../reference/audio.md#a-dualsenses-microphone-over-bluetooth)
- `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0xe15, call site sdk+0x1f8704`, i.e. inside `nn::audio::CreateAudioOutManagerByHipc+0xb4`, before any audio device opened. → [`OpenAudioOut` without a release event (SDK 22)](../reference/audio.md#openaudioout-without-a-release-event-sdk-22)
- `[RED] sf: the game connects to service auddmg:u itself, which the host does not serve`, then `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0xa99` under `nn::audio::GetDefaultVirtualOutputDevice` (`sdk+0x221520`, `audio::detail::GetVirtualOutputDeviceProperties`). → [SDK 21's OutputStream on a virtual output device](../reference/audio.md#sdk-21s-outputstream-on-a-virtual-output-device)
- `[RED] sf: the game connects to service auddmg:u itself, which the host does not serve`, then `game called nn::diag abort: Result 0xa99, call site sdk+0x224d1c` (`audio::detail::GetVirtualOutputDeviceProperties`), right after `GetDefaultVirtualOutputDevice`. → [The virtual output device's properties, and the OutputStream open without flags](../reference/audio.md#the-virtual-output-devices-properties-and-the-outputstream-open-without-flags)
- a game that resamples its own audio plays, in one block of every four, the sound of four blocks earlier: Metro 2033 Redux (44.1 kHz to 48 kHz in the game's own resampler) re-appended 518 of its 2,095 loud 8192-byte blocks (24.7 %) still holding the contents of the block 4 appends back, so that audio left 170.6 ms behind; after the fix 0 of 1,989, with no block appended twice or lost and no underruns. → [The AudioOutBuffer handed to the SDK is its own 40-byte object](../reference/audio.md#the-audiooutbuffer-handed-to-the-sdk-is-its-own-40-byte-object)
- on a Mac connected over HDMI to a 5.1 TV, receiver or soundbar, a game that writes six channels plays in stereo: its center, LFE and back channels come out of the front pair as a downmix. The game is told the device has two channels. → [Stereo only on a 5.1 system](../reference/audio.md#stereo-only-on-a-51-system)
- on a Mac with a multi-output audio interface, sounds placed at the player are almost silent while the music plays. The log reads `audio output route: "<device>" has 22 channels, the host plays six (5.1)`. → [Center and back channels silent on an audio interface](../reference/audio.md#center-and-back-channels-silent-on-an-audio-interface)
- an abort in `sm::Initialize` under the hardware Opus decoder's C interface (`codec::GetHardwareOpusDecoderWorkBufferSize`). → [Hardware Opus decoders built on the SDK's software decoders](../reference/audio.md#hardware-opus-decoders-built-on-the-sdks-software-decoders)
- the light limiter crashed on a null state pointer (`ApplyLightLimiterEffect`, `sdk+0x228494`; `sdk+0x228614` on 1.2.1). → [At least one rendered frame between two updates](../reference/audio.md#at-least-one-rendered-frame-between-two-updates)
- the sound runs about 50 times faster than the output; the loop runs 11,000 updates a second on one core. → [A sound driver that never waits on the renderer event](../reference/audio.md#a-sound-driver-that-never-waits-on-the-renderer-event)
- movies at about half their rate (16 fps for a 29.97 fps Bink movie), frames of about 62 ms with the GPU at 0.4 ms and the CPU nearly idle; `catch-up renders so far` grew by about 1,800 per 2,000 renders while a movie played. → [An update that waited for the catch-up queue](../reference/audio.md#an-update-that-waited-for-the-catch-up-queue)
- villager dialogue crackles while music and sound effects play cleanly. On a 44.1 kHz / 256-frame device, `catch-up renders so far` grows by about 277 per 2,000 renders. `DM_AUDREN_TRACE=1` shows two renders between updates or two updates 0.02 ms apart followed by an 11.6 ms gap. → [Burst renders when a device callback releases multiple blocks](../reference/audio.md#burst-renders-when-a-device-callback-releases-multiple-blocks)
- constant static behind otherwise clean sound; the output settles near one queued block, with 12–38 underruns per 2,000 callbacks on speakers and up to 275 on AirPods, although rendering averages about 0.07 ms of each 5 ms block. → [Late audio producer wakeups despite fast rendering](../reference/audio.md#late-audio-producer-wakeups-despite-fast-rendering)

## Frame rate

- clang reports division by zero in `fstep_tick` (`hle_nvn.c`). → [Frame cap checked and used from the same sample](../reference/frame-rate.md#frame-cap-checked-and-used-from-the-same-sample)
- a 30 fps game to run at 60; after the floor was lowered, slow motion on a GPU-bound device (GitHub issue #9). → [sead's dynamic present interval, `ksys::VFR` and the host's frame step](../reference/frame-rate.md#seads-dynamic-present-interval-ksysvfr-and-the-hosts-frame-step)
- on a Mac whose frames take longer than a vsync, the game runs in slow motion rather than at a lower frame rate: movement, animation and the game's clocks fall behind real time, while the frame counter shows 30-45 fps. → [A fixed frame step plays in slow motion on a slow Mac](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-fixed-frame-step-plays-in-slow-motion-on-a-slow-mac)
- a game that holds about 60 fps with `DM_FPS_HOLD=off` runs at about 40 with the hold on, stuttering: the `frame step: game time …` lines count many frames held 2 and 3 vsyncs, `up / down` climbs to 30 or more a session, and the long frames come out at 33 and 50 ms with little GPU time in them. → [Three late frames among frames on time raised the hold](../reference/frame-rate.md#three-late-frames-among-frames-on-time-raised-the-hold)
- a game that holds 60 fps with the frame step drops to 30 for about a second (30 presents, sometimes 60) a few times in a few minutes of play, each time just after one long frame (`[longframe]` at 43 to 68 ms). The `frame step: hold 1 → 2` line says `3 late frames`, and its cost is what hold 1 holds at. → [A lone stall among near-misses raised the hold](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-lone-stall-among-near-misses-raised-the-hold)
- a game whose stub takes the host's frame step runs at a rate between the whole-vsync holds (55-59.7 fps GPU-bound, or any rate with `DM_FPS_HOLD=off`): the time it loses is paid back one whole vsync at a time, so motion jumps every few frames, and where frames are longer than two vsyncs at hold 1 it is not paid back at all: the log's `frame step: game time … (0.908 of real time)` with nearly every frame late and a vsync paid back in each. → [Frames that are not whole vsyncs long under the frame step](../reference/frame-rate.md#frames-that-are-not-whole-vsyncs-long-under-the-frame-step)
- with the practice tools' frame advance (the game's note), one tap of L plays more than 1/60 s of the game: 2/60 s in a scene this Mac runs at 30 fps, and 1.06 to 1.08 vsyncs a frame with L held in a scene at the limit of 60 fps. The practice tools' log says `31 of ours in the first second paused` where it says 61 at 60 fps. → [Frame advance stepped by the hold and the measured frame time](../reference/frame-rate.md#frame-advance-stepped-by-the-hold-and-the-measured-frame-time)
- none seen; found while writing the reset. After one, a frame step judged by the screen (present snapshots with the display link) and the `@presented_ms` timing column would have read no time at all. → [Present times read by the window's own count after a reset](../reference/frame-rate.md#present-times-read-by-the-windows-own-count-after-a-reset)
- on a warm start, Ocarina of Time stepped the frame hold 1 → 2 → 3 at presents 9 and 12 (cost 1.92 and 5.57 vsyncs, GPU 0.02 and 0.01 vsyncs) on a progress screen while the pipeline archive was written, then stayed at hold 3 (about 18 fps) for about 1,470 presents, though the scene's GPU work was 12.5 ms. → [Late frames with an idle GPU stepped the hold up and unpaced holds did not probe](../reference/frame-rate.md#late-frames-with-an-idle-gpu-stepped-the-hold-up-and-unpaced-holds-did-not-probe)
- after a pause (the window out of focus, minimized or covered), a fullscreen switch or a window uncovered, a frame-step game runs at double speed for a quarter of a second (`15 vsyncs paid back` per pause) and can drop to 20 or 30 fps for a moment; the log shows `frame step: hold 1 → 3` or `1 → 2` right after `oe focus state → 1` or `front end: window visible`. → [A pause or a window switch steps the hold up or pays back fast](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-pause-or-a-window-switch-steps-the-hold-up-or-pays-back-fast)
- `scripts/regress.py` reports a hang (`no frame after … s`, a state dump with every game thread waiting) in some runs of Tears of the Kingdom, baseline and candidate alike; the log's last focus line before it is `oe focus state → 2`, with no `→ 1` after it. → [A regress run stops when another app takes the key window](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-regress-run-stops-when-another-app-takes-the-key-window)
- otherwise stationary gameplay benchmarks show roughly 125–135 ms frame spikes at their verification screenshots; adaptive-cap results vary between runs. → [Benchmark captures perturb the measured frame pacing](../reference/frame-rate.md#benchmark-captures-perturb-the-measured-frame-pacing)
- on an iPhone, a game with the frame step at 60 fps drops a frame in most windows after a few minutes, and its GPU time per frame roughly doubles to triples (17-26 ms for the same scenes). → [A thermally serious iPhone holds 30 fps](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-thermally-serious-iphone-holds-30-fps)
- in a game the host steps (`FRAME_STEP`), a prerendered movie plays at the wrong speed, and the frame step's rate is not the movie's picture rate. Too fast: at 60 fps a 30-picture movie plays at twice its speed, its sound falls behind, and the log has `[movie]` lines with pictures 33 ms apart (`time 0 / 33000 / 67000 µs`) and no frame step cap change at the movie. Too slow: at a 30 fps cap (`DM_FPS_TARGET=30`) a 60-picture movie plays at half speed, and its `[movie] … decoder …: N s of pictures in M s` lines show half as much movie as time. → [The frame step follows a movie's picture rate](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-frame-step-follows-a-movies-picture-rate)
- after a 30 fps cap ends (a prerendered movie, the thermal cap), a GPU-bound game stays at 30 fps in a scene that held 60 before the cap; the log shows `frame step cap 30 → 60 fps` and a few frames later `frame step: hold 1 → 2 vsyncs at present … (3 late frames; …)`, with no probe after it. → [The frame step after a 30 fps cap ends](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-frame-step-after-a-30-fps-cap-ends)
- on a Mac, a GPU-bound game at 60 fps falls to 30 or 20 fps when the scene gets heavier and stays there until a menu or a lighter scene, while the GPU clock sits at its floor (338–400 MHz on an M5 Pro) and the GPU is powered 60–75% of the time; the log shows probes back to hold 1 that fail after about 33 frames (`frame step: hold 2 → 1 vsyncs … (probe, GPU 1.67× its time at hold 1)` then `the probe failed`), and the host's profiler (`@gpu_mhz`) shows the clock rising during each probe and falling back after it. → [A probe's grace lasts while its frames keep getting cheaper](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-probes-grace-lasts-while-its-frames-keep-getting-cheaper)
- a GPU-bound game whose GPU time is more than a vsync even at hold 1 keeps probing back to 60 fps and failing: the log repeats `frame step: hold 2 → 1 vsyncs … (probe, GPU …× its time at hold 1)` and `the probe failed` every few seconds, each after a grace of late frames at hold 1, often followed by hold 3 (20 fps). On an M2 Pro, Ocarina of Time standing in Hyrule Field at night: 22 probes in a minute, all failed, 159 frames at 20 fps; a 30 fps cap (`DM_FPS_TARGET=30`) had none. → [A scene that does not fit the shorter hold probed against another scene's GPU time](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-scene-that-does-not-fit-the-shorter-hold-probed-against-another-scenes-gpu-time)
- on a Mac, 20 or 30 fps for 10–40 s after a new area loads, then a steady 60 again, with nothing compiling or uploading in between; the log shows `frame step: hold 1 → 2` and `hold 2 → 3 vsyncs … (3 late frames` within a few presents of each other at the load, long-frame lines of exactly 50 ms with `GPU` 22–44 ms and no new pipelines or uploads, and much later `hold 3 → 1 vsyncs … (probe, GPU 6.24× its time at hold 1` followed by `the probe held`. → [Single frames over the fit keep a longer hold](../reference/frame-rate.md#single-frames-over-the-fit-keep-a-longer-hold)
- on a Mac whose GPU clock barely follows the hold, a scene that costs a little under a vsync stays at 30 fps for good once a heavier moment or a load steps it up, though it held 60 before and would hold it again: `frame step: hold 1 → 2` and then no `hold 2 → 1` line, few or no probes, and the `held 1 / 2 / 3 vsyncs` counts all at 2. GPU time is the same at either hold, about 0.86–1.0 vsyncs (14.5–16.7 ms). Ocarina of Time on an M4 Pro at full resolution, GPU p50 15.0 ms: 48.1 fps, holding 2 vsyncs much of the time; its castle town market at a mean of 0.86 vsyncs: 30 fps for the rest of the scene after its load. → [A scene that fits hold 1 kept at 30 fps by the fit's margin](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-scene-that-fits-hold-1-kept-at-30-fps-by-the-fits-margin)
- a game whose profile has a frame step (`FRAME_STEP` in its `patches.py`) runs at double speed when packed from another update; the log has no `frame step in whole vsyncs` line, and the frame counter shows 60. → [A derived profile drops the frame step its build lacks](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-derived-profile-drops-the-frame-step-its-build-lacks)
- a fixed-step game runs at double speed (or slow motion at 30) after `DM_FPS_TARGET` is set, in `Documents/env.txt` or the launcher, on a build its profile's frame step stubs do not know. → [A present cap applies only with a frame step or an engine limit the host writes](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-present-cap-applies-only-with-a-frame-step-or-an-engine-limit-the-host-writes)
- a game holds 30 fps with spare CPU and GPU time; it asks for present interval 2 once (`the game sets present interval 1 → 2`) and nothing else limits it. With `present_interval display` it runs at 60, but `fps_target` logs `present cap … not applied` and the player's **Frame rate** (30 or 60) does nothing: frames stay at one a display tick. → [A present interval that is the game's only limit, in display mode](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-present-interval-that-is-the-games-only-limit-in-display-mode)
- slow motion when the GPU is the bottleneck: a game's interval controller that times the GPU never steps up (GitHub issue #9). → [GPU timestamps per submission segment](../reference/frame-rate.md#gpu-timestamps-per-submission-segment)
- a game with dynamic resolution steps down to its floor and stays there while its GPU time does not follow the resolution: Nintendo Switch Sports Resort in a table tennis match at 854x480 for minutes (560 frames of 600 at 854x480), GPU p50 about 12 ms at 854x480 as at full size, 60 fps either way. Whether it falls is chance, and a fall is for good: of four runs of the same route that left the frame's span as it was, one held full size and three fell and stayed down. → [The GPU's wait for the host's translation between submissions](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-gpus-wait-for-the-hosts-translation-between-submissions)
- on a Switch 2 game, GPU times measured from raw counter reports read 0.6144 of their length: a 16.7 ms frame reads 10.2 ms, and the timestamps lag the guest clock by a third of its uptime. → [NVN2 timestamps are nanoseconds](../reference/frame-rate.md#nvn2-timestamps-are-nanoseconds)
- a Switch 2 game's wind, foliage and particles move about 20 times too fast although the frame rate is right. The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time (Switch 2): foliage, falling leaves and hanging plants. → [A game that reads raw timestamp reports at another rate](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-game-that-reads-raw-timestamp-reports-at-another-rate)
- `t_counter_clock`'s check "feedback conversion uses the resumed guest epoch" failed in about one run of six (`gpu_s_guest_ns` returned 0, unknown). In a game, a counter report whose completion handler reads the clock in the same tick as the command buffer's `GPUStartTime` or `GPUEndTime` fell back as if that GPU time were unknown (GitHub issue #1896). → [A feedback time read in its own clock tick counted as in the future](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-feedback-time-read-in-its-own-clock-tick-counted-as-in-the-future)
- a Switch 2 game holds 20 or 30 fps on the Mac although its frame work fits in one vsync: its frame-rate controller raises the present interval (`the game sets present interval 1 → 3` in the log) and never steps back down. → [The Switch 2's system tick is 31.25 MHz](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-switch-2s-system-tick-is-3125-mhz)
- with a game's target frame rate written to 120 and a 120 Hz display link, the game holds 120 where frames fit, but a scene whose frames take 14 ms runs in slow motion at 72 fps, and on a 60 Hz screen the whole game runs at half speed. → [120 fps follows the frame step](../reference/frame-rate.md#120-fps-follows-the-frame-step)
- with `DM_FPS_TARGET=120`, a 120 Hz screen and the cap at 120 (`120 fps: cap 0 → 120 fps`), the game holds 60: every trial of hold 1 ends in `hold 1 → 2 … (3 late frames; cost 2.0…)` with the CPU mostly idle and the GPU under 1 ms a frame. → [The display's vsync event follows 120 fps](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-displays-vsync-event-follows-120-fps)
- with `DM_PRESENT_LINK=0`, at a frame cap the game holds, a frame reaches the screen 3–5 periods after the game queues it, and the lag steps up after hitches and stays: Ocarina of Time at a 30 fps cap, 92–155 ms from the game's enqueue to the screen (p50; p95 within 1–4 ms of it in each 600-frame window), against 32–45 ms when the game runs slower than its cap. → [Frames pile up for the screen at a cap the game holds, without the display link](../reference/frame-rate.md#frames-pile-up-for-the-screen-at-a-cap-the-game-holds-without-the-display-link)
- the app is active and the game keeps running, but the picture freezes while display-link callbacks stop; the log repeatedly drops queued frames. → [Present thread rescues a silent display link](../reference/frame-rate.md#present-thread-rescues-a-silent-display-link)
- in fullscreen, a frame of about 135 ms every 3, 6, 12, 24 s, each followed by up to half a second of standing picture; the log repeats `display link: rescue ended, trying pacing mode 1`, then `display link silent for 18x ms (active)` and `display link silent for 5xx ms while active … (rescue #n)`. → [A display link tried again after a rescue stays silent](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-display-link-tried-again-after-a-rescue-stays-silent)
- at fullscreen startup the display link logs rescue and recovery, then frame timing and screenshot completions stop. The Presentation Thread waits in `mtl_present_wait` while `nvn-present` waits on its queue. → [Display-link rescue during startup can leave the present queue without a consumer](../reference/frame-rate.md#display-link-rescue-during-startup-can-leave-the-present-queue-without-a-consumer)
- `[longframe]` lines of 40–180 ms with no new pipelines, a short submit and normal GPU time, which read like CPU hitches in the game: single frames of 60–100 ms, or bursts of 5–12 frames, one of 140–180 ms and the rest about 40 ms. → [Long frames a test run makes itself](../reference/frame-rate.md#long-frames-a-test-run-makes-itself)
- with the display link driving presents, a presented frame comes out black when three presents are on the GPU at once, or two after a dropped frame; on an M6 the same reuse also logs `[RED] display command buffer error` with `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`. → [A present command allocator is reused only after its own present completes](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-present-command-allocator-is-reused-only-after-its-own-present-completes)
- "sometimes the speed increases when jumping" (Mario 1.2.1 on the iPhone): in `display` mode interval-2 frames were shown for 16.7 ms and the game visibly jumped ahead. "When pressing B, Link's move pace is speeding up" (Breath of the Wild on the iPhone); `old frames dropped by merging`. → [Frames of a dynamic present interval held on screen and at the acquire](../reference/frame-rate.md#frames-of-a-dynamic-present-interval-held-on-screen-and-at-the-acquire)
- a loading screen that should take seconds runs for minutes at 60 fps with a few long frames of new pipelines; the log has `the game sets present interval 1 → 0` before it and `0 → 1` after it, and the graphics worker thread spends most of its time in `nvnWindowAcquireTexture` → `mtl_link_wait_ticks`. → [Present interval 0 waits for no vsync](../reference/frame-rate.md#present-interval-0-waits-for-no-vsync)
