- a game the profile upgrades to 60 fps (`fps_target 60`, `present_interval display`) drops to about 25 fps in a window on an M2 Pro, from the first frames and in every scene, with no red line, once `nn::vi::ListFrameHistory` lists the presents (nxnative's host did; the layer frame history is not in this repository). On an M6 it runs at 54–58 fps with 33 ms frames and empty display-link ticks. The `present interval set by the game` in the `interval mode display` line is 2. → [A game's own present pacing reads the interval the window presents at](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-games-own-present-pacing-reads-the-interval-the-window-presents-at)
- a game that asks NVN for fewer active swap chain textures than it gave the window gets one frame more queued between its rendering and the screen than it chose (input latency), with a log line `WindowBuilderSetNumActiveTextures n (the host rotates through the SetTextures count)`. → [Swap chain textures a game leaves inactive](../reference/frame-rate.md#swap-chain-textures-a-game-leaves-inactive)
- Donkey Kong Bananza ran its heavy areas at 15 fps where its GPU took about 46 ms a frame: the frames came every 66.7 ms, and the presentation thread spent 85% of its time in `nvnWindowAcquireTexture` waiting for display-link ticks. → [Odd present intervals on the 30 Hz display link](../reference/frame-rate.md#odd-present-intervals-on-the-30-hz-display-link)
- with `present_interval display`, `DM_PRESENT_HZ=120` and `DM_PRESENT_LINK=2` (a 120 Hz display link) a game stays at 60 fps although its frame takes 8 ms; the display link logs about two ticks per present. With a requested 90 Hz, truncating the tick conversion instead allows 120 fps (reproduced through the real acquire with simulated link ticks). → [One frame per tick of a 120 Hz display link](../reference/frame-rate.md#one-frame-per-tick-of-a-120-hz-display-link)
- a queued frame can replace a 60 fps frame after just one 8.33 ms tick of a 120 Hz display link. → [Queued frames wait for the frame's display-link ticks](../reference/frame-rate.md#queued-frames-wait-for-the-frames-display-link-ticks)
- frames miss the screen while the GPU stays at its lowest clock (444 MHz on an M2 Max); more work (a higher render scale) only makes the frames slower. → [Present snapshots: the game's swapchain texture free at once](../reference/frame-rate.md#present-snapshots-the-games-swapchain-texture-free-at-once)
- with snapshots on, Xenoblade Chronicles 3's 3D scene is one flat colour in a third to a half of the frames (black, grey, blue, orange, white; a new colour each time), while the HUD drawn over it is right. The more ring slots, the more often: 32 of 80 screen captures with 2 slots, 49 of 80 with 4, none with snapshots off (LNPANDY/NX2Apple#1117). → [Present snapshots: the game's swapchain texture free at once](../reference/frame-rate.md#present-snapshots-the-games-swapchain-texture-free-at-once)
- none seen in a game. When the presented texture changes size or format while snapshots are on, a display command buffer still queued could sample the ring slot's old texture outside the residency set, which faults the queue (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`, then every later command buffer ignored), as in A present snapshot's copy needs the same residency commit. → [A replaced snapshot ring texture stays resident until the slot's next use](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-replaced-snapshot-ring-texture-stays-resident-until-the-slots-next-use)
- a `regress.py` run of an NVN game stays at 20 fps from the start (`frame N not reached`, a NEW-FAIL) in some runs only: `frame step: hold 1 → 2` and `2 → 3 vsyncs` in the first 15 presents, then `display link recovered: stalled 1017 ms` about once a second to the end, and no trial back to a shorter hold. → [A game window another process starts opens behind other windows](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-game-window-another-process-starts-opens-behind-other-windows)
- asked for 60 Hz, the system fires the display link on every third refresh of a 165 Hz screen, 55 Hz, so games paced by it ran at 55 fps (27.5 at 30). → [Virtual 60 Hz ticks on screens that are not a multiple of 60](../reference/frame-rate.md#virtual-60-hz-ticks-on-screens-that-are-not-a-multiple-of-60)
- virtual ticks keep the average, but a frame still stays up two or three refreshes in turn (13.9 / 20.8 ms at 144 Hz), and Apple's HUD showed the frame interval jumping between them. → [Fullscreen switches the screen to a rate the frame rate divides](../reference/frame-rate.md#fullscreen-switches-the-screen-to-a-rate-the-frame-rate-divides)
- in fullscreen on a variable-refresh screen, some starts ran cleanly while others had the display link go silent for 100–500 ms two or three times, enter rescue, and run at 20–30 fps for a minute depending on when the mode switch landed. → [The display link started before fullscreen's mode switch](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-display-link-started-before-fullscreens-mode-switch)
- a Mac game in fullscreen holds 60 fps for 45 s to 3 minutes, then the same work takes about twice the GPU time (Tears of the Kingdom 10.5 → 19.7 ms) and the frame step falls to 30 fps. `powermetrics` shows the GPU at its lowest clock (338 MHz on an M4 Pro) while it is 72–98% busy, with thermal pressure nominal. Probes back to 60 fail, because the clock does not rise within a probe. `gamepolicyd` logs `Game mode enabled` and `Set DPS to 2`. → [macOS Game Mode holds the GPU at its lowest clock](../reference/frame-rate.md#macos-game-mode-holds-the-gpu-at-its-lowest-clock)
- a GPU-bound scene misses its frame rate with a late frame every few frames, while the game issues the same calls, draws and passes in the fast and the slow frames. The real GPU span (`@gpu_end_ms − @gpu_beg_ms`) of a quarter to a third of the frames is 5–15 ms over the others; runs with identical settings differ by several fps; in `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` a slow frame has encoders that normally take tens of microseconds taking 1–3 ms (a 128x128 pass of one draw at 2,972 µs), or one large pass at three times its usual time, in different places from one slow frame to the next. → [Other apps' GPU work inside the game's frame](../reference/frame-rate.md#other-apps-gpu-work-inside-the-games-frame)
- a Mac game started with `DM_FULLSCREEN=1` from an app that is itself in fullscreen (the launcher's console mode) came up as a window. → [A game opened from a fullscreen app stays a window](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-game-opened-from-a-fullscreen-app-stays-a-window)
- on a ProMotion built-in screen, only in fullscreen, the frame step went 1 → 2 → 3 vsyncs on frames costing 0.1–0.6 vsyncs (`3 late frames; cost 0.32 vsyncs`) and the game presented 15 frames a second; a window, or the screen set to a fixed 60 Hz, did not do it. The `DM_NVN_TSV` rows show presents shown one 120 Hz refresh late (`@pres_at_ms` − `@link_target_ms` = 8.3 ms) with a skipped tick after each (`@link_gap_ms` 33 ms), and `@link_lead_ms` about 16 ms against about 50 ms in a window. → [Frame latency 2 for the display link in fullscreen](../reference/frame-rate.md#frame-latency-2-for-the-display-link-in-fullscreen)
- in fullscreen on a MacBook Pro's ProMotion screen, a 60 fps game held 40 fps with its GPU at 10 ms a frame: frame times alternated 16.7 and 33.3 ms, and each `on-screen` line counted half its frames as held too long (`dropped 150` of 300, `discarded 0`). The same route in a window held 60.0 fps, and the title screen showed the same 40 fps in fullscreen. → [The 60 Hz link at 40 fps in fullscreen on a ProMotion screen](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-60-hz-link-at-40-fps-in-fullscreen-on-a-promotion-screen)
- in fullscreen on a variable-refresh screen that keeps its mode (`DM_SCREEN_MATCH=0`, or no fixed-rate mode), a game whose GPU work takes about a vsync runs 4–5 fps below what the same build does with no window, with the same GPU time. Frame times sit at 16.7 ms with 25 ms and 33 ms ones between (`@presented_ms` in 8.3 ms steps), the `on-screen` line counts 6–12 of 300 frames held too long, and the frame's fence wait (`@sync_ms`) is 3–5 ms where the GPU-bound run with no window has 10. → [A GPU-bound game on the 120 Hz link pays a refresh for every late frame](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-gpu-bound-game-on-the-120-hz-link-pays-a-refresh-for-every-late-frame)
- the game freezes for 0.5 s to half a minute, then carries on, at a moment the display link goes silent (a long frame, a screenshot, the window covered); `DM_NVN_TSV` shows `@wait_ms` growing frame by frame to seconds before it. → [The fallback pacer slept a lead the display link built up](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-fallback-pacer-slept-a-lead-the-display-link-built-up)
- `DM_NVN_TSV` reports an `@late_ms` value close to 18.4 trillion ms for an acquire that woke before its texture's slot. → [An early display-link wake logs zero lateness](../reference/frame-rate.md#an-early-display-link-wake-logs-zero-lateness)
- on a MacBook Air M2's fixed 60 Hz built-in display, the reporter measured about 50 ms from the game's present call to on-screen presentation with the display link, at both requested frame latencies 1 and 2, versus 29 ms with `DM_PRESENT_LINK=0`. After loading, all three runs held 60 fps with 0.3% of on-screen intervals outside 16.7 ± 2 ms and no frame-step changes. → [Fixed-rate display-link delay remains at latency 1](../reference/frame-rate.md#fixed-rate-display-link-delay-remains-at-latency-1)
- at a 60 fps target the game stutters in bursts in fullscreen on a fixed 60 Hz external screen, while the same area is smooth in fullscreen on the Mac's built-in screen. GPU time is about 10–13 ms, within the 16.7 ms frame budget. In `DM_NVN_TSV`, `@pres_at_ms` minus `@link_target_ms` is 12–21 ms for the late presents and within 4 ms for the others. → [Presents miss their target in fixed-rate external fullscreen](../reference/frame-rate.md#presents-miss-their-target-in-fixed-rate-external-fullscreen)
- with the display link, one link tick more from the game's present to the screen than without it, every frame, in a window and in fullscreen: 40.3 ms against 31.7 ms with `DM_PRESENT_LINK=0` on a fixed 120 Hz screen at frame latency 1 (`@pres_at_ms` − `@t_s` in `DM_NVN_TSV`), with no dropped or late frames (issue #2648). → [The callback took the frame queued after the previous tick](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-callback-took-the-frame-queued-after-the-previous-tick)
- on the 60 Hz display link at frame latency 1, a 60 fps game whose GPU work is about 10 ms a frame drops to a steady 30.0 fps right after a drain into the latch; the 300-tick line counts every tick latched with no timeout, and `DM_PRESENT_LATCH=0` holds 60 fps. → [The late latch held GPU-heavy 60 fps games at 30 fps](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-late-latch-held-gpu-heavy-60-fps-games-at-30-fps)
- at a 30 fps target, in fullscreen on a fixed 60 Hz screen, frames stay on screen 66.7 ms, often many in a row; in a window a late frame stays 50 ms. The `display link up to tick` lines count 300 ticks per 300 presents, but over 12-18 s instead of 10. → [The 30 Hz display link in fullscreen on a fixed-rate screen](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-30-hz-display-link-in-fullscreen-on-a-fixed-rate-screen)
- input lag of one frame more than the display link needs, from the first late frame on and for the rest of the run: the `display link up to tick` line's `game enqueue to target display p50` sits one link tick above its value before the hiccup (60 fps in a window: 65.4 ms, then 82.0 ms; a 30 fps cap in a window: 115.4 ms from the first 300 frames on), with no dropped and no empty ticks. → [A frame standing in the present queue after a late frame](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-frame-standing-in-the-present-queue-after-a-late-frame)
- autorelease work takes a measurable share of replay time, with temporary arena-buffer ownership traffic a few times per draw. → [Arena buffer ownership on allocation](../reference/frame-rate.md#arena-buffer-ownership-on-allocation)
- a game runs at about 10 fps with little GPU work: the submit thread waits for a command allocator (`mtl_sub_begin` → `mtl_wait_at`), the GPU sits idle between encoders for hundreds of ms (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`), the Metal 4 `SubmissionQueue` thread spends nearly all its time in `iokit_user_client_trap` (almost all system time), and the `memory up to frame` line shows a residency set of 10,000 objects or more. → [Dedicated c1 constants as slices of a bank](../reference/frame-rate.md#dedicated-c1-constants-as-slices-of-a-bank)
- the submit thread waits for the GPU at the start of a submission (`mtl_sub_begin` → `mtl_wait_at`), many times a frame, in a game that makes many small submissions a frame; the `translation detail` line's `GPU waits so far` climbs with all 8 allocator pools in use. → [One command allocator for each of a frame's submissions](../reference/frame-rate.md#one-command-allocator-for-each-of-a-frames-submissions)
- a game that counts samples in many submissions a frame runs far below its GPU time on a phone: frames of 60-120 ms where the GPU needs 25-45 ms, the game's main thread waiting in `nvnSyncWait` (`nvn_sync_wait` → `mtl_wait_at`), the Metal 4 `SubmissionQueue` threads in the kernel's GPU driver (IOGPUFamily) nearly all the time, the `main thread yields per frame` count in the tens of thousands, and the `residency set up to frame` line showing thousands of commits per 300 frames with `line <n>` of `vis_take`'s overflow (`newBufferWithLength:VIS_SLOTS * 8`) at the top of what was added. → [Visibility buffers made and dropped for each counting submission](../reference/frame-rate.md#visibility-buffers-made-and-dropped-for-each-counting-submission)
- CPU-visible Metal memory (`footprint`'s `IOAccelerator (graphics)`) of several hundred MB in thousands of 32-48 KB regions, most of it the main submission's command allocators: `DM_NVN_ALLOCPROF=1`'s `command allocator usage` shows 8 of 36-80 MB each, a few MB each in light scenes. On some builds, frames with many render passes were slow while the GPU was not the limit. → [The alias flush on every encoder's end barrier](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-alias-flush-on-every-encoders-end-barrier)
- the submit thread waits on the texture / sampler registry lock while a lower-priority registration thread is preempted holding it. → [Registry lock priority inheritance](../reference/frame-rate.md#registry-lock-priority-inheritance)
- in a replay profile, `_platform_memcmp` is most of the time spent in serial lookups (`tex_mtl` through `g_by_serial`). → [Hash-table lookups of 8-byte keys](../reference/frame-rate.md#hash-table-lookups-of-8-byte-keys)
- `objc_retain`, `objc_release` and `objc_retainAutoreleasedReturnValue` spread over the replay thread's samples, under small per-draw helpers. → [Retain and release in every draw's helpers](../reference/frame-rate.md#retain-and-release-in-every-draws-helpers)
- the GPU sits idle each frame while the host translates the submission (a game that does not submit frame N+1 until frame N's GPU work is done). → [Submissions handed to the GPU in segments](../reference/frame-rate.md#submissions-handed-to-the-gpu-in-segments)
- the GPU idles in the middle of the frame, before a long render pass (a G-buffer of thousands of draws), while the host translates that pass: segments are committed only between passes, so the whole pass reaches the GPU at its end. → [A long render pass split while the GPU has nothing](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-long-render-pass-split-while-the-gpu-has-nothing)
- one frame in 50 or so takes 25-40 ms in a scene where the frame normally fits, with the host on time: `@sync_ms` is high, `@sub_wall_ms` is not, and the frame before has a GPU span (`@gpu_end_ms - @gpu_beg_ms`) of 25-40 ms while the summed `@gpu_ms` has not moved. Runs fall into two states, and on the same code one can have 5 long frames and the next 60. A bad run also shows 186 % CPU against 133 %, and the previous frame still running when the next starts (p50 4 ms, p90 10 ms, against about 0 in a good run). → [The GPU idles with the next command buffer already committed](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-gpu-idles-with-the-next-command-buffer-already-committed)
- a GPU-bound scene some frames a second under its rate with the GPU's own work under a vsync: per frame the game has no command buffer on the GPU for 1-2 ms, nearly all of it before the frame's first one (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`: the gap in front of the frame's first render pass; `DM_NVN_TSV`: `@gpu_end_ms - @gpu_beg_ms` 2 ms over the summed `@gpu_ms`), while the replay thread sat idle for milliseconds just before. → [The GPU waits at a frame's start for commands the game queued long before](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-gpu-waits-at-a-frames-start-for-commands-the-game-queued-long-before)
- on an iPhone at the launcher's 30 fps cap a game falls to 5-18 fps and stays there, in any scene, light ones included, until the app starts again; a restart may fall into it at once. The CPU is nearly idle and resolution does not matter: `NVN submit+present` 4 ms, `GPU p50` 34-50 ms for work that takes 14-16 ms at the GPU's full clock, whole frames of 55-130 ms, the game's dynamic resolution at its floor with no effect. The frame step's lines read `frames late at hold 2 … (a 60 Hz display link: 50 ms)` and never `no late frame for 8 s`. In a `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` profile the frame spans 50-80 ms for 33 ms of encoders, every encoder runs about 1.4 times longer than in a run that holds 30 fps (2.7 times longer than right after a load), and three frames of four have a gap of 22-37 ms after the frame's first command buffer, with every later submission already committed. → [A frame's submissions queued behind its first one while the iPhone's GPU clock is low](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-frames-submissions-queued-behind-its-first-one-while-the-iphones-gpu-clock-is-low)
- some launches run at about half the frame rate from early on and stay there, where another launch of the same build at the same spot does not. The GPU is mostly idle (70-87 % in a kernel trace on a 16 GB M2), frames wait in the sync wait, and the deepest moments are frames of 300-400 ms and more. The submission thread (`com.Metal4.SubmissionQueue`) spends most of its time in the kernel, as in the GPU idling with the next command buffer committed. In 10 s `spindump`s of two such runs, 27-28 % of that thread's kernel samples are in `IOGPUScheduler::freeToWireSysMemory` (all under the queue's event signal, `IOGPUDeviceUserClient::t_signal_shared_event`) and 62-63 % in `IOGPUMemory::prepare_memory_slow` (nearly all under the commit, `IOGPUCommandQueue::submit_command_buffers`); a run outside the state has 0-1 samples there. A Metal System Trace shows 53,000-93,000 `Wire <size> System Memory` events a second on the game's threads, against 18-1,552 in the other runs. Seen on 16 GB Macs with the guest's memory in files (nxnative's `DM_GUEST_FILE=1`, a switch removed in this fork). → [The driver wires memory tens of thousands of times a second with residency sets on the queues](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-driver-wires-memory-tens-of-thousands-of-times-a-second-with-residency-sets-on-the-queues)
- the submit thread's CPU samples half in `__CFBasicHashFindBucket_Linear` under `CFSetContainsValue` (from `tex_mtl_` and `tex_by_id`), a few hundred thousand to over a million instructions of translation per draw, the submission growing with the game's CPU-written textures. → [The set of CPU-written textures hashed by serial](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-set-of-cpu-written-textures-hashed-by-serial)
- CPU submission spikes during texture updates, with the residency log showing tens of MB of new private staging allocations per frame. → [Reusing upload staging after a slot completes](../reference/frame-rate.md#reusing-upload-staging-after-a-slot-completes)
- periodically rewritten textures cause CPU submission spikes even with no new staging allocations or shader compilation; a small cube-array image produces thousands of deswizzle dispatches and buffer-to-texture copies. → [Small array texture uploads without per-face staging](../reference/frame-rate.md#small-array-texture-uploads-without-per-face-staging)
- `bind_vbufs`, and `mtl_gpu_addr` under it, a large share of the replay thread; the `[nvn] vertex bindings` line's address lookups and reuses many times the draws. → [Vertex streams the shader does not read looked up every draw](../reference/frame-rate.md#vertex-streams-the-shader-does-not-read-looked-up-every-draw)
- the replay thread sending `textureType` and `pixelFormat` Objective-C messages on every texture slot during full argument encodes, pool table walks, handle tables, image bindings and depth-plane checks. → [Full argument encodes messaged each texture's type and pixel format](../reference/frame-rate.md#full-argument-encodes-messaged-each-textures-type-and-pixel-format)
- the submit thread's CPU samples dominated by `mtl_addr` / `mtl_gpu_addr` (vertex buffers in `draw`, uniform buffers in `encode_args`). → [Address to memory pool lookups by binary search](../reference/frame-rate.md#address-to-memory-pool-lookups-by-binary-search)
- the replay thread's samples under `mtl_gpu_addr`'s cache misses: `pc_get`, then `pool_held` and its lock. → [Pool lookups by page missed with thousands of pools](../reference/frame-rate.md#pool-lookups-by-page-missed-with-thousands-of-pools)
- a submit thread busy in `encode_args` with few blocks reused: the per-frame TSV's `@args_enc` close to `@draws`, though most draws keep their textures and samplers. → [Argument blocks encoded again when only a uniform buffer moved](../reference/frame-rate.md#argument-blocks-encoded-again-when-only-a-uniform-buffer-moved)
- `fb_prepare`, `fb_depth_rule`, `ubd_conflict_textures` and `ubd_conflict_sh` in the replay thread's samples, mostly in their own loops. → [Per-draw checks walked every reflected binding](../reference/frame-rate.md#per-draw-checks-walked-every-reflected-binding)
- gameplay has low CPU clocks, large render submission wall time compared with thread CPU time, and default QoS (`0x15`) on the main guest thread while workers report user-interactive (`0x21`). → [Main guest thread keeps inherited default QoS](../reference/frame-rate.md#main-guest-thread-keeps-inherited-default-qos)
- stalls of 30–60 ms about once a second, at 30 fps and at 60 (99th percentile 63–127 ms); the main thread waits 20–34 ms for the render thread once a second. → [Time-constraint computation declared below the thread's peaks](../reference/frame-rate.md#time-constraint-computation-declared-below-the-threads-peaks)
- on an iPhone at the 30 fps cap, frames late with the CPU work mostly on efficiency cores: 1-25% of the process's CPU time on performance cores, the submit thread at 1.55-1.66 GHz with 16-24 ms of CPU a frame, frame p50 35-38 ms (25-26 fps) where the GPU needs 20-27 ms. → [Frame threads with no deadline of their own run on efficiency cores at 30 fps](../reference/frame-rate.md#frame-threads-with-no-deadline-of-their-own-run-on-efficiency-cores-at-30-fps)
- the acquire thread's declared computation is too low for a frame acquired in several calls (a third or half of the frame's CPU time), or, after the game acquires from another thread, the new thread gets no policy of its own, its computation is inflated to the cap, or the old thread keeps the frame's deadline. → [A time-constraint computation sampled per acquire or across acquire threads](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-time-constraint-computation-sampled-per-acquire-or-across-acquire-threads)
- a `sample` of the queue replay thread shows `own_alias_publish` → `alias_take_over` → `nvn_hm_get` and `memcmp` under the passes' first draws (`draw_` → `rt_dims` → `tex_mtl_at`): 3% of the thread's samples, 6% of its busy time, in a scene where the game makes its render targets again as new texture objects. → [A storage takeover looked at a spared entry once per 64 KB cell](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-storage-takeover-looked-at-a-spared-entry-once-per-64-kb-cell)
- a movie or full-screen compute pass far slower than the same work should be, nearly all of it GPU time: `GPU p50` near the frame time with `submit+present` under 1 ms. `[RED] raw global memory: a write log ran out of slots` on older chains. With `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` a relaxed timestamp can put the time before the compute encoder's reported start, so the encoder itself looks short and the gap before it is what is long. → [A shader that writes a whole frame through raw global memory](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-shader-that-writes-a-whole-frame-through-raw-global-memory)
- a game with dynamic resolution stays at its full size while the GPU runs far over budget: `GPU p50` above the frame time target, frame times locked to 2 vsyncs, and the same pass sizes frame after frame in `DM_NVN_GPUPROF`. → [Dynamic resolution that never moves, and draw passes that wait for each other](../reference/frame-rate.md#dynamic-resolution-that-never-moves-and-draw-passes-that-wait-for-each-other)
- a game with `draw_barriers game` gets no benefit from it: the 300-frame log line `draw-pass entry barriers full N / saved 0`, every draw pass behind a full barrier, and in a Metal System Trace many small idle gaps between consecutive render encoders of one command buffer (Ocarina of Time on an M1 Max: 141 a frame, 2.3 ms in all). → [Draw passes that wait for each other again while images share memory](../reference/frame-rate.md#draw-passes-that-wait-for-each-other-again-while-images-share-memory)
- a game with many small compute dispatches a frame spends most of its GPU time in one compute / copy encoder: `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` shows an encoder with hundreds of dispatches at about 30 µs each, and the frame rate drops where the dispatch count rises, with the submit thread and the render passes well inside the frame. → [Compute dispatches that wait for each other](../reference/frame-rate.md#compute-dispatches-that-wait-for-each-other)
- frames of 150 ms to more than a second in play, long after the prewarm finished, with `[longframe]` lines naming new pipelines and the `pipelines up to frame` line showing compute pipelines `built` on the submit thread; a game with thousands of compute shaders hits them at every new effect. → [Compute pipelines built at their first dispatch](../reference/frame-rate.md#compute-pipelines-built-at-their-first-dispatch)
- after a loading screen the game's resolution stays at its lowest (Ocarina of Time: 683x384) for minutes while it holds 60 fps with GPU time well under the frame, and climbs back only slowly or not at all. → [Dynamic resolution at its floor while the GPU has time to spare](../reference/frame-rate.md#dynamic-resolution-at-its-floor-while-the-gpu-has-time-to-spare)
- with the game's dynamic resolution on, a Mac that cannot draw the full size in one vsync stays at 30 fps while the resolution stays high: the frame step holds 2 vsyncs, its probes of 1 fail at a cost of 1.1 to 1.2, and the controller reports loads under its threshold. → [A dynamic resolution threshold above one vsync holds 30 fps](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-dynamic-resolution-threshold-above-one-vsync-holds-30-fps)
- many passes a frame on the same attachments, with the residency set line showing `passes ended to land them for a read` in the thousands and `refused …: earlier submission running` matching it. Ocarina of Time: 10,463 per 300 frames, each ending the pass over a uniform block no GPU write touched. → [A full table of running GPU writes ends passes for nothing](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-full-table-of-running-gpu-writes-ends-passes-for-nothing)
- the submit (replay) thread spends a large share of its time in `ubc_wait`, under `pthread_mutex_lock` / `pthread_cond_signal`, while the `dm-ub-copy` thread waits for the same mutex far longer than it copies; translation cost jumps several-fold on one host build and not on another with the same code. Ocarina of Time on one build: 192 of the replay thread's 392 busy samples in `ubc_wait`, the copy thread 168 samples waiting for the mutex and 19 copying, 221-333k instructions a draw (9 runs) and 23-57 ms of submit CPU a frame, against 38.9k and 7.1 ms with `DM_NVN_UB_COPY_THREAD=0`. Builds that did not convoy still had 0.3-0.9 % of the replay thread's busy samples there. → [The uniform copy thread's hand-off convoys on its mutex](../reference/frame-rate.md#the-uniform-copy-threads-hand-off-convoys-on-its-mutex)
- a game that reads textures through a pool (Shaders report which textures they read through a pool) renders far below its maximum dynamic resolution: Ocarina of Time's main pass at 763x429 with the GPU at 17 ms a frame. → [A host's own store in a fragment shader moves its depth test](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-hosts-own-store-in-a-fragment-shader-moves-its-depth-test)
- passes that read textures through a pool (Shaders report which textures they read through a pool) cost far more GPU time than their texture reads explain, most on an iPad: Ocarina of Time's colour pass about 13 ms on an iPad Pro M5, 6 times the Mac's, while its prepass cost 1.3 times. → [Pool marks queue on one word when they test it with an atomic load](../reference/frame-rate.md#pool-marks-queue-on-one-word-when-they-test-it-with-an-atomic-load)
- a busy scene runs far below what its GPU time allows, with the main thread busy for most of each second. The `pipelines up to frame` line shows archive lookups that found none in the thousands per 300 frames, at about 3 ms each, many draws with a hybrid pipeline, and `in flight 384`. Ocarina of Time's castle town market: 9,208 searches, 30.5 s in 300 frames, CPU translation 114 ms a frame. → [Archive searches repeated while the build table is full](../reference/frame-rate.md#archive-searches-repeated-while-the-build-table-is-full)
- frames of 50–170 ms on entering an area, in every run again, although the prewarm lists and the pipeline archive hold everything the area draws. The long-frame line shows `new pipelines 0` or `1` with tens of milliseconds, and the `pipelines up to frame` line shows that time under `predictions`, with a hundred or more `archive lookups found` at about 1 ms each and as many `pulling pipelines made`, against one or two `draws with a hybrid pipeline`. Ocarina of Time's castle town: one prediction made 160 stand-ins in 104 ms, three predictions 244 in 191 ms. → [Pulling stand-ins made for predicted keys that are never drawn](../reference/frame-rate.md#pulling-stand-ins-made-for-predicted-keys-that-are-never-drawn)
- while an area's pipelines are still being built and many draws use a pulling stand-in, the submit thread spends 1 ms or more a frame in predictions: the `pipelines up to frame` line shows thousands of `predictions` per 300 frames, against tens of thousands of `draws with a hybrid pipeline`, while `builds admitted` stays low. → [Predictions replayed every submission of an area's stand-in warm-up](../reference/frame-rate.md#predictions-replayed-every-submission-of-an-areas-stand-in-warm-up)
- on a first visit to an area, on an iPhone, the submit thread spends seconds per 300 frames in predictions: the `pipelines up to frame` line shows `predictions` at 1-7 ms each (478 / 3,533 ms, 527 / 1,724 ms in one window each) with tens of `archive lookups found` at tens of milliseconds and as many `pulling pipelines made`, while the game runs at 12-22 fps. → [Predicted keys' bases looked up on the submit thread](../reference/frame-rate.md#predicted-keys-bases-looked-up-on-the-submit-thread)
- on a first visit, single frames of 0.3–2 s while the pipeline archive holds the area's pulling bases. The `pipelines up to frame` line shows one to eight `draws that waited: pulling base not made yet` with hundreds of milliseconds each, few `draws with a hybrid pipeline`, and its background lookups finding every base they looked for (`base lookups handed to the background 21 (found 21)`). Ocarina of Time on an iPhone 17, entering Kakariko Village and Hyrule Castle: 1 / 861 ms, 2 / 1,081 ms, 8 / 1,786 ms in one 300-frame window each, against 1 / 95 ms on the build without the background lookups. → [A draw that came during another thread's archive lookup compiled its base](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-draw-that-came-during-another-threads-archive-lookup-compiled-its-base)
- in play the picture stops for good while the sound goes on. The log has no red line, no GPU error and no further frame line, and the pipeline archive keeps writing its files. macOS reports no GPU event and the compiler service stays alive. It happens at an area load that compiles pipelines in play, more often late in a game, in areas the prewarm lists do not cover. Ocarina of Time on a 16 GB M2 Pro: three times in one evening (sessions of 39, 25 and 5 minutes), each time during or right after a burst of pipeline compiles; the system log's last `DrawableLifetime` signpost of the process falls inside the burst, the burst's other compiles end up to 3.5 s later, then nothing until the player quits. → [A lookup left to another caller never published its archive result](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-lookup-left-to-another-caller-never-published-its-archive-result)
- frames of 30–150 ms as an area loads, with `new pipelines 0` and a long-frame line such as `texture uploads 31 first + 6 re-uploads, 235.0 MB / 23.7 ms`. With `DM_NVN_REUPLOADS=1` the same large texture is re-uploaded many times within a few frames, several times with the same `submission` number, each for a few written pages (`pages written since its upload: 7 of 2466`). → [A texture written while it is drawn read whole at every use](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-texture-written-while-it-is-drawn-read-whole-at-every-use)
- in a `sample` of the queue's thread (`NVN queue replay`), `mtl_sub_commit` has much time of its own and in `gw_put`, though a submission writes few pages. A game that makes many small submissions a frame pays it each time: Ocarina of Time makes about 30. → [A submission's commit read the whole storage-write page set](../reference/frame-rate.md#a-submissions-commit-read-the-whole-storage-write-page-set)
- the submit thread spends time in `-[AGXTexture newTextureViewWithPixelFormat:…]` under `tex_by_id` and `img_binding` (a `sample` of the process) in a scene that does not change, and the `texture views by texture (vc_get)` line of the memory report counts views made at about half the rate of views reused, while the number of textures holding views stays flat. Ocarina of Time on the Kokiri Forest deck: 121,284 views made against 454,107 reused by frame 5,700, about 40 a frame. → [Texture views made again for render targets initialized again every frame](../reference/frame-rate.md#texture-views-made-again-for-render-targets-initialized-again-every-frame)
- `newTextureViewWithPixelFormat` under `fb_prepare` in the replay thread's samples, every frame. → [Same-pass feedback views made at every draw](../reference/frame-rate.md#same-pass-feedback-views-made-at-every-draw)

## NVN objects

- heap corruption; a stack smash where a function returned to `0x100000000`; a crash in SHA-256 on the code address 0x16300. → [Host records must fit the game's SDK object sizes](../reference/nvn-objects.md#host-records-must-fit-the-games-sdk-object-sizes)
- out-of-range vertices, NaN, a black band and flicker; seen on the iPhone, while Mac runs of the same scene looked clean. → [Inlined SDK code writes into `NVNcommandBuffer` +0x10](../reference/nvn-objects.md#inlined-sdk-code-writes-into-nvncommandbuffer-0x10)
- a running game with sound draws a black screen; every uniform buffer returns the same initialized address, with its allocation offset reported as its size. → [Guest ring allocators write `NVNbuffer` offset and size directly](../reference/nvn-objects.md#guest-ring-allocators-write-nvnbuffer-offset-and-size-directly)
- a game registers the same few sampler states into its sampler pool again and again: Super Mario Odyssey made 3,368 to 5,737 `nvnSamplerPoolRegisterSampler` calls for 168 distinct states in two runs of about 110 s from the regress route's save, against 168 for 168 after the fix. Each re-registration rewrites a pool ID that recorded draws may still use. Its picture on that route (frames 4,500 to 6,300) was the same either way, apart from the hint video's corner, which also varies between runs of one host. → [Games hash a sampler's bytes from 8 on](../reference/nvn-objects.md#games-hash-a-samplers-bytes-from-8-on)
- sprites broke apart in some runs only, depending on the allocation order; buffers overlapped. → [Sizes the hardware never reports as 0 (ZCull save area)](../reference/nvn-objects.md#sizes-the-hardware-never-reports-as-0-zcull-save-area)
- a default sampler selects mipmaps unexpectedly; builder reset loses the assigned device or reports different initial state from native NVN. → [Native NVN defaults](../reference/nvn-objects.md#native-nvn-defaults)
- `MemoryPoolMap` or `BufferMap` returns a writable pointer for a CPU-inaccessible or virtual GPU range; a buffer initializes directly on a physical backing pool. → [CPU mappings and physical backing pools](../reference/nvn-objects.md#cpu-mappings-and-physical-backing-pools)
- a null write as soon as the main thread starts recording a command buffer (13 Sentinels: a constructor at `main+0x2fa9a0` runs on `this = 0` when the prologue enters its first battle). → [Heaps cut from a pool at alignment relative to its GPU address](../reference/nvn-objects.md#heaps-cut-from-a-pool-at-alignment-relative-to-its-gpu-address)
- `[RED] memory pool … created at retry N`. → [`newBufferWithBytesNoCopy` retried under memory pressure](../reference/nvn-objects.md#newbufferwithbytesnocopy-retried-under-memory-pressure)
- a buffer is created with a null pool right after a scene change. → [Virtual memory pools mapped onto one physical pool](../reference/nvn-objects.md#virtual-memory-pools-mapped-onto-one-physical-pool)
- `[RED] MapVirtual: non-contiguous mapping (true sparse) not implemented`. → [Sparse virtual pools built from separate physical pools](../reference/nvn-objects.md#sparse-virtual-pools-built-from-separate-physical-pools)
- `[RED] virtual pool …: more than 1024 separate mapped runs`, then a GPU hang minutes later: `[RED] the host has waited 10 s for GPU work`, an indirect dispatch of absurd size (`283343572 x 965150577 x 1006648320 groups`), and the game aborts once its 15 s `nvnSyncWait` expires. → [More than 1,024 mapped runs in a sparse pool](../reference/nvn-objects.md#more-than-1024-mapped-runs-in-a-sparse-pool)
- in a game that maps and unmaps sparse pages while it runs: an indirect dispatch of absurd size, a GPU hang, then a crash in the game's own code reading a command list that holds another object's data. → [Pages changed under a buffer the GPU still uses](../reference/nvn-objects.md#pages-changed-under-a-buffer-the-gpu-still-uses)
- `[RED] shader <key>: the buffer address the game pushed at c[24] + … is …, in no memory pool: bound to a dummy buffer`, once or twice in minutes, for an address inside a mapped run of a sparse pool; the draw or dispatch of that frame reads zeros. → [A sparse pool's runs replaced in two steps](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-sparse-pools-runs-replaced-in-two-steps)
- `[RED] shader <key>: the buffer address the game pushed at c[24] + … is …, in no memory pool: bound to a dummy buffer (…; sparse pool … (+… of … reserved, … runs): page not mapped…, in no run)`. → [A buffer address on an unmapped page of a sparse pool](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-buffer-address-on-an-unmapped-page-of-a-sparse-pool)
- `[RED] MapVirtual: non-contiguous mapping (true sparse) not implemented`, on small virtual pools; streamed textures black or missing (OCTOPATH TRAVELER's village ground and plants). → [Virtual pools of one texture each, mapped before and after the texture](../reference/nvn-objects.md#virtual-pools-of-one-texture-each-mapped-before-and-after-the-texture)
- none seen in a game yet. The failure it prevents is a texture, buffer, event or command memory made on a virtual pool before its first mapping pointing into address space the host has given back (`CRASH sig=10/11` at an address in no pool, or a texture that reads nothing), once a whole-pool mapping aliases the pool. → [A virtual pool aliased after objects were made on it](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-virtual-pool-aliased-after-objects-were-made-on-it)
- `[RED] sampler ID 4095 not registered`, beside `texture ID 1048575 outside the pool … treated as an empty slot: reads 0`. → [Sampler IDs past the sampler pool](../reference/nvn-objects.md#sampler-ids-past-the-sampler-pool)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnTextureBuilderGetSparseTileLayout`, then an out-of-memory abort in Unreal's allocator (`Failed to allocate 1879113728 bytes from Standard Allocator`, on `DM_DBG=1`). → [The sparse tile layout of a texture](../reference/nvn-objects.md#the-sparse-tile-layout-of-a-texture)
- `CRASH sig=10` in `memmove` from `pkg_upload` → `nvn_deswizzle`, at an address 32 GB into a sparse virtual pool; textures with unaliased tails came out magenta. → [Textures read through a 4 KB page table of a sparse pool](../reference/nvn-objects.md#textures-read-through-a-4-kb-page-table-of-a-sparse-pool)
- a texture of a sparse pool keeps showing the pages it was first read from after the game maps other pages under it, until something unrelated sends its texture ID through the slow path. And in any game with a sparse pool, every serial change (a program bound, a texture uploaded again) re-checks every texture ID and builds every texture pool table whole: the `[nvn-mtl] pool for handles traced to no slot` line counts its builds under `too many or sparse`. → [Sparse mappings under cached texture bindings](../reference/nvn-objects.md#sparse-mappings-under-cached-texture-bindings)
- `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorOutOfMemory` on every command buffer from the first frames, no frame presented, "GPU allocated" near the Mac's memory; `large texture … in a sparse virtual pool` lines name textures of gigabytes. → [Large sparse textures as Metal placement sparse textures](../reference/nvn-objects.md#large-sparse-textures-as-metal-placement-sparse-textures)
- the placement heaps (`sparse tiles: placement heap N`) keep growing through a session, to several times the memory the game maps behind its sparse textures. On a 16 GB M1 Pro, Mario Kart World's race reached 11 heaps (2.75 GB) within three minutes, wired memory 8.7 GB, and the system swapped: frames went from 25 to 50 ms. A GPU write through one place of a page is also not seen at another. → [Physical pages mapped at several places of a placement sparse texture](../reference/nvn-objects.md#physical-pages-mapped-at-several-places-of-a-placement-sparse-texture)
- frames of 100 ms and more every few frames on a Mac short of memory, with no failing command buffer; a Metal System Trace shows the driver wiring and unwiring all the time (3.2 s of a 6 s trace on the game below), and the `restop` line of `DM_CTL` shows gigabytes of shared buffers. → [Large pools wrapped in chunks](../reference/nvn-objects.md#large-pools-wrapped-in-chunks)
- what the GPU copies, fills or renders into a pool never shows, with no error and no red line: garbled text where the game copies its font textures inside one pool. `DM_NVN_POOL_CHUNKS=0` clears it when the buffer is a chunk. → [GPU writes dropped by a pool buffer made over write-protected pages](../reference/nvn-objects.md#gpu-writes-dropped-by-a-pool-buffer-made-over-write-protected-pages)
- GPU memory grows with every scene change (Engage's idle title demo reached 5.4 GB of GPU memory and 3.6 GB resident in 850 s). → [Finalized pools kept alive by residency and the sharing table](../reference/nvn-objects.md#finalized-pools-kept-alive-by-residency-and-the-sharing-table)
- a reset in Kokiri Forest spent 1.3-1.7 s finalizing the game's 4,488 memory pools, nearly all of it waiting for a lock in `defer_obj_at`. → [Thousands of finalized pools each drained the deferred-release queue](../reference/nvn-objects.md#thousands-of-finalized-pools-each-drained-the-deferred-release-queue)
- the memory line's `freed but alive` count only grows, while no host table holds those buffers; "GPU allocated" is far above resident memory (Ghost Trick: Phantom Detective, 125 to 146 buffers and 6.6 to 8.7 GB of GPU allocations against 2.2 GB resident). → [Finalized pool buffers kept by a guest thread's autorelease pool](../reference/nvn-objects.md#finalized-pool-buffers-kept-by-a-guest-threads-autorelease-pool)
- GPU and resident memory keep growing during texture streaming while pool, residency and serial-object counts stay nearly flat, with no freed pool buffers alive. → [Finalized textures kept by registered descriptor views](../reference/nvn-objects.md#finalized-textures-kept-by-registered-descriptor-views)
- the submit thread spends milliseconds a frame in `pool_gpu` → `tex_by_id` → `tex_mtl` / `tex_get` (a `sample` of the process), and the `[nvn-mtl] pool for handles traced to no slot … built in X ms` line shows a rebuild of the texture pool table every frame at about 2 ms. Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave's battle map: 1.9 ms a build, three builds a frame, about 14 % of the main thread. → [Texture ID views revalidated only for the serials that changed](../reference/nvn-objects.md#texture-id-views-revalidated-only-for-the-serials-that-changed)
- the `[nvn-mtl] pool for handles traced to no slot` line counts thousands of builds while the game streams, and the submit thread spends milliseconds a frame walking the texture pool table again. Ocarina of Time, a seven-minute walk through Kokiri Forest: about 61,000 builds of its 33,025-entry table, 53 s in all. → [Pool tables patched instead of built whole](../reference/nvn-objects.md#pool-tables-patched-instead-of-built-whole)
- the submit thread spends a tenth of a millisecond or more a frame in `memcpy` under `pool_walk` in a game that patches its pool tables often (0.13-0.18 ms on an Apple M6; PR #2663 measured about 1 ms of submit and present on an M1 at 30 patches a frame). Ocarina of Time patches 16 to 24 tables a frame of 33,025 entries while Link walks through Kokiri Forest (one per texture type, Pool tables that held only their own texture type), about 5 MB of copies a frame for a few hundred changed entries each; PR #2663 saw about 30 a frame on an older host. → [A patched pool table copied whole](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-patched-pool-table-copied-whole)
- none reproduced here. Expected: an intermittent GPU page fault (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`), then ignored submissions, while the game streams an area: textures registered, first read or given stand-ins, memory pools finalized. → [Live pool tables written while the GPU reads them](../reference/nvn-objects.md#live-pool-tables-written-while-the-gpu-reads-them)
- while a game streams an area in, frames of 100 ms to about 1 s with the GPU at its usual time, no new pipelines and no texture uploads in them: the `[longframe]` lines put the whole frame in `submit+present`, and a long session falls into slow motion for a minute or two at a time. In the `[nvn-mtl] memory up to frame` line, `finalized textures still referenced 512 (small table, N put in total)` stays at 512 and N jumps by millions in one report (651 to 3,669,055 in 300 frames at an area transition; 43 million in one window riding into Hyrule Field), and `deferred-release queue` reaches tens of thousands. After the same game's pause menu, which it streams behind, 50 to 55 fps for about five seconds with `[nvn] pool tables … built whole` at 1.4 to 1.6 a frame (9 ms each) and the pool line's `a new submission over too many retired IDs` counting nearly every build. → [More finalized textures in use than their table held](../reference/nvn-objects.md#more-finalized-textures-in-use-than-their-table-held)
- a pool table keeps naming an sRGB / linear twin's view after the texture it is a view of left the sharing table and, 17 submissions later, the residency set: a texture ID with no residency behind it, which gives GPU address faults in the shaders that sample it (as in issue #1326), and a view that no longer shares its storage's bytes with anything. Seen in `t_pool_notes` only, in no game. → [A view's entry did not hold its parent](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-views-entry-did-not-hold-its-parent)
- in Breath of the Wild, Metal texture classification accounted for 44 of 130 samples in `tex_mtl_` at about 3,000 checks per frame, an estimated 1.5% of submit-thread CPU. PR #1467 → [Repeated Metal texture conformance checks](../reference/nvn-objects.md#repeated-metal-texture-conformance-checks)
- surfaces that read textures by handle through a texture pool come out black or unlit. PRAGMATA: Cabin's monitor in the Shelter stays black (the console shows a cyan animated face), and every cube map read through the pool samples zero (environment reflections and light). No `[RED]` line. → [Pool tables that held only their own texture type](../reference/nvn-objects.md#pool-tables-that-held-only-their-own-texture-type)
- `[RED] [pool-type] texture ID … (ser=… 1x1 format 0x25 target 1) is Metal type 2, read through a type 5 texture pool table: left out (dummy), no view serves it`, or a 3D texture `read through a type 2 texture pool table`, in the frame after a scene change, with a different ID each run. → [Read marks of a texture type kept when an ID changes type](../reference/nvn-objects.md#read-marks-of-a-texture-type-kept-when-an-id-changes-type)
- the host stops with `[dm] CRASH sig=10` (or 11) on a game thread's store into memory a memory pool covered, while another thread finalizes that pool, or finalizes it and initializes a new one over the same memory. Neither is known from a game yet. → [A memory pool retired while another thread uses its tracking slot](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-memory-pool-retired-while-another-thread-uses-its-tracking-slot)
- content kept by its dirty-page stamp is used again after a load although the game wrote new data over it: the game destroyed a memory pool, wrote the next area's data into the same memory and made a new pool over it, and a copy taken from the old data stays, with no red line. A cold start into the same place is right. Not seen in a game on the Mac or iPhone yet; a fork that keeps vertex and index bytes on the GPU by these stamps drew Skyward Sword HD's Skyloft with the opening's meshes after a new game (crates one flat grey shape or missing, large flat green, grey or magenta shapes, flat grey sky). → [A pool registered over memory an earlier pool held counts older stamps as written](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-pool-registered-over-memory-an-earlier-pool-held-counts-older-stamps-as-written)
- the serial table grows by about 10 entries a frame (276,000 after 25,000 frames). → [Dead texture serials kept in a ghost table](../reference/nvn-objects.md#dead-texture-serials-kept-in-a-ghost-table)
- none seen on a game. A finalized texture could resolve to an sRGB / linear view of storage that no longer held its bytes. → [A finalized twin view left in the ghost ring](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-finalized-twin-view-left-in-the-ghost-ring)
- none seen on a game. Windows in which a texture serial could resolve to a texture the sharing table had already let go, or two serials over one storage to two textures. → [Sharing-table lookups and registrations in separate holds](../reference/nvn-objects.md#sharing-table-lookups-and-registrations-in-separate-holds)
- `nvn_replay` aborts on a game's capture with `-[NvProg width]: unrecognized selector` (from `rt_dims`) or `-[NvProg mipmapLevelCount]`: a render target resolves to a program. → [A replay's own objects take serials above a capture's](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-replays-own-objects-take-serials-above-a-captures)
- `DM_NVN_CAPTURE` reports `a record names … (0 bytes), outside every pool … texture registry entry …` for some texture IDs, with addresses that look like data; one capture stopped with `a sparse virtual pool (step 1 does not capture page tables): the capture is incomplete`, another with `straddles a region`. → [A captured texture ID whose memory pool was finalized](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-captured-texture-id-whose-memory-pool-was-finalized)
- `CRASH sig=11 … pc=nvn_sparse_protect+0x40`, an address that looks like data, `x8` 8 below it, after a long session; the backtrace `tex_by_id` ← `tex_mtl` ← `pkg_upload`. The pool need not be sparse. → [A texture read after its memory pool object was freed](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-texture-read-after-its-memory-pool-object-was-freed)
- `regress.py capture` stops with `frame N was not captured within … s` although the game reached the frame before it; a capture takes 5 to 20 minutes where an earlier one of the same frame took 2 to 3, and a sample of the host shows the submitting thread in `lzfseEncodeBase` under `nvn_cap_submit`. → [A frame capture runs under the standard thread policy](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-frame-capture-runs-under-the-standard-thread-policy)
- `nvn_replay` stops with `program #N (a key …) stage S: the translated shader's bindings or inputs differ from the ones the records were captured against`, and `regress.py replay` reports every game's capture as one the candidate cannot replay, while the shaders' reflection (`<key>_b.json`) is the same in both chains apart from the chain field. → [A capture refused after a change to the reflection structs](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-capture-refused-after-a-change-to-the-reflection-structs)
- `texture object no longer valid`, followed by `CRASH sig=10` in `nvn_sparse_pool` through `sparse_gpu_wrote` and `attach`. In Rhythm Heaven Groove's frog stage, the supposed texture contained a texture handle followed by an `OP_DRAW_IDX` record. → [Bound render targets outlive command recordings](../reference/nvn-objects.md#bound-render-targets-outlive-command-recordings)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferGetControlMemoryFree` (or `…GetControlMemorySize`) once, after the game gives a command buffer control memory. → [Control memory capacity queries](../reference/nvn-objects.md#control-memory-capacity-queries)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnQueueBuilderGetCommandMemorySize` (or `…GetComputeMemorySize`, `…GetControlMemorySize`, `…GetRayTracingMemorySize`, `nvnQueueBuilderSetRayTracingMemorySize`), once each, as the game makes its queue. → [Queue builder size getters](../reference/nvn-objects.md#queue-builder-size-getters)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferSetFloatBlendClamp`; now `[RED] nvnCommandBufferSetFloatBlendClamp 1 (a float blend result that overflows becomes the largest finite value): Metal stores infinity`, once. → [Float blend clamp (`nvnCommandBufferSetFloatBlendClamp`)](../reference/nvn-objects.md#float-blend-clamp-nvncommandbuffersetfloatblendclamp)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferSetRepresentativeFragmentTest`, once. → [Representative fragment test (`nvnCommandBufferSetRepresentativeFragmentTest`)](../reference/nvn-objects.md#representative-fragment-test-nvncommandbuffersetrepresentativefragmenttest)
- `FATAL NVN command memory: the game's pool is empty…`. → [Host-lent command memory segments](../reference/nvn-objects.md#host-lent-command-memory-segments)
- `FATAL NVN command memory: the game's pool is empty` after the host-lent segment pool grows to 1 GiB, despite the game reusing its command-memory ring. → [Small command-memory grants lose their guest origin](../reference/nvn-objects.md#small-command-memory-grants-lose-their-guest-origin)
- a recorded list that the game keeps calling replays another list's records from some point on: wrong uniform buffers or state bound, a scene that alternates between right and wrong lighting frame by frame. Only once the host lends segments (the line `the host lends a segment` in the log). → [A command memory grant over a list whose tail is lent](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-command-memory-grant-over-a-list-whose-tail-is-lent)
- `FATAL NVN command memory: the game's pool is empty and host-lent segments reached 1024 MB without being reclaimed by the game's blocks`, after the log's `the host lends a segment` line counts up at a steady rate and the segment pool grows with every loan (`segment pool 4096 segments` at the 4,096th): about two loans a frame, the cap at frame 1,939 to 2,180 (72 to 77 s at 30 fps). The first loan is at frame 0, after `the game's callback did not add enough (… need 1400 bytes)`. → [A recording that runs short before it writes into the game's memory](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-recording-that-runs-short-before-it-writes-into-the-games-memory)
- frames stop after about 15 s; a game thread spins in its command-memory callback (a ring allocator at `main+0x9cc120` waiting for fences that have all signalled). → [What `GetCommandMemoryUsed` counts](../reference/nvn-objects.md#what-getcommandmemoryused-counts)
- `CRASH sig=10` in `mtl_exec` after minutes of play, the fault address equal to `x19` and shaped like `0x1000000003`. → [Prerecorded lists in slots of their measured size](../reference/nvn-objects.md#prerecorded-lists-in-slots-of-their-measured-size)
- a model load runs out of its resource heap: `[RED] BufferInitialize: pool has no storage (pool 0x0 …)`, then `CRASH sig=11` in `h_nvnMemoryPoolGetFlags` (Model heaps sized by the host's command record sizes). → [A recording's end and links are in control memory](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-recordings-end-and-links-are-in-control-memory)
- `FATAL NVN command list 0x…: record {op 8, size 504} at 0x…, 0 bytes into its piece of 16 at 0x…`, then `CRASH sig=6` in `nvn_queue_submit`; now `[RED] NVN command list 0x…: record {op …, size …} at 0x…, … bytes into its piece of … at 0x…, does not fit: the piece was recorded over after it was called`. → [A called recording recorded over before its caller is submitted](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-called-recording-recorded-over-before-its-caller-is-submitted)
- a jump to pc=0 on a worker thread, with the backtrace `0x0` ← `cmd_alloc_keep` ← `h_nvnCommandBufferCopyBufferToTexture` and `lr` in the game's `main`, just after the instruction that called the game's command memory callback. → [Copy strides are command buffer state](../reference/nvn-objects.md#copy-strides-are-command-buffer-state)
- `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x50` on a game thread, at `pc=main+0x385201c` in Super Smash Bros. Ultimate (`ldr x0, [x8, #0x10]` with `x8 = 0x40`), as a battle starts. Rare: it crashed on 1 of 2 battle starts in one run on a Mac mini and on none of 11 in another. → [`GetCommandMemorySize` read while another thread records](../reference/nvn-objects.md#getcommandmemorysize-read-while-another-thread-records)
- the title showed one stretched 64×64 icon over the 3D scene, a new game a white screen; post-process passes with stale texture handles; `[RED] bind record … overwritten after recording`. → [Recorded commands patched in place by the game (`OP_BIND_TEX1`)](../reference/nvn-objects.md#recorded-commands-patched-in-place-by-the-game-op_bind_tex1)
- intermittent scene-wide lighting flashes and a persistent pale outline around a character. Extra GPU readbacks or synchronous screenshots can make the flashes disappear temporarily. Separately: twice the render passes and attachment loads a frame, with a correct picture, in a game that signals events inside its passes. → [GPU event waits order work across queues](../reference/nvn-objects.md#gpu-event-waits-order-work-across-queues)
- choosing X-2 after the collection launcher crashes in `keys_hash`, reading control address `0x8000000000`; the program object now contains command-buffer data. → [Bindings belong to each queue](../reference/nvn-objects.md#bindings-belong-to-each-queue)
- about 23 s after launch, when the title screen hands over to the opening demo, a crash in `keys_hash` (`prog_key` ← `prog_get` ← `bind_stages` ← `prog_resolve` ← `mtl_queue_activate`), reading a control address made of float data; the program object now holds other data. → [Finalized programs leave every queue's bindings](../reference/nvn-objects.md#finalized-programs-leave-every-queues-bindings)
- `nvnCommandBufferFenceSync` unimplemented, every `SyncWait` timed out at once. → [Fence syncs recorded in command buffers](../reference/nvn-objects.md#fence-syncs-recorded-in-command-buffers)
- `FATAL submit re-entered` in the first frames. → [Submissions from two queues on two threads](../reference/nvn-objects.md#submissions-from-two-queues-on-two-threads)
- going to the background (minimizing the Mac window; in nxnative's iOS app, `sceneDidEnterBackground`) while the "Preparing shaders" or "Preparing pipelines" screen is up blocks the main thread until the step ends: minutes on a first start, long enough for iOS to end nxnative's app. The log's `[nvn] graphics suspended` line comes only after the `pipeline prepare:` line. → [Going to the background during the prepare step](../reference/nvn-objects.md#going-to-the-background-during-the-prepare-step)
- `SIGSEGV` at frame 1 before the logos (`main+0x309128` on 13 Sentinels Asia 1.00, `main+0x309e28` on Western 1.10, `x0 = entries + 0xffffffff * 0xe8`) whenever the first submission's archive open or prepare step takes 7 s or more — either opening a large `.mtl4a` archive from a slow external disk (13 of 13 starts ≥ 7 s on a USB 2.0 disk, 0 of 8 below 6.5 s, in PR #2936) or running a cold `prepare_prebuild` (10.0 s for 376 shader pairs on an Apple M6). → [Guest threads running ahead during the prepare step or archive open](../reference/nvn-objects.md#guest-threads-running-ahead-during-the-prepare-step-or-archive-open)
- frames stop, no crash, the main thread sits in `nvnSyncWait(sync, -1)`, and the watchdog's wait-state line shows the main fence one short (`main fence submitted N / completed N-1`). → [A GPU command buffer that never completes](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-gpu-command-buffer-that-never-completes)
- `game called nn::diag abort` right after a screen change (PRAGMATA: the Initial Settings menu's Next), with nothing slow before it. → [A sync never fenced since its Initialize is signalled](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-sync-never-fenced-since-its-initialize-is-signalled)
- the frame's blocking SyncWait on its newest fence wakes about 0.26–0.29 ms after the GPU end (M2 Max; the wait ledger, `DM_NVN_WAITS=1`), on the critical path of every frame. → [The game's SyncWait wakes on the GPU's own signal of its fence](../reference/nvn-objects.md#the-games-syncwait-wakes-on-the-gpus-own-signal-of-its-fence)
- the game stops after a GPU error it recovers from (a page fault, a hang or timeout, an innocent victim's discard): a thread waits in `waitUntilSignaledValue:timeoutMS:` on the shared event although the fence's feedback has come back (`g_done >= fence`). → [A failed command buffer completes without signalling its fence](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-failed-command-buffer-completes-without-signalling-its-fence)
- `[nvn-mtl] FATAL post ring: the writes of fence <N> never ran` at a submission's commit (`mtl_sub_commit`), with the new fence exactly the ring's size past `<N>`, in a game that makes many submissions a frame. → [The post-completion ring is smaller than the submissions in flight](../reference/nvn-objects.md#the-post-completion-ring-is-smaller-than-the-submissions-in-flight)
- loading screens many times slower on the iPhone than on the Mac (Three Houses' first loading screen 78 s on the iPhone, 5 s on the Mac). → [`nvnQueueFinish` waits for the GPU, not the display](../reference/nvn-objects.md#nvnqueuefinish-waits-for-the-gpu-not-the-display)
- CPU texture uploads spend time recalculating block-linear addresses and calling variable-length `memcpy` for every 16-byte segment. → [CPU deswizzle copies use slice and row offsets](../reference/nvn-objects.md#cpu-deswizzle-copies-use-slice-and-row-offsets)
- a Switch 2 game's textures from its files keep their shapes but break up along every edge: misplaced 4×4 blocks (16-byte pieces) in a regular weave, invisible in flat areas. Normal maps make lit surfaces look woven; opacity masks punch holes. → [The Switch 2's order inside a GOB](../reference/nvn-objects.md#the-switch-2s-order-inside-a-gob)
- driver comparisons report the wrong block dimensions for packaged 2D / 3D textures, or too little storage for linear block-compressed textures (64x64 BC1 with stride 128: 2048 bytes instead of 8192). → [Packaged texture block dimensions and linear storage rows](../reference/nvn-objects.md#packaged-texture-block-dimensions-and-linear-storage-rows)
- huge stretched terrain walls and ceilings, flickering, while characters and the HUD are right: `WriteTexels` into a texture overwrote the mesh data stored right after it. → [Minimal texture layouts](../reference/nvn-objects.md#minimal-texture-layouts)
- menus and dialogue boxes draw but contain no text, while images and pictogram digits are fine; the sky is black (CPU-written linear colour ramps). → [Linear textures in CPU-visible pools](../reference/nvn-objects.md#linear-textures-in-cpu-visible-pools)
- a trap during graphics initialization in `SetupSurfaceInfoMemoryInside`, asserting `ctx.memorySize <= localmemsetupinfo.memorySize`. → [Linear render-target storage without the LINEAR flag](../reference/nvn-objects.md#linear-render-target-storage-without-the-linear-flag)
- `nvnTextureReadTexels` of a block-linear texture with the `IMAGE` flag that the CPU wrote (`WriteTexels`) returns scrambled rows: the bytes are its block-linear storage, copied as if they were linear rows. → [A CPU-written block-linear IMAGE texture reads back with scrambled rows](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-cpu-written-block-linear-image-texture-reads-back-with-scrambled-rows)
- on an iPhone only, a scene's backgrounds are solid magenta and magenta frames flash, while the Mac is right. The log has `[RED] linear texture offset … / row pitch 32 not aligned to 64 bytes; created as a private texture (CPU writes invisible)`, then `[RED] deswizzle failed format 0x25 target 1`. Or movie backgrounds are grey and flicker, or show green and magenta blocks, after `linear texture ser=… 736x480 format 0x1 flags 0x14: offset … / row pitch 736 not aligned to 64 bytes` and `[RED] linear texture 8x8 format 0x9a flags 0x14: rows of 32 bytes no view can take, a private texture the CPU's writes do not reach`. → [Linear textures a device cannot view on their pool's buffer](../reference/nvn-objects.md#linear-textures-a-device-cannot-view-on-their-pools-buffer)
- video frames, or a game's built-in default textures, are all zero. → [Textures written with `nvnTextureWriteTexels`](../reference/nvn-objects.md#textures-written-with-nvntexturewritetexels)
- after a few minutes of play the main thread spends whole frames in `WriteTexels` (Metroid Dread's frames stopped at about 175 s). → [`WriteTexels` regions written in place by block-linear address](../reference/nvn-objects.md#writetexels-regions-written-in-place-by-block-linear-address)
- a movie in native-texture mode draws black (or a texture the game fills with `WriteTexels` stays empty), and the texture is `LINEAR` or `LINEAR_RENDER_TARGET`. When only part of the texture is written, the log has `[RED] WriteTexels deswizzle failed`. A write of the whole texture logged nothing. → [`WriteTexels` into a linear texture dropped the write](../reference/nvn-objects.md#writetexels-into-a-linear-texture-dropped-the-write)
- black screen with every geometry right (`DM_NVN_HILITE` shows each sprite and glyph in place); the uniform buffer right (a `CBDUMP`), so every texture read 0. → [A pool's storage at creation is its textures' content](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-pools-storage-at-creation-is-its-textures-content)
- a game draws its UI and text but every image is black; `packaged textures: first uploads 0` while `TextureInitialize` counts the textures the game created (SD GUNDAM G GENERATION CROSS RAYS: 74, of which the 10 BC3 ones hold data and none is uploaded). After the fill works, one overlay still comes up with its text over nothing: the panel's background is missing and the text of the next block sits on top of it. → [`COMPRESSIBLE` in a pool the CPU writes is only a hint](../reference/nvn-objects.md#compressible-in-a-pool-the-cpu-writes-is-only-a-hint)
- a texture the game filled with `memcpy` and flushed reads as zeros (BotW's sky brownish grey, the distant haze brown). → [First-use fill of storage the game flushed](../reference/nvn-objects.md#first-use-fill-of-storage-the-game-flushed)
- a CPU-cached buffer holds texture pixels, but a texture sharing its storage reads back as zeros after `nvnBufferFlushMappedRange`. → [Buffer flushes publish the corresponding pool range](../reference/nvn-objects.md#buffer-flushes-publish-the-corresponding-pool-range)
- magenta (ASTC's error colour) where UI images belong: a title logo and menu panels, while the 3D scene and the text draw; `DM_NVN_ZBLOCK=1` reports nothing (the textures are not uploaded on the CPU at all); `DM_NVN_TEXINFO=WxH` shows storage holding data at first use (`non-zero …`) in a pool with flags 0x22 (`CPU_UNCACHED | GPU_CACHED`). → [Storage of a CPU-uncached pool at first use](../reference/nvn-objects.md#storage-of-a-cpu-uncached-pool-at-first-use)
- a picture that should change with a selection is black, or shows the previous item's picture while the item's name and description are right. `DM_NVN_TEXINFO=WxH` shows `swizzled=0` at first use with storage holding data, no packaged layout, in a `CPU_CACHED` or `CPU_UNCACHED` pool, and `DM_NVN_DIRTYCHECK=1` reports no missed write. → [Block-compressed storage the CPU wrote before first use](../reference/nvn-objects.md#block-compressed-storage-the-cpu-wrote-before-first-use)
- a movie plays black (Age of Calamity: its intro movie under the "+ Skip" prompt). → [Mapped-range flushes taken as uploads (`tex_flush_upload`)](../reference/nvn-objects.md#mapped-range-flushes-taken-as-uploads-tex_flush_upload)
- open-world ground is one flat colour on a flat plane with blocky seams, or water and sand are missing, while course meshes, rocks and the kart look right. `DM_NVN_TEXINFO=WxH` shows uncompressed textures with `swizzled=0`, no packaged layout and storage holding data in a `CPU_CACHED` pool. → [Uncompressed storage the CPU wrote, in a pool the GPU has not written](../reference/nvn-objects.md#uncompressed-storage-the-cpu-wrote-in-a-pool-the-gpu-has-not-written)
- after some walking, the ground opens in holes (black, or the water and sky below showing through) and the hole spreads to areas loaded later, including ones that were fine before; saving and loading the game makes it right again for a while. A diagnostic line at the GPU texture's creation shows uncompressed terrain textures in a `CPU_CACHED | GPU_CACHED` pool (flags 0x24) whose storage holds data, not flushed, made as GPU textures of zeros. → [Uncompressed storage beside storage the GPU writes](../reference/nvn-objects.md#uncompressed-storage-beside-storage-the-gpu-writes)
- after some walking, a whole terrain tile is missing (the ground vanishes, or the tile sits flat and low) or shows another surface with a hard square edge (a square of grass across a cobbled path, a grass patch on a mountain trail); a reload makes it right for a while. The host's copy of the tile's 260x260 R32F height map or RGBA8 hole map is all zeros while the game's memory holds data (`DM_NVN_TEXINFO=260x260` shows storage non-zero at first use; a `texraw` dump of the host texture is zero). → [A span's sliver inside a texture](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-spans-sliver-inside-a-texture)
- magenta surfaces (Metal decodes an all-zero ASTC block as the error colour (1, 0, 1)); a zero BC block decodes black, which looks like missing light; black screen while the UI (or a dialog box) draws in the right places under `HILITE` or in the pass dump; `packaged textures: first uploads 0`. → [CPU writes with no flush, caught by page protection (`tex_dirty_upload`)](../reference/nvn-objects.md#cpu-writes-with-no-flush-caught-by-page-protection-tex_dirty_upload)
- a texture the game builds from CPU-filled textures read back with `nvnTextureReadTexels` comes out zero. → [`ReadTexels` from guest memory and textures written after first use](../reference/nvn-objects.md#readtexels-from-guest-memory-and-textures-written-after-first-use)
- with `tex_dirty_upload`, a texture the CPU fills again after its first use keeps its old content: Ocarina of Time's ground opened flat blue holes with grass and rocks floating over them (Kokiri Forest, the village floor, the Deku Tree) after an hour of play, as terrain tile slots were reused; no red line, and the frame's `texture uploads … re-uploads` stays at 0 while the pool's pages change. → [Textures made for GPU use kept from their CPU rewrites](../reference/nvn-objects.md#textures-made-for-gpu-use-kept-from-their-cpu-rewrites)
- `[RED] upload batches belong to the submit thread only: up_begin called on thread '…'`, then an abort, with `pkg_upload` ← `tex_mtl` ← `mtl_read_texels` on the stack; or `CRASH sig=10` in `nvn_deswizzle` ← `mtl_read_texels`, at an address in a sparse virtual pool's reserved range. → [`ReadTexels` on a texture of a sparse pool](../reference/nvn-objects.md#readtexels-on-a-texture-of-a-sparse-pool)
- the same abort as above, `[RED] upload batches belong to the submit thread only: up_begin called on thread '…'` with `pkg_upload` ← `tex_mtl_` ← `mtl_read_texels`, on a texture of an ordinary pool; at random, from minutes to hours into a session (after a shrine, loading a save, entering a sky island), more often with less memory. → [`ReadTexels` while another thread reclassifies the texture](../reference/nvn-objects.md#readtexels-while-another-thread-reclassifies-the-texture)
- a streamed texture's small mip levels hold another texture or zeros while its large levels are right (`texraw <serial>.<level>`), so surfaces sampled with a mip bias or at a distance show bright speckles, dark blotches and sharp detail where the Switch shows soft texture (Yoshi's felt), from the moment finer levels stream in (the opening going dark). → [GPU copies into textures uploaded from their storage](../reference/nvn-objects.md#gpu-copies-into-textures-uploaded-from-their-storage)
- while a scene streams in, frames of seconds whose `[longframe]` line has hundreds of re-uploads and gigabytes (`texture uploads 98 first + 280 re-uploads, 12287.6 MB / 8091.3 ms`); picture, sound and controller drift behind. With `DM_NVN_REUPLOADS=1` each re-upload follows one GPU copy into the texture, which wrote a few of its thousands of pages. → [A GPU copy into a texture uploaded from its storage uploaded all of it again](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-gpu-copy-into-a-texture-uploaded-from-its-storage-uploaded-all-of-it-again)
- for a frame or more, whole characters, skies and walls show coloured block noise or another texture's blocks, mostly at scene changes and loading screens; the frame after is right. More often on a slower GPU (MacBook Neo) than on an M4 Pro. → [Buffer copies over the storage of textures uploaded from it](../reference/nvn-objects.md#buffer-copies-over-the-storage-of-textures-uploaded-from-it)
- what the GPU drew into a texture is lost later: a texture re-created from the same memory, or one the game moved with `memcpy`, comes out zero; on the Switch it holds the drawing. Tears of the Kingdom: the grass of a meadow is drawn with no length and the ground under it goes dark, a few seconds after loading, tile by tile around Link. → [Render passes into textures uploaded from their storage](../reference/nvn-objects.md#render-passes-into-textures-uploaded-from-their-storage)
- after several area changes (dungeon entries and exits), terrain is covered in crossing stripes ("plaid"), tile by tile, and stays so until the game is restarted; loading a save alone does not bring it. A texture re-created over storage the game moved with `memcpy` comes out zero although the GPU drew its source. `texraw` of the affected textures (Ocarina of Time: 260x260 RG8 normal tiles, format `0xd`, 9 levels) is all zero, and the log names them `texture written by the CPU before its first use … (uncompressed, in storage the GPU has not written)` although the CPU only copied them. → [GPU textures in storage the CPU can read](../reference/nvn-objects.md#gpu-textures-in-storage-the-cpu-can-read)
- the `[nvn] residency set up to frame …` line shows hundreds of additions from `mirror_readback`'s line in `nvn_mtl.m` every 300 frames, and as many removals, with the residency commits' time up. Tears of the Kingdom: about 1,500 every 300 frames on an iPhone 17 (1.4.2, seedds/nxnative#931), about 370 on a Mac. → [Readback buffers kept for reuse](../reference/nvn-objects.md#readback-buffers-kept-for-reuse)
- textures that should hold CPU-written data come out magenta (ASTC's error colour) or black. → [Sampled textures in a sparse pool uploaded through the page table](../reference/nvn-objects.md#sampled-textures-in-a-sparse-pool-uploaded-through-the-page-table)
- bright magenta patches on materials that sample block-compressed textures. The Metal texture can contain all-zero ASTC blocks while the corresponding CPU-visible pool holds texture data. → [Block-compressed textures with sparse layout in CPU-visible pools](../reference/nvn-objects.md#block-compressed-textures-with-sparse-layout-in-cpu-visible-pools)
- a sparse texture whose levels the game fills with buffer-to-texture copies stays magenta (ASTC's error colour) or black for good; with `DM_NVN_ZBLOCK=1` its `[zb] re-upload` lines show the copied levels all-zero at every upload. A copy into a level of a texture bound from a coarser level (`sampled from level N … (finer levels not mapped)`) went to the wrong level. → [GPU copies into a sparse pool's textures, stored through the page table](../reference/nvn-objects.md#gpu-copies-into-a-sparse-pools-textures-stored-through-the-page-table)
- the submit thread spends most of its time in `nvn_dirty_since` under `nvn_sparse_since`, called from `tex_mtl` (render-target and texture lookups of textures in a sparse pool). → [A sparse texture's CPU-write check at every lookup](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-sparse-textures-cpu-write-check-at-every-lookup)
- some textures are re-uploaded every frame (several MB per frame). → [Dirty-page ranges of pools that share a host page](../reference/nvn-objects.md#dirty-page-ranges-of-pools-that-share-a-host-page)
- a CPU-written texture stays stale although its storage changed while the host made a zero-copy pool buffer. → [A write missed while opening shared pool pages](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-write-missed-while-opening-shared-pool-pages)
- packaged textures re-uploaded at every use with unchanged content; upload staging and frame times rising. → [Writes beside a packaged texture in a shared host page](../reference/nvn-objects.md#writes-beside-a-packaged-texture-in-a-shared-host-page)
- with `tex_dirty_upload`, textures the CPU never writes (window textures, render targets) are logged as `CPU-written texture …` or `registered texture written by the CPU after its first use: uploaded from its storage` soon after boot. The present thread then re-uploads a window texture from its storage, and the host aborts: `[RED] upload batches belong to the submit thread only: up_begin called on thread 'nvn-present'`, with `pkg_upload` ← `tex_mtl_` ← `present_now` on the stack. → [Writes beside a registered texture in a shared host page](../reference/nvn-objects.md#writes-beside-a-registered-texture-in-a-shared-host-page)
