- about one run in four stalled early (around the opening movie or the title), with no log output, not even the time-limit report. → [Dirty-page tracker race and early stalls](../reference/nvn-objects.md#dirty-page-tracker-race-and-early-stalls)
- a CPU-written texture stays stale after later writes; letters can be missing from a glyph cache. → [Dirty-page writes missed during upload](../reference/nvn-objects.md#dirty-page-writes-missed-during-upload)
- a host system call writing into storage after its NVN pool was finalized fails with `EFAULT`; a direct write can fault after the tracker stopped owning the page. → [A retired pool remains read-only](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-retired-pool-remains-read-only)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnSamplerBuilderGetLodBias`, `nvnSamplerBuilderGetCompare`, `nvnSamplerBuilderGetBorderColor`, `nvnSamplerBuilderGetMaxAnisotropy` or `nvnSamplerBuilderGetReductionFilter` during renderer setup. → [Sampler builder queries leave renderer state unread](../reference/nvn-objects.md#sampler-builder-queries-leave-renderer-state-unread)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnSamplerBuilderSetBorderColorui` during renderer initialization. → [Integer sampler border colours](../reference/nvn-objects.md#integer-sampler-border-colours)
- `[RED] sampler ID 4096 not registered` (or another ID past the sampler pool), once, from the first frames on. → [Bound handles are the driver's 32-bit words](../reference/nvn-objects.md#bound-handles-are-the-drivers-32-bit-words)
- device / subsystem setup returns quietly and the next stage never starts. → [Version and capability checks that fail quietly](../reference/nvn-objects.md#version-and-capability-checks-that-fail-quietly)
- none seen: a Switch 2 game asking `nvnDeviceGetInteger` got the Tegra X1's values (measured on a Switch 1), and items past 0x5F answered 0 with a red line. → [The Switch 2's device values](../reference/nvn-objects.md#the-switch-2s-device-values)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferBeginTransformFeedback` (or another of the 17 entries below), once per entry; `[RED] nvnProgramInitializeFromBuilder with N ray tracing modules: the host has no ray tracing … no program`; `[RED] shader … is a geometry shader: the host has no such stage …, and its program draws without it` (also `hull`, `domain`); or `[RED] primitive type 14 not implemented …`. → [Entries for transform feedback, tessellation and ray tracing report in red](../reference/nvn-objects.md#entries-for-transform-feedback-tessellation-and-ray-tracing-report-in-red)
- a SIGSEGV on the game's `Renderer` thread a few seconds into loading, before the first frame, at `main+0x1aa90ac` (Frostpunk 1.0.1): a virtual call through an object whose first words are floats (±1.0, a quad's vertices), with the slot argument 64. → [Separate sampler bindings per stage](../reference/nvn-objects.md#separate-sampler-bindings-per-stage)
- a jump to pc=0 right after the NVN bootstrap loader (`lr=main+0x37fe930`, `[RED] NVN address lookup of an unknown name: nvnSetExternalDebuggerEnabledNVX`). → [Extensions fetched by name and called unchecked](../reference/nvn-objects.md#extensions-fetched-by-name-and-called-unchecked)
- SIGBUS in `AGCDeserializedReply`, address in the guest stack region. → [NVN entry points on the big host stack](../reference/nvn-objects.md#nvn-entry-points-on-the-big-host-stack)
- red `NVN unimplemented function called` lines for `nvnColorStateGetBlendEnable`, `nvnBlendStateGetBlendFunc`, `nvnPolygonStateGetCullFace`, `nvnDepthStencilStateGetDepthTestEnable`, `nvnBufferBuilderGetSize`, `nvnTextureBuilderGetWidth`, …. → [State read back through NVN getters](../reference/nvn-objects.md#state-read-back-through-nvn-getters)
- querying a configured builder logs `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called`, returns a null storage/device pointer, or reports the wrong size, flags or sampling mode. → [Builder queries return pointers and retained state](../reference/nvn-objects.md#builder-queries-return-pointers-and-retained-state)
- an abort in device setup when `nvnTexturePoolGetSize` / `nvnSamplerPoolGetSize` returned 0. → [Pool size getters, texture address and command memory size](../reference/nvn-objects.md#pool-size-getters-texture-address-and-command-memory-size)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnTextureBuilderGetMemoryOffset`, then `nvnTextureBuilderGetRawStorageClass`. → [Builder storage getters](../reference/nvn-objects.md#builder-storage-getters)
- `[nvn-mtl] FATAL buffer copy address is in no memory pool src=0x70d08a8b00 dst=0 n=0x29a00` as a match loads: a recorded `CopyBufferToBuffer` whose destination is 0. → [`Map` of a pool without CPU access returns NULL](../reference/nvn-objects.md#map-of-a-pool-without-cpu-access-returns-null)
- red stub lines for NVN calls. → [NVN calls that were red stubs](../reference/nvn-objects.md#nvn-calls-that-were-red-stubs)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnDeviceRegisterFastClearStencil` at startup, or the depth registration reaching the same stub. → [`nvnDeviceRegisterFastClearDepth` is a hint](../reference/nvn-objects.md#nvndeviceregisterfastcleardepth-is-a-hint)
- `nvnWindowGetPresentInterval`, `nvnCommandBufferSetCopyImageStride` missing. → [SDK 8 window and copy-stride entry points](../reference/nvn-objects.md#sdk-8-window-and-copy-stride-entry-points)
- `[RED] texture bound to an image slot lacks the shader-write usage`, and compute image writes go to a dummy texture. → [Image bindings supply shader-write intent](../reference/nvn-objects.md#image-bindings-supply-shader-write-intent)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called` for each. → [`nvnTextureBuilderGetViewOffset`, `nvnTextureViewCompare`, `nvnBlendStateSetAdvancedNormalizedDst`](../reference/nvn-objects.md#nvntexturebuildergetviewoffset-nvntextureviewcompare-nvnblendstatesetadvancednormalizeddst)
- a pool walk makes gigabytes of textures from far fewer images: Ocarina of Time's 32,768 IDs name 3,607 storages, and one texture per ID came to 2 GB. → [Texture IDs of one storage share one packaged texture](../reference/nvn-objects.md#texture-ids-of-one-storage-share-one-packaged-texture)
- area loads take longer and longer over a long session in one process: Ocarina of Time loading saves from the pause menu grew from 80 ms (>30 ms frames per load) in loads 1–20 to 584–640 ms in loads 121–200 (1.2–1.5 s for some areas), all in `submit+present` with the GPU idle; a `sample` shows `mtl_alias_drop_pool` as the hottest function, with the replay thread and game threads waiting on `g_smu` and `g_cpu_tex_mu`. Upstream issue #2507 reports the same after 4–9.5 hours of play. → [Packaged-texture and CPU-written tables walked by every memory pool Finalize](../reference/nvn-objects.md#packaged-texture-and-cpu-written-tables-walked-by-every-memory-pool-finalize)
- after 15 to 45 minutes of play, a segment command buffer fails with `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault` and every later submission is refused (`SubmissionsIgnored`): the picture freezes, or turns pink, while the audio goes on. With `DM_RES_CHECK=1`, `missing residency` lines name 260x260 textures before the fault. → [A texture shared by its packaged texture's serials left the residency set with one of them](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-texture-shared-by-its-packaged-textures-serials-left-the-residency-set-with-one-of-them)
- the NVN submit thread spends CPU instructions handing Objective-C objects back to callers at texture resolution, registered texture-ID lookup, and render pass begin (PR #1473). → [ARC traffic at texture and render encoder lookups](../reference/nvn-objects.md#arc-traffic-at-texture-and-render-encoder-lookups)
- `CRASH sig=11` in `class_conformsToProtocol` (`_class_conformsToProtocol_unrealized_nolock`) on a Metal completion handler's thread, `bt: class_conformsToProtocol -[NSObject conformsToProtocol:] defer_drain __mtl_sub_commit_block_invoke`, with a garbage class pointer in x0. → [A deferred-release drain read entries it had handed back to the queue](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-deferred-release-drain-read-entries-it-had-handed-back-to-the-queue)
- thousands of first uploads a frame in a scene whose textures do not change: Ocarina of Time, about 15,000 a frame. → [Finalizing a copy of a texture object](../reference/nvn-objects.md#finalizing-a-copy-of-a-texture-object)
- the host stops with `NSInvalidArgumentException: -[NvPkg buffer]: unrecognized selector sent to instance` under `tex_by_id` (via `pool_entry`, `pool_gpu`, `encode_args_`, `draw_`, `mesh_tasks_draw`); not a GPU hang, no `kIOGPU` errors. It came after minutes of play, right after a save or at a scene change. → [Freeing a memory pool during a texture ID walk](../reference/nvn-objects.md#freeing-a-memory-pool-during-a-texture-id-walk)
- after a GPU copy into one image of a texture uploaded from its storage, and a CPU write into another image of it in the same command buffer, the copied image comes out as the storage's old bytes through every texture object over that storage, while the storage itself holds the copy. The CPU-written image and the untouched ones are right. → [A pending storage write through one alias is lost by another alias's upload](../reference/nvn-objects.md#a-pending-storage-write-through-one-alias-is-lost-by-another-aliass-upload)
- sparse-texture checks spend substantial submission CPU time searching thousands of memory pools. A cache of any containing live range can also miss writes to a shared host page and leave texture content stale. → [Cached dirty-range lookups with overlapping pools](../reference/nvn-objects.md#cached-dirty-range-lookups-with-overlapping-pools)

## Rendering

- a direct zero-sized grid changes replay state or reaches Metal's invalid empty-grid dispatch API. → [Direct compute dispatches with an empty grid](../reference/rendering.md#direct-compute-dispatches-with-an-empty-grid)
- Shader Validation aborts with `Texture is not resident` in a fragment sampling copy even though the texture's heap is resident. → [Views of placed textures need their parent resident](../reference/rendering.md#views-of-placed-textures-need-their-parent-resident)
- a compressed texture's Metal copy and guest storage disagree after a GPU copy. FIFA 23 shows a corrupt checkerboard in place of the controller-button glyph before Select. → [GPU copies into compressed IMAGE textures](../reference/rendering.md#gpu-copies-into-compressed-image-textures)
- clang reports null dereferences in `nvnWindowInitialize` and `clear_tex`; clearing a null texture crashes with `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP=1`. → [Null textures in diagnostic logs](../reference/rendering.md#null-textures-in-diagnostic-logs)
- Metal aborts on a texture descriptor with an absurd size (`height (1869367126) greater than … 16384`). → [Texture objects copied into command records](../reference/rendering.md#texture-objects-copied-into-command-records)
- a decoded movie stays black even though its YUV textures contain the picture; disabling its depth test makes it visible. → [New textures start as zero](../reference/rendering.md#new-textures-start-as-zero)
- a scene background is green and retains silhouettes of moving characters; pixels a character covers can recover their normal colour. The resolved scene is already wrong before postprocessing. → [New textures start as zero](../reference/rendering.md#new-textures-start-as-zero)
- glyphs render as white boxes (the font atlas is `(1, 1, 1, R)`). → [Texture swizzle through swizzled Metal views](../reference/rendering.md#texture-swizzle-through-swizzled-metal-views)
- RGB565, RGB5A1 or RGB5 textures sample with red and blue exchanged: smeared, banded rock, the shapes right and the colours wrong; or what the GPU cleared, drew or copied into such a texture reads back to the game, or samples, with red and blue exchanged. → [RGB565-family textures in Metal's channel order](../reference/rendering.md#rgb565-family-textures-in-metals-channel-order)
- outdoor battle ground and lower foliage are nearly black while the characters and the UI stay lit: a baked shadowmask takes the sunlight away. RGBA4 textures sample with their four channels reversed. → [RGBA4 textures in Metal's channel order](../reference/rendering.md#rgba4-textures-in-metals-channel-order)
- dark boxes and cream cloud rectangles behind HUD prompts, a black stamina wheel and a black System menu background (layout captures). → [`nvnTextureCompare` compares the channel mapping](../reference/rendering.md#nvntexturecompare-compares-the-channel-mapping)
- Metal validation aborts in `tex_by_id` with `MTLPixelFormatDepth32Float` not compatible with `MTLPixelFormatR32Float`. → [Sampling a depth attachment through a red texture view](../reference/rendering.md#sampling-a-depth-attachment-through-a-red-texture-view)
- `newTextureViewWithPixelFormat` takes a large share of the replay thread (45% in Age of Calamity) when a game registers textures into a ring of IDs before every bind. → [Texture views cached by texture and view parameters](../reference/rendering.md#texture-views-cached-by-texture-and-view-parameters)
- `newTextureViewWithPixelFormat` stays a large share of the submit thread although the view cache hits. Ocarina of Time (Switch 2) made about 330 views a frame in Kokiri Forest, about 310 of them for the same textures again. → [Texture views re-made for finalized textures every submission](../reference/rendering.md#texture-views-re-made-for-finalized-textures-every-submission)
- daylight scenes washed out to white (every read of a texture returned one fixed value); `texture type mismatch` red lines, among them `declares slot … as Metal type 6, texture ID … is type 5` (a cube in a cube array slot). → [Compatible views for a mismatched declared texture type](../reference/rendering.md#compatible-views-for-a-mismatched-declared-texture-type)
- with `MTL_SHADER_VALIDATION=1`, `Invalid Metal usage: Texture is not resident` for a texture allocated in `tex_fit`; without validation those reads are undefined. → [Compatible views made from the texture under a view](../reference/rendering.md#compatible-views-made-from-the-texture-under-a-view)
- a type mismatch when a Metal 1D texture is bound to a shader's `texture2d` argument. → [1D textures as one-row 2D textures](../reference/rendering.md#1d-textures-as-one-row-2d-textures)
- with Metal API validation, an abort at a texture upload: `(destinationOrigin.y + sourceSize.height)(64) must be <= height(1)`. Without it, the upload writes past each layer into the next ones, and the texture holds junk rows: a light that flashes across a surface as the camera crosses a distance band. → [A 1D array texture uploaded as its layer count of rows per layer](../reference/rendering.md#a-1d-array-texture-uploaded-as-its-layer-count-of-rows-per-layer)
- shaders that sample a cube texture as `texturecube_array` get a type mismatch. → [Cube textures held as 6-layer 2D arrays](../reference/rendering.md#cube-textures-held-as-6-layer-2d-arrays)
- an abort in `MTLTextureDescriptor` validation, `arrayLength (510) … greater than the max allowed size (341)`, when the prologue movie ends. → [Cube map arrays count layer-faces](../reference/rendering.md#cube-map-arrays-count-layer-faces)
- an abort in Metal's validation: `newSliceRange.length(1) must be a multiple of (6)`. → [Cube views count their layers in cubes](../reference/rendering.md#cube-views-count-their-layers-in-cubes)
- `texture type mismatch … declares slot 10 as Metal type 5, texture … is type 3` (undefined reads); the later `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader declares a cube, texture is 2D` could also report an unused conditional input as an error. → [A 2D texture in a cube slot binds the zero cube](../reference/rendering.md#a-2d-texture-in-a-cube-slot-binds-the-zero-cube)
- `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader … declares slot … as Metal type 7, texture ID … is type 2 … bound as is` (or type 3, a 2D array); the reads are undefined. → [A 2D texture in a 3D slot binds the zero 3D texture](../reference/rendering.md#a-2d-texture-in-a-3d-slot-binds-the-zero-3d-texture)
- `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader … declares slot … as Metal type 2, texture ID … is type 4 (NVN target 5, …); bound as is`, for a shader whose MSL declares the slot `texture2d_ms`. → [Multisample texture slots](../reference/rendering.md#multisample-texture-slots)
- translucent UI (frosted text bands, button guides, dialog headers) yellow and white. → [Integer views for images a shader declares uint or int](../reference/rendering.md#integer-views-for-images-a-shader-declares-uint-or-int)
- a buffer image appears in both integer atomic and float load/store arguments. The float argument's format differs from the shader declaration. → [Float views for integer buffer images](../reference/rendering.md#float-views-for-integer-buffer-images)
- a compute pass's image stores are lost with no red line: the levels or layers it writes stay as they were. With `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`, its `W` lines read `target=3 shader wants target 2` (a 2D array in a slot the shader declares 2D). → [Image views of the type a shader declares](../reference/rendering.md#image-views-of-the-type-a-shader-declares)
- flat-coloured objects with no texture detail in an otherwise correct scene: the UVs and colours a vertex factory fetches from buffers read 0. Then `texture buffers bound as 2D rows … N times, about N made` in the log, a Metal texture for nearly every bind. → [Buffer textures behind handles in uniform buffers, and rows of finalized descriptors](../reference/rendering.md#buffer-textures-behind-handles-in-uniform-buffers-and-rows-of-finalized-descriptors)
- the whole picture pale and washed out, its blacks lifted to grey (a frame's median luminance 0.46 against 0.16 in console footage, its darkest percent 0.18 against 0.01): bloom reads as a haze over everything and the dither of fine detail (Star Fox's fur) as grain. A pass dump shows a compute shader writing an `RGBA8_SRGB` texture (Metal format 71) through an image. → [sRGB textures loaded and stored raw through an image](../reference/rendering.md#srgb-textures-loaded-and-stored-raw-through-an-image)
- `texture type mismatch: shader declares 2, texture is 9`; the slot read 0. → [Buffer textures read by 1D integer fetches (row wrap)](../reference/rendering.md#buffer-textures-read-by-1d-integer-fetches-row-wrap)
- none of its own seen here. Pull request #984 reports GPU hangs from huge light counts on its build of the same game. → [Integer buffer textures read through float slots](../reference/rendering.md#integer-buffer-textures-read-through-float-slots)
- none visible on an M6, where at most 0.23 % of pixels differ between paired runs. GitHub issue #783 reports wrong light offsets on an M2. → [Integer textures read through float slots](../reference/rendering.md#integer-textures-read-through-float-slots)
- Metal API validation aborts in `validateCPUWriteable` before an unsupported buffer-texture copy can report its fallback. → [Private buffer texture fallbacks must not query CPU contents](../reference/rendering.md#private-buffer-texture-fallbacks-must-not-query-cpu-contents)
- synthetic NVN readback sees an earlier producer's bytes after a deferred update or an active virtual remap, or reads early through a wrapped 1D fetch or a BUFFER descriptor table. These are API-level reproductions; no game artifact is attributed to them (issue #2247). → [Sampled BUFFER reads follow source writes and mappings](../reference/rendering.md#sampled-buffer-reads-follow-source-writes-and-mappings)
- particle effects drawn at the wrong scale or place, or flickering, where the particle data is a buffer texture at a pool offset that is not a multiple of 16; log line `buffer texture at pool offset …, below Metal's 16-byte alignment`. → [Buffer textures at offsets below 16 bytes](../reference/rendering.md#buffer-textures-at-offsets-below-16-bytes)
- walls, floors and props vanish and come back from frame to frame in Assassin's Creed II (the Abstergo elevator corridor, the background of Ezio's birth scene); before the fix the log had `[RED] image buffer texture at pool offset …, below Metal's 16-byte alignment: shader writes to it land at the 16-byte boundary below`. Forcing occlusion queries visible (`DM_NVN_VIS_ALL=1`, a probe of PR #1399's author, never on main) does not help. → [Buffer images below the 16-byte alignment](../reference/rendering.md#buffer-images-below-the-16-byte-alignment)
- lighting that alternates between lit and unlit from one frame to the next on an iPhone, the same scene steady on a Mac; log lines `texture buffer (texture ID …, … at pool offset …) has no 2D-row form for a wrapped 1D fetch: the slot reads 0` and `texture type mismatch: … a buffer texture: the slot reads 0 (dummy)`. → [Buffer texture rows at a pool offset the device will not view](../reference/rendering.md#buffer-texture-rows-at-a-pool-offset-the-device-will-not-view)
- torn digits and a background in blue steps (Rhythm Heaven Groove's latency calibration). → [Automatic block height truncates](../reference/rendering.md#automatic-block-height-truncates)
- magenta (the ASTC error colour) on streamed textures in a sparse pool; `DM_NVN_ZBLOCK` finds such textures (all-zero blocks). → [Sparse textures laid out in 64 KB tiles](../reference/rendering.md#sparse-textures-laid-out-in-64-kb-tiles)
- a fixed magenta rectangle over part of a packaged ASTC texture (Bayonetta Origins' tutorial-card background), the same in every frame. → [Packaged textures carry width alignment as well as height and depth](../reference/rendering.md#packaged-textures-carry-width-alignment-as-well-as-height-and-depth)
- a CPU-filled texture shuffled into a patchwork of rectangles (OCTOPATH TRAVELER's world map on the traveler selection). → [Sparse textures take the block height of their 64 KB tile](../reference/rendering.md#sparse-textures-take-the-block-height-of-their-64-kb-tile)
- none visible on an M6 on the Frigate Orpheon's deck. Laid out 1 GOB high, the mapped tiles of a streamed 3D texture fall in scattered places, so lookups read zero in the holes; GitHub issue #783 reports a black gunship hull in the opening on an M2. → [Sparse 3D textures take the standard 3D tile](../reference/rendering.md#sparse-3d-textures-take-the-standard-3d-tile)
- crashes that move between runs (30–70% of boots, in the first minute): in the allocator, on bogus pointers, in unrelated threads. Unreal's purgatory proxy (05-debugging) finds freed 64 KB-aligned blocks modified; with the parked blocks read-only, the faulting store is a `memcpy` of texture mip data from the texture streamer into the buffer `RHILockTexture2D` returned. → [Sparse textures of 8- and 16-byte blocks: blocks at most 8 GOBs high](../reference/rendering.md#sparse-textures-of-8--and-16-byte-blocks-blocks-at-most-8-gobs-high)
- a small CPU-filled sparse texture shows part of its image beside fragments, cut off at the bottom (Yoshi's Crafted World's loading icon: the left half of a chalk drawing and two stray strokes). → [Sparse textures smaller than a tile keep the automatic block height](../reference/rendering.md#sparse-textures-smaller-than-a-tile-keep-the-automatic-block-height)
- streamed textures read 0 (magenta on Monster Hunter Rise's title screen's clothes). → [Sparse textures bound from their first mapped level](../reference/rendering.md#sparse-textures-bound-from-their-first-mapped-level)
- `[RED] texture view id … starts at mip 2 beyond 1 levels` (or mip 1), once per run, for a streamed texture; views from level 1 of such a texture read the level after it. → [Texture views of a sparse texture bound from a later level](../reference/rendering.md#texture-views-of-a-sparse-texture-bound-from-a-later-level)
- textures a mip level sharper or blurrier than on the Switch where the game biases its sampler; in the Vulkan layer, a red line `sampler LOD bias … not implemented`. → [Sampler LOD bias](../reference/rendering.md#sampler-lod-bias)
- whatever a game keeps in a texture that it samples past its edge with clamp-to-border reads the wrong value there: grass flattened along Link's path in Tears of the Kingdom, its bend map filled with "bent" wherever the window scrolled. → [Border colours Metal has no sampler state for](../reference/rendering.md#border-colours-metal-has-no-sampler-state-for)
- material pipelines spill registers even when every sampler uses a native Metal border colour. → [Unused border corrections retain sample registers](../reference/rendering.md#unused-border-corrections-retain-sample-registers)
- the process ends at once (the window vanishes), with `-[MTLTextureDescriptorInternal validateWithDevice:]` failing an assertion such as `requests 9 mipmap levels, but the dimensions (1, 1, 1) can only support 1`, or `sampleCount must be > 1 for multisample textures`. → [Texture fields Metal refuses](../reference/rendering.md#texture-fields-metal-refuses)
- large static and mesh-shader models (Ocarina of Time's castle town wall, rocks, trees, Lon Lon Ranch's barn) draw black for exactly one frame, with the fog in front and the terrain untouched, in pairs four or five frames apart; a few times a minute in play, at fixed places of a route. → [Models black for one frame when a streaming step replaces their textures](../reference/rendering.md#models-black-for-one-frame-when-a-streaming-step-replaces-their-textures)
- a patch of scenery (Ocarina of Time's cliff beside the castle town wall) draws darker for exactly one frame, once, as the game gives it a sharper texture; a few times a minute while walking through Hyrule Field. Single frames, not the pairs of the entry above; no red line. → [A streamed texture's first read drew its surface dark for one frame](../reference/rendering.md#a-streamed-textures-first-read-drew-its-surface-dark-for-one-frame)
- the first frame after a scene cut is dark where newly visible materials are: PRAGMATA's cut from the tower to the tablet draws the cockpit and tablet with their G-buffer base colour at 0.068 on that frame against 0.18 on the next. → [A sparse pool's streamed textures read 0 at their first read](../reference/rendering.md#a-sparse-pools-streamed-textures-read-0-at-their-first-read)
- ≈13 MB/frame leak. → [Transient render targets shared by storage](../reference/rendering.md#transient-render-targets-shared-by-storage)
- the residency line adds and removes thousands of allocations every 300 frames, nearly all from one line, the `res_add` of a buffer texture's view in `tex_mtl_`; the memory line's `finalized textures still referenced` stays at the ghost table's 512 while its puts in total climb by about a hundred or more a frame. No red line and no visible effect: the cost is submit-thread time in residency commits. → [Transient buffer textures shared by storage](../reference/rendering.md#transient-buffer-textures-shared-by-storage)
- a model has correct geometry, base colour and normals but appears as a black silhouette; deferred lighting's material-ID depth texture stays zero. → [IMAGE usage does not split texture storage](../reference/rendering.md#image-usage-does-not-split-texture-storage)
- a surface that Unreal draws from a runtime virtual texture (a landscape floor) stays black; its BC tile caches read all zero in a texture dump, and nothing writes them on the GPU or the CPU; no red line. Or a picture the game compresses on the GPU (a fighter's portrait) shows colourful static where the image should be; no red line. → [Writes into an uncompressed alias of a compressed texture](../reference/rendering.md#writes-into-an-uncompressed-alias-of-a-compressed-texture)
- GPU memory grows with every image shown; the memory line's `shared textures` count keeps rising. → [Shared-storage entries taken over by a new texture](../reference/rendering.md#shared-storage-entries-taken-over-by-a-new-texture)
- while the game makes many textures (Ocarina of Time's cutscenes), frames of hundreds of milliseconds up to a second, all of it in `submit+present` (the `[longframe]` lines); `sample` of the process names `alias_take_over` the submit thread's hottest function. → [A storage takeover walked the serial table for each entry](../reference/rendering.md#a-storage-takeover-walked-the-serial-table-for-each-entry)
- a scene that makes tens of thousands of textures never finishes loading: no frame is presented, the submit thread stays at 100 % of a core, and `sample` names `alias_take_over` (under `tex_mtl_` ← `tex_by_id` ← `encode_args`). → [A storage takeover walked the sharing table for each texture made](../reference/rendering.md#a-storage-takeover-walked-the-sharing-table-for-each-texture-made)
- a command buffer fails with a GPU page fault on the same frames in every run, in a compute or draw command buffer whose shaders sample through the texture pool. Ocarina of Time with `DM_NVN_RT_RECLAIM=1` (PR #1588's render-target reclaim; the switch was never on main): f3,677 and f4,851, compute shader 9e6b00b050e30801, each run. → [A texture ID with no texture kept its cached view](../reference/rendering.md#a-texture-id-with-no-texture-kept-its-cached-view)
- GPU memory grows as resolution changes or rooms load, although almost every retained texture still has a live NVN descriptor; reclaiming unused sharing-table entries cannot recover it. → [Live resolution variants share physical image storage](../reference/rendering.md#live-resolution-variants-share-physical-image-storage)
- after the game renders at another resolution for a while (a pause menu, a resolution switch) and comes back, a render target it loads instead of clearing holds non-finite or random pixels, in a screen-shaped region that opaque draws wipe away and transparent ones keep; bloom spreads them into a strobing glow. A non-finite scan (`DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`) counts about a million of them in the first pass back. → [A resolution family member reads another size's compressed blocks as garbage](../reference/rendering.md#a-resolution-family-member-reads-another-sizes-compressed-blocks-as-garbage)
- an engine's effects pass draws (hundreds of draws, instances that follow the weapon) and nothing appears: particles, beams, decals, muzzle flashes, the laser sight and the spiderweb sprites are all missing, while the scene, lighting, HUD and bloom are right. The pass's colour target is never bound, sampled or copied by any later pass. Metro 2033 Redux: `DM_CTL texlist` showed ser 2630 (640x360, `RGBA8_SRGB`, 4 samples) and ser 2611 (1280x720, `RGBA8_SRGB`, 1 sample) over one pool storage, `0x3c0000` bytes each, with different `mtl=` pointers. → [A 4-sample target over a single-sample image's pool storage](../reference/rendering.md#a-4-sample-target-over-a-single-sample-images-pool-storage)
- while a game's dynamic resolution moves, the residency set's `added` count in the `residency set up to frame` lines stays at hundreds of MB per 300 frames from the shared-texture site, and frames stutter. On a 16 GB Mac, wired memory and swap follow, with p95 frame times near 100 ms. Ocarina of Time after 04419bc2, which freed its dynamic resolution from its floor (GitHub issue #1831). → [Render targets that move with the dynamic resolution get new Metal memory each time](../reference/rendering.md#render-targets-that-move-with-the-dynamic-resolution-get-new-metal-memory-each-time)
- on an iPhone the game is ended for memory (a jetsam event: iOS ends an app once the pages it touched reach its memory limit) at an area load, with the guest memory already in files. On the Mac, `footprint` shows most of the process as `Owned physical footprint (unmapped) (graphics)`: Ocarina of Time reached 6.36 GB of footprint stepping into the village, 4.4 GB of it Metal memory, the phone's limit being about 6.1 GB. → [Render targets drawn over stayed while their texture objects lived](../reference/rendering.md#render-targets-drawn-over-stayed-while-their-texture-objects-lived)
- the whole picture came out darker and more saturated on the Mac and the iPhone: comparing with a Switch screenshot, every channel at every level equalled the Switch value decoded from sRGB once more (0.55 → 0.26, 0.75 → 0.52). → [Render-target views that change the format](../reference/rendering.md#render-target-views-that-change-the-format)
- whenever a Switch 2 game's NDSR upscaler runs, the upscaled 3D scene is solid red with black specks, and the UI drawn over it has its red channel gone (white text cyan, skin teal): menus whose scene is not upscaled look right. The log has `[RED] render target view format 0xb cannot be attached (base texture Metal format 93 was created without the format-view usage): drawing in the base format` once. → [A render-target view of another class: the texture is upgraded, not drawn in its base format](../reference/rendering.md#a-render-target-view-of-another-class-the-texture-is-upgraded-not-drawn-in-its-base-format)
- a movie (or another picture the game converts each frame) does not show: the screen keeps the last 3D frame while the decoder delivers every picture. A pass dump shows a pass writing one serial and the composite sampling another serial of the same size, the two Metal formats `RGBA8Unorm` (70) and `RGBA8Unorm_sRGB` (71). → [Textures of an sRGB / linear pair over the same storage](../reference/rendering.md#textures-of-an-srgb--linear-pair-over-the-same-storage)
- the 3D picture is black under a HUD that draws: a game's upscaler writes its output through an R32 integer image and samples it in a packed format. A pass dump shows a compute dispatch writing an `R32Uint` (53) image and the next one sampling another serial of the same size, and the texture's storage info (`DM_NVN_TEXINFO`) the same pool and offset for both. → [Textures of two 32-bit formats over the same storage](../reference/rendering.md#textures-of-two-32-bit-formats-over-the-same-storage)
- a clear of, or a format-converting copy into, a texture made without render-target use is declined. → [Render-target use added on demand](../reference/rendering.md#render-target-use-added-on-demand)
- `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x794` in `-[AGXG16GFamilyRenderContext_mtlnext drawIndexedPrimitives:…]+0x50`, called from `draw_`, right after the log line `texture upgraded to allow format views` (Metal API validation reports nothing first). → [A texture upgrade while a draw binds its arguments ends the pass](../reference/rendering.md#a-texture-upgrade-while-a-draw-binds-its-arguments-ends-the-pass)
- `render target is a compressed-format texture …`; the pass's draws did nothing (the attachment was left out), or the pipeline-descriptor validation aborts the process. → [Compressed textures as render targets through an uncompressed shadow](../reference/rendering.md#compressed-textures-as-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow)
- the boot aborts in Metal's pipeline validation: `MTLPixelFormatDepth16Unorm is not color renderable`. → [A depth texture drawn through a colour view](../reference/rendering.md#a-depth-texture-drawn-through-a-colour-view)
- a G-buffer target holds only scattered squares (a different order for each 32×32 tile). → [The same texture attached at two colour slots](../reference/rendering.md#the-same-texture-attached-at-two-colour-slots)
- `no render target could get an MTLTexture` (the pass skipped). → [Passes with no render target](../reference/rendering.md#passes-with-no-render-target)
- a frame-time spike every few frames (the Metal HUD's frame-time line jumps by several ms at a regular interval) while the scene stands still; `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` over consecutive frames shows the slow frame with about 130 more render passes, each one draw into a 64x64 to 8x8 face of a cube or array level, and the next pass starting late. → [Passes into other layers of one texture level run as one layered pass](../reference/rendering.md#passes-into-other-layers-of-one-texture-level-run-as-one-layered-pass)
- in `DM_NVN_GPUPROF`, render passes with six colour attachments on the faces of one cube level take far longer than their size explains: a 2x2 level as long as a 16x16 one (65–120 µs each), a 64x64 level 200–300 µs. → [A draw into the six faces of a cube level at once ran short of threads](../reference/rendering.md#a-draw-into-the-six-faces-of-a-cube-level-at-once-ran-short-of-threads)
- in `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`, face-split passes (`L … face-split pass`) of one size a few passes apart, each into another texture or another cube of an array and each with its one layered attachment; in `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` each small one costs about as much GPU time as a larger pass. → [Face-split passes of one level size, one per texture](../reference/rendering.md#face-split-passes-of-one-level-size-one-per-texture)
- a GPU-bound frame with many passes on the same targets, one after the other, each preceded by a compute encoder of one dispatch (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`: `compute / copy after pass … dispatches 1` between render passes of the same size); about 4 ms of GPU idle a frame inside command buffers in Metal System Trace. → [Compute dispatches between the draws of a pass run ahead of it](../reference/rendering.md#compute-dispatches-between-the-draws-of-a-pass-run-ahead-of-it)
- a small render pass with many draws takes far longer than its pixel count suggests: in Ocarina of Time, a 128x128 pass of 212-245 draws took 1.4-1.6 ms of a frame on an M2 Pro, while the 1366x768 main pass of 846 draws took 5.3 ms. A Metal System Trace with GPU counters reads about 2% ALU and 1.7% fragment occupancy over its fragment interval, against about 30% ALU and 12% over the main pass's. → [Small passes shaded on a few cores](../reference/rendering.md#small-passes-shaded-on-a-few-cores)
- a scene drawn only in a small square in the top-left corner of a black screen; speckled and blocky patches; a full-screen overlay showing one tile; the texture dump right after the scene pass shows the picture only in the corner, and `DM_NVN_HILITE` on the scene's shaders lights only the corner. → [Clears of attachments larger than the render pass](../reference/rendering.md#clears-of-attachments-larger-than-the-render-pass)
- a point light (a lantern, a lighter) lights only one side of the room with straight, sharp edges, and everything else around it is dark as if in shadow; a dump of the shadow cube (DM_CTL `texraw`, every face) shows face 0 drawn and faces 1-5 all zero; more generally, layers above the first of an array, cube or 3D target keep their old or zero content after a clear. → [Clears of layered render targets in a pass that is not layered](../reference/rendering.md#clears-of-layered-render-targets-in-a-pass-that-is-not-layered)
- a scene drawn into a scissored part of a target is wiped to the clear colour, while what a later pass draws on top (UI, menus) shows. No red lines. → [A waiting lazy clear landed after a scissored clear and its draws](../reference/rendering.md#a-waiting-lazy-clear-landed-after-a-scissored-clear-and-its-draws)
- `texture clear case not implemented … mask 0x1`. → [A channel mask that covers every channel is a whole clear](../reference/rendering.md#a-channel-mask-that-covers-every-channel-is-a-whole-clear)
- `texture clear case not implemented … mask 0x8` while entering a rendered scene. → [Texture clears with partial channel masks or regions](../reference/rendering.md#texture-clears-with-partial-channel-masks-or-regions)
- red lines for unimplemented mid-pass, partial-mask and partial-scissor clears (the clears were dropped); `depth-stencil clear with a scissor or partial stencil mask, cleared whole` (logged once). → [Clears inside a pass, with a partial mask or a partial scissor](../reference/rendering.md#clears-inside-a-pass-with-a-partial-mask-or-a-partial-scissor)
- a pass loads a whole depth/stencil target every frame only to clear it: Donkey Kong Bananza clears a 2880x1620 Depth32F-S8 target with stencil mask `0x1`, before any pass is open, and loads 22.8 MB a frame for it. → [A depth clear over the whole target with a partial stencil mask](../reference/rendering.md#a-depth-clear-over-the-whole-target-with-a-partial-stencil-mask)
- a tile grid in Ultrahand and Recall highlights; an `EQUAL` depth test rejects fragments writing the same depth as a cleared Depth16 target. → [Depth16 load-clears match fragment writes](../reference/rendering.md#depth16-load-clears-match-fragment-writes)
- solid magenta blocks, exactly (255, 0, 255), hard-edged and aligned to the 16-pixel grid (16×16, sometimes two stacked): Super Mario 3D World and Super Mario Odyssey showed them only on the iPhone; Captain Toad and Origami King on a Mac, Apple M6, Captain Toad in about half the frames of the opening course, moving with the falling leaves. On an RG11B10F target (no alpha) the blocks are pure black or neon green, (0, 252, 0), and flicker, sometimes only part of a tile: Super Mario Bros. Wonder on an Apple M6 and an M1, most visible on the speech bubbles of dialogue; or non-finite pixels that bloom spreads into green blobs (NieR:Automata on the Mac). A rendered cutscene alternates brightness every game frame, with blocky bands of light and shadow switching on and off: each lighting-zone bit flips every frame (Xenoblade Chronicles 3, a read-only image load). → [Same-pass feedback read through a snapshot](../reference/rendering.md#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot)
- a scene full of deferred decals runs far below its target where the same scene with `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=0` holds it, and the log's `same-pass feedback sampling: snapshot #…` count climbs by hundreds a frame. Tears of the Kingdom's first shrine on an iPad Pro 11 (M5): GPU p50 58.8 ms, about 15 fps, about 284 snapshots a frame (seedds/nxnative#737); Breath of the Wild on the Great Plateau, camera toward the Temple of Time, on an iPad Pro 13 (M4): 60 → about 42 fps, about 45 one-draw passes more a frame on the 1920×1080 G-buffer. Splatoon Raiders in inked stages on a MacBook Air (M2): GPU p50 22–26 ms against 11–14 ms in the hub, a one-draw pass and a whole-map snapshot of its 6400×6400 ink map (80 MB) for each ink stamp (seedds/nxnative#1063). The `[nvn] same-pass feedback up to frame …` line (every 300 frames) gives the snapshots, reuses and tile reads per frame. → [Same-pass feedback read from the tile](../reference/rendering.md#same-pass-feedback-read-from-the-tile)
- the `[nvn] residency set up to frame …` line (every 300 frames) shows hundreds to thousands of additions from `fb_snap`'s line in `nvn_mtl.m`, gigabytes of them, and as many removals, with the residency commits' time and the submit thread's kernel time up. Tears of the Kingdom on an iPhone 17 (1.4.2, seedds/nxnative#931): 1,500-1,600 additions of 2.4 MB textures every 300 frames, residency commits 250-600 ms every 300 frames against about 25 in an older build, kernel time on the submit thread 1-2 ms a frame against 0.2. → [Snapshot textures taken by format and size](../reference/rendering.md#snapshot-textures-taken-by-format-and-size)
- a scene with many bindless decals runs well below its target with the GPU busy, and a pass dump (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`) shows long runs of one-draw passes on the same targets, each loading and storing every attachment. Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave's battle maps: 31 consecutive one-draw passes on four 1024×576 targets a frame, 126.5 passes and about 1.4 GB of attachment loads and stores a frame. → [Bindless shaders taken as depth readers](../reference/rendering.md#bindless-shaders-taken-as-depth-readers)
- a game that reads textures through a pool runs slower than its draws explain, with passes of one draw each on the same targets. A pass dump (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`) shows `SetRenderTargets` going on in the open pass and the pass ending anyway. Ocarina of Time in Kokiri Forest: two frames of four had 219 passes, 127 of them on the 128x128 reflection probe's cube faces, and frame times of 15, 15, 18, 18.5 ms in a cycle. → [Pool shaders taken as depth readers](../reference/rendering.md#pool-shaders-taken-as-depth-readers)
- blocks over the scene where an effect compares against the scene's depth, changing from frame to frame. Short screen-aligned dashes or blocks over rock and wall surfaces, light blue (the sky behind) or black, that stay on the same surfaces as the camera moves; how large and how many depends on the tree and the render size. Per-pass snapshots (`DM_NVN_TEXDUMP` with `DM_NVN_TEXSNAP`) show the surface drawn whole, then a later full-screen pass painting the blocks over it. Or light square blocks, 8×8 pixels, in the sky over distant hills and trees, where a soft-edged effect fades against the scene's depth. → [Depth sampled in the pass that writes it](../reference/rendering.md#depth-sampled-in-the-pass-that-writes-it)
- NaN texels in the upper levels of a texture the game fills level by level (each level drawn from the one above), and from there NaN wherever the texture is read: black speckles in whole 2×2 quads, white blotches once bloom spreads them, and a dark frame from the eye adaptation. → [Mip-chain pass sampling the level it renders](../reference/rendering.md#mip-chain-pass-sampling-the-level-it-renders)
- bloom wide and hard around bright edges: neon letters swell into fat, filled shapes. With `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=0` they are thin again, but the white blotches of the entry above come back. → [Upsample pass reading the level above the one it renders](../reference/rendering.md#upsample-pass-reading-the-level-above-the-one-it-renders)
- text a game renders into textures for the title and the menus comes out upside down, while the scene looks right. → [Lower-left window origin](../reference/rendering.md#lower-left-window-origin)
- geometry appears inside out and almost black; a fullscreen indirect-lighting triangle is entirely culled even though its vertex data and polygon state match the game. → [Queue initialization captures the raster origin](../reference/rendering.md#queue-initialization-captures-the-raster-origin)
- a movie pass clears its target but its back-face-culled YUV-to-RGB quad disappears when a second NVN device records commands for the game's queue. → [Viewport origin belongs to the recording device](../reference/rendering.md#viewport-origin-belongs-to-the-recording-device)
- a pink window with every command buffer ignored while headless runs look right: the first presents fail on the display command buffer with `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`, and after a few of those macOS ignores every submission of the process ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)"). → [The residency set committed before a present](../reference/rendering.md#the-residency-set-committed-before-a-present)
- `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault` or `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang` on a command buffer, and nothing says which draw or dispatch caused it; `DM_RES_CHECK=1` reports no `missing residency`. → [Naming the shaders of a GPU fault](../reference/rendering.md#naming-the-shaders-of-a-gpu-fault)
- `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault` on a command buffer, and the report macOS writes for it (`gpuEvent-<process>-*.ips` in `/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports`, its `bif0_fault`) is a read at address 0 or a few bytes past it (0x40). `DM_RES_CHECK=1` reports no `missing residency` and no `released too early`. → [A GPU fault at address 0](../reference/rendering.md#a-gpu-fault-at-address-0)
- with present snapshots on (`DM_PRESENT_SNAP`, the launcher's **Frame buffering**), a frozen, corrupted picture from the first presents; the first error is already `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorSubmissionsIgnored` ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)") on the display command buffer, then on every upload, segment and submission command buffer, and `[RED] present snapshots: the previous copy into a ring slot did not finish within 2 s`. With snapshots off the same game runs. → [A present snapshot's copy needs the same residency commit](../reference/rendering.md#a-present-snapshots-copy-needs-the-same-residency-commit)
- after a GPU hang or GPU page faults, every upload, segment, submission and display command buffer comes back `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorSubmissionsIgnored` ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)"). Nothing is drawn again while the game's sound and logic go on, until the game aborts itself (`game called nn::diag abort`, about 70 s later); in full screen, WindowServer froze for 40 s and its watchdog killed it. With the fix the log has `[RED] GPU access revoked: the … command buffer was ignored`, and a Mac app shows an alert and quits. → [A GPU whose access macOS revoked stops the game](../reference/rendering.md#a-gpu-whose-access-macos-revoked-stops-the-game)
- an abort at the first present: `[RED] upload batches belong to the submit thread only: up_begin called on thread 'nvn-present-link'`, with `pkg_upload`, `tex_mtl` and `present_now` in the backtrace. → [A CPU-written window texture resolved where the present is queued](../reference/rendering.md#a-cpu-written-window-texture-resolved-where-the-present-is-queued)
- a game that renders below the screen's resolution looks soft on a phone, in a large Mac window or on a 4K screen. → [The present upscales through MetalFX](../reference/rendering.md#the-present-upscales-through-metalfx)
- a scene turns narrow, with black bars at its sides, while the swapchain or window textures keep their size; circles come out as tall ovals. With MetalFX the log has `present upscaling: ... 1200x960 → 1800x1440` in a 2560x1440 window. → [A crop only picks texels; the picture has the layer's shape](../reference/rendering.md#a-crop-only-picks-texels-the-picture-has-the-layers-shape)
- a game whose output is larger than the rectangle it is shown in (a 2560x1440 docked output on a 1080p screen, or in a window smaller than it) has stair-stepped, uneven edges that crawl as they move, more than its own frame shows at full size. → [A picture shown smaller than it is drawn: stair-stepped edges from the present's single tap](../reference/rendering.md#a-picture-shown-smaller-than-it-is-drawn-stair-stepped-edges-from-the-presents-single-tap)
- one-draw passes on one target, hundreds of times a frame, with the same attachments each time (a `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` frame: `load` and `store` of the whole target in every line); a low frame rate that follows the target's size. → [Setting the same targets again continues the pass](../reference/rendering.md#setting-the-same-targets-again-continues-the-pass)
- hundreds of one-draw passes loading and storing the same attachments; `same-target continuation: first case` never appears even though each draw uses the same targets. → [Empty target bindings between draws broke continuation](../reference/rendering.md#empty-target-bindings-between-draws-broke-continuation)
- a pass on a very large target costs about the same however little it draws; `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` shows time proportional to the target's area. → [A render area for passes on very large targets](../reference/rendering.md#a-render-area-for-passes-on-very-large-targets)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferShadingRateImagePalette`, or `nvnCommandBufferBindShadingRateImage` or `nvnCommandBufferSetShadingRatePaletteMode`, every frame. → [Variable rate shading ignored](../reference/rendering.md#variable-rate-shading-ignored)
- `regress.py` flagged Bastion as a REGRESSION on a screenshot (RGB difference about 30) whose game picture was the same on both sides: a strip beside the picture was black in some runs and purple (68, 34, 136) in others, on the baseline as well as the candidate. → [Screenshots of a cropped window held what lay outside the crop](../reference/rendering.md#screenshots-of-a-cropped-window-held-what-lay-outside-the-crop)
- angle-dependent character smearing, or vertex-only pipeline failures with `invalid depth/stencil pipeline formats 16843012/260`, although the pass began with valid `260/260` formats. → [Ten attachment records for eight colors and depth-stencil](../reference/rendering.md#ten-attachment-records-for-eight-colors-and-depth-stencil)
- Metal aborts at pipeline creation: `depthAttachmentPixelFormat (MTLPixelFormatInvalid) and stencilAttachmentPixelFormat (MTLPixelFormatDepth32Float_Stencil8) must match`. → [Combined depth-stencil formats stay paired in passes and pipeline keys](../reference/rendering.md#combined-depth-stencil-formats-stay-paired-in-passes-and-pipeline-keys)
- undefined values blended into attachments the fragment shader leaves alone. → [No writes to colour outputs the shader does not write](../reference/rendering.md#no-writes-to-colour-outputs-the-shader-does-not-write)
- undefined values in the components of a target the fragment shader does not write: a NaN or garbage alpha (or G, B) that later passes, such as bloom, spread. → [No writes to colour components outside the shader's output map](../reference/rendering.md#no-writes-to-colour-components-outside-the-shaders-output-map)
- foliage, hair and fences drawn as solid quads (the parts the texture leaves transparent filled with its colour or black), dissolve effects solid instead of breaking up; once per run `SetAlphaRef …: the host has no alpha test`. → [The alpha test through a library of its own](../reference/rendering.md#the-alpha-test-through-a-library-of-its-own)
- `[RED] shader ... draws with alpha function 5 but has no alpha test entry point (its colour 0 has no float alpha): drawn without the test` on depth-pass programs. An RGB-only colour output can also lose fragments despite exporting no alpha. → [Alpha-test comparisons without an alpha export](../reference/rendering.md#alpha-test-comparisons-without-an-alpha-export)
- data a game builds in a target by combining draws comes out wrong; in Xenoblade Chronicles 2 indoor lighting has hard-edged dark boxes (world-space boxes in perspective, darker with the time of day) and lighter translucent rectangles elsewhere; in Skyward Sword HD a brown cast covers the distance and near objects turn into dark silhouettes, coming and going with the camera. A pass dump shows `lo=7` (or any value other than 3) on the `D` lines of the draws that write the target. → [Logic operations through a library of its own](../reference/rendering.md#logic-operations-through-a-library-of-its-own)
- the title background and gameplay render black while the HUD and text remain visible. A pass dump shows the scene and its color lookup table rendered before tone mapping, but the bloom input holds positive infinity. → [Source-only blending on floating-point targets](../reference/rendering.md#source-only-blending-on-floating-point-targets)
- `nvnBlendStateSetAdvancedMode 8` (SRC_OVER) logged and blended with the state's own functions. → [Advanced blend modes as Porter-Duff factors](../reference/rendering.md#advanced-blend-modes-as-porter-duff-factors)
- surfaces out of direct light nearly black, as if there were no ambient, sky or bounce light at all: shadows on grass pitch black where console footage shows them a soft, lit green, cliffs and the character black in shadow. More generally, data a game packs into an 8-bit (or 16-bit, RGB10A2) normalized target as a half step, such as material flags written as (n + 0.5) / 255, reads back one higher than on the console. No red line. → [Float colours rounded into normalized targets as NVIDIA's colour unit does](../reference/rendering.md#float-colours-rounded-into-normalized-targets-as-nvidias-colour-unit-does)
- blocks of another sky layer in the sky, in stair-stepped squares and half squares that follow the dome's mesh. In Mario Kart 8 Deluxe's Sky-High Sundae, salmon-red blocks of the sunset panorama show through the blue sky, the same blocks every time from the same camera. → [Depth on the far plane](../reference/rendering.md#depth-on-the-far-plane)
- a two-sided stencil draw whose back faces test against their own reference value passes nowhere (or everywhere) on the back faces; nothing in the log. In Mario Golf: Super Rush the ground decals are missing: the driving range's scoring target in Golf Adventure (a ring with a blue centre on the fairway) is not drawn, in the normal view or the top view. → [Front and back stencil reference values](../reference/rendering.md#front-and-back-stencil-reference-values)
- striped lighting: black and white bands that follow depth on every lit surface, as if every pixel were in or out of shadow at random. → [Stencil-mode texture views read through `X32_Stencil8`](../reference/rendering.md#stencil-mode-texture-views-read-through-x32_stencil8)
- a character drawn as a dithered silhouette inside a dithered square (DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined's hero). → [Stencil-mode textures from the texture builder](../reference/rendering.md#stencil-mode-textures-from-the-texture-builder)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferSetProvokingVertexMode`, once. → [The provoking vertex is the first](../reference/rendering.md#the-provoking-vertex-is-the-first)
- `[nvn] SetConservativeRasterEnable on: ignored by host for now`, followed by `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferSetConservativeRasterDilate`. → [Conservative rasterization remains unimplemented](../reference/rendering.md#conservative-rasterization-remains-unimplemented)
- characters (skinned models) invisible or stretched into streaks while the level renders; `vertex attribute format … does not match shader input type …` for a remaining mismatch. → [Integer vertex inputs](../reference/rendering.md#integer-vertex-inputs)
- red lines 89 and 96 (`vconv_bind`, the CPU conversion every draw); about a quarter of Age of Calamity's replay thread in battle. → [Shadow vertex inputs](../reference/rendering.md#shadow-vertex-inputs)
- a mesh that should be drawn is missing, without a missing-shader line: in nxnative, the Slosher's airborne ink in Splatoon Raiders, whose alpha-tested depth pass reads attribute 12's W while the draw binds attributes 0-9, so its color pass (depth `EQUAL`) drew nothing. → [Absent vertex attributes lose W](../reference/rendering.md#absent-vertex-attributes-lose-w)
- a fullscreen post-process draw writes no color, then a later blur composite darkens scenery while the character remains visible. → [Float32 vertex padding through integer inputs](../reference/rendering.md#float32-vertex-padding-through-integer-inputs)
- `Vertex attribute m_36(5) of type uint4 cannot be read using MTLAttributeFormatHalf2` (draws dropped: black silhouettes); UVs collapse onto one texel (flat tan characters); vertices far out of view (negative 16-bit values widened with zeros). → [CPU conversion of vertex attributes the input type cannot take](../reference/rendering.md#cpu-conversion-of-vertex-attributes-the-input-type-cannot-take)
- `[RED] BindVertexAttribState n=17`, tens of thousands of times, and a scene that draws wrong or not at all: the bind was refused, so the draws kept the previous layout. → [More than 16 vertex attributes](../reference/rendering.md#more-than-16-vertex-attributes)
- `vertex attribute format … not mapped` (printed once). → [Vertex formats Metal lacks](../reference/rendering.md#vertex-formats-metal-lacks)
- `[RED] vertex attribute conversion: a range of 915138 vertices exceeds the per-frame staging (format 0x14)` and geometry stretched into streaks from the picture's centre (a stadium's stands and roof), while the pitch, the HUD and the characters draw right. → [Long vertex ranges in the CPU conversion](../reference/rendering.md#long-vertex-ranges-in-the-cpu-conversion)
- the `vertex conversion:` line reports millions of vertices per submission and the submitting thread's translation takes most of a frame: 7.65 million vertices, 122 MB written every frame, 26.8 ms on an M4 (about 37 fps) in the stadium above, as PR #1727 measured. → [Kept vertex conversions](../reference/rendering.md#kept-vertex-conversions)
- the `vertex conversion:` line reports millions of vertices per submission (`0 kept` when the game has sparse mappings), the `translation` line's submit CPU takes most of a frame while the GPU idles, and red line 99 (`… feeds a float input`) or the line for an integer-read-as-float format appears. EA SPORTS FC 27 at the Estadio da Luz: 10.7–12.0 million vertices per submission, submit CPU 17–19 ms a frame, 50–58 fps. → [Attributes the vertex fetch cannot read, pulled](../reference/rendering.md#attributes-the-vertex-fetch-cannot-read-pulled)
- `[RED] BindVertexBuffers first=16 n=1` … `first=31`, thousands of times a run and no visible fault. → [Null vertex buffers bound past stream 15](../reference/rendering.md#null-vertex-buffers-bound-past-stream-15)
- the assertion `buffer index (31) must be < 31`. → [Vertex streams 14 and 15 at Metal slots 14 and 15](../reference/rendering.md#vertex-streams-14-and-15-at-metal-slots-14-and-15)
- Scaleform fills come out as solid white or light grey sheets over the whole screen, hard-edged triangles and bands, and the menu text is missing (a language list or a title screen that is all white behind its button prompt). → [Vertex streams at addresses that are not 4-byte aligned](../reference/rendering.md#vertex-streams-at-addresses-that-are-not-4-byte-aligned)
- hard-edged translucent slabs and triangles over the scene that move with the camera, where a full-screen pass blends an effect in (Super Mario Odyssey: white wedges through the Cascade Kingdom's waterfall mist, one edge through the centre of the screen). → [Vertex fetches past a stream's bound size](../reference/rendering.md#vertex-fetches-past-a-streams-bound-size)
- `[RED] vertex stream N's buffer 0x… is in no memory pool; draw skipped` (`; its attributes read zeros` for a pulling pipeline). Before the line existed: a draw whose vertices came from an earlier draw's buffer, with nothing in the log. → [A vertex stream in no memory pool](../reference/rendering.md#a-vertex-stream-in-no-memory-pool)
- scenes and characters black, only foliage, light shafts and the HUD drawn; pass dumps full of `Z … draw skipped: no program bound`. → [Programs bound per stage](../reference/rendering.md#programs-bound-per-stage)
- a draw disabled by `SetRenderEnable(false)` executes after an event or fence, or a conditional clear erases a target whose preceding timestamp report should make it skip. → [Conditional rendering survives queue continuations](../reference/rendering.md#conditional-rendering-survives-queue-continuations)
- `[RED] failed to create render pipeline (vertex 790573a52b9c5053_b fragment f39fb42850f50ecd_b): Fragment input(s) user(locn0),user(locn1) … not written by vertex shader`, once on entering the first investigation; `texture type mismatch: shader declares a cube, texture is 2D` (a pixel shader run with another pair's bindings). → [Composite program taken again after a replay state restore](../reference/rendering.md#composite-program-taken-again-after-a-replay-state-restore)
- `no program bound before the draw`, `program has no vertex shader` (a draw after a dispatch is dropped); `the dispatched program has no compute shader` (a dispatch after a graphics bind). → [Compute programs bound apart from graphics programs](../reference/rendering.md#compute-programs-bound-apart-from-graphics-programs)
- a red line: the 8-bit index buffer had GPU-side writes in the submission (indices read stale). → [8-bit indices widened on the GPU after a write in the same submission](../reference/rendering.md#8-bit-indices-widened-on-the-gpu-after-a-write-in-the-same-submission)
- indirect draws whose record the GPU writes earlier in the frame cannot be read on the CPU at replay; GPU-driven effects drawn as indirect quads, which Metal has no primitive for. → [Indirect draws and dispatches read by the GPU](../reference/rendering.md#indirect-draws-and-dispatches-read-by-the-gpu)
- an empty G-buffer pass (the title screen showed only the UI); later, the submitting thread waiting for the GPU before each such draw (36–54 fps in Kamura Village on an iPhone). MIO also reported missing level geometry with maxima of 4,594 and 8,192 draws. → [Indirect-count multi-draws](../reference/rendering.md#indirect-count-multi-draws)
- in a pass dump (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`), `M … indirect-count multi-draw masking: render pass split here` inside scene passes, and the periodic `indirect-count multi-draw masking:` line counts `render pass split` times in the tens of thousands. Each split loads the pass's colour and depth again (Mario Kart World: 2048x1152, 20 MB). → [Indirect-count multi-draws split the pass an earlier draw opened](../reference/rendering.md#indirect-count-multi-draws-split-the-pass-an-earlier-draw-opened)
- a hitch of a second or more as a level opens: a few frames of 330–540 ms whose submission takes 2–3 times its CPU time, and the wait ledger (`DM_NVN_WAITS=1`) shows thousands of indirect-count multi-draw waits for those frames only. → [CPU reads after a drain wait only for later writes](../reference/rendering.md#cpu-reads-after-a-drain-wait-only-for-later-writes)
- a black screen while the frame loop runs at full rate, with `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferDrawTexture`; once drawn, the picture alternates frame by frame between the right image and another texture's (a solid colour, a still image). → [`nvnCommandBufferDrawTexture`](../reference/rendering.md#nvncommandbufferdrawtexture)
- the HUD and sky render but the battle's characters and terrain are absent; the replay logs `draw skipped: rasterizer discard` between model draws. → [Rasterizer discard preserves vertex storage writes](../reference/rendering.md#rasterizer-discard-preserves-vertex-storage-writes)
- exploding meshes and displaced model parts after vertex-only work; post-vertex captures (nxnative's vertex dump, since removed) can contain valid positions for some meshes while other palettes are incorrect. → [Primitive barriers order vertex storage writes](../reference/rendering.md#primitive-barriers-order-vertex-storage-writes)
- a character's mesh stretched for one frame into a long wedge or sheet that reaches another character, and other characters missing in that frame; intermittent (about one frame in a hundred near a group of characters). A pass dump shows `barrier bits 0x1 (render pass open)` before vertex-only draws (`fs=-`, `discard=1`) that bind a storage buffer `rw` which the draws before them read `ro`. → [Ordering barriers before a draw that writes](../reference/rendering.md#ordering-barriers-before-a-draw-that-writes)
- the whole picture upside down (Absolum's title logo and "Press Any Button" mirrored top to bottom); a red-reporting stub for `nvnCommandBufferSetViewportSwizzles`. → [Viewport swizzles applied as a flipped viewport](../reference/rendering.md#viewport-swizzles-applied-as-a-flipped-viewport)
- a `sample` of the submit thread shows `ubo_read` calling `-[MTLBuffer contents]` with `objc_retainAutorelease` at every handle read, and `objc_autoreleasePoolPop` at commit draining those buffers. Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave: about 4 % of the submit thread in the reads and as much again in the drain. → [Bindless handles read from guest memory directly](../reference/rendering.md#bindless-handles-read-from-guest-memory-directly)
- `[RED] texture→texture copy case not implemented: 1920x1080x1→1920x1080x1 flags 0 format 260→252 3D 0→0`. → [Depth plane copies between depth formats](../reference/rendering.md#depth-plane-copies-between-depth-formats)
- `FATAL deferred encoding: DmRecEnc has no stand-in for -copyFromTexture:sourceSlice:sourceLevel:sourceOrigin:sourceSize:toBuffer:destinationOffset:destinationBytesPerRow:destinationBytesPerImage:options:` with Metal encoding on its own thread (`DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD=1`), at the first depth plane copy. → [Depth plane copies on the encode thread](../reference/rendering.md#depth-plane-copies-on-the-encode-thread)
- `FATAL deferred encoding: DmRecEnc has no stand-in for -drawMeshThreadgroupsWithIndirectBuffer:threadsPerObjectThreadgroup:threadsPerMeshThreadgroup:`, then `CRASH sig=6` under `mtk_encode`, with Metal encoding on its own thread (`DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD=1`), at the first indirect task / mesh draw. → [Indirect task / mesh draws on the encode thread](../reference/rendering.md#indirect-task--mesh-draws-on-the-encode-thread)
- with Metal encoding on its own thread (`DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD=1`), a frame's GPU span is far above its GPU work, and the gaps sit before the first encoder of a submission: `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` with its CPU timeline shows the GPU done with everything committed while the next submission is still being encoded. The wait ledger (`DM_NVN_WAITS=1`) shows `encode thread drain` blocked about once per submission. → [The GPU idles before each submission while the replay waits for the encode thread](../reference/rendering.md#the-gpu-idles-before-each-submission-while-the-replay-waits-for-the-encode-thread)
- a GPU page fault (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`) 30 to 40 s after a save loads, then the game hangs or stops. `DM_NVN_SKIP_MESH=1` makes it go away. → [Mesh draws with no task shader above 1024 workgroups](../reference/rendering.md#mesh-draws-with-no-task-shader-above-1024-workgroups)
- a scene opens near white and fades to its normal colours over several seconds, at each scene change, while the settled picture is right. → [Pending clears land before a compute dispatch](../reference/rendering.md#pending-clears-land-before-a-compute-dispatch)
- a regular lattice of dots over the ground, and the light wrong: three of four layers of an interleaved ambient occlusion array stay empty, while the draws and their bindings look right. → [`[[primitive_id]]` in a specialized Metal 4 pipeline](../reference/rendering.md#primitive_id-in-a-specialized-metal-4-pipeline)
- the start after a play session spends minutes in the prepare step ("Preparing shaders") with MTLCompilerService at 700-980% CPU, and only a few bases are new: `pipeline prepare: 30838 lines (…) in 713.42 s on 10 workers before the first frame, progress screen shown; bases 262 compiled, 8346 from the archive, 637 reused`, then `pipeline archive compacted: 14 files of bases with a vertex layout gave 8543 bases to the files just written and were removed`. → [Every start after play captures the whole pipeline archive again](../reference/rendering.md#every-start-after-play-captures-the-whole-pipeline-archive-again)
- hundreds of `[RED] background render pipeline creation failed: error: cannot commit transaction no. '50463' on '…/com.apple.gpuarchiver/gpuarchiver-…/PersistentState': MDB_MAP_FULL: Environment mapsize limit reached` (or `cannot write 'UNITSMAPPINGS' database: MDB_MAP_FULL`), then `pipeline archive: compaction left the old files (the flush failed)`; the draws of those pipelines are missing and the frame rate falls (Ocarina of Time's Kakariko Village at 30-70 ms a frame, 1,502 such lines in one session). → [Metal's archiver store filled by one flush period](../reference/rendering.md#metals-archiver-store-filled-by-one-flush-period)
- every start on an archive aborts a few seconds in, during the pipeline prewarm, with `failed assertion 'Mesh Render Pipeline Descriptor Validation meshFunction must not be nil.'` and `CRASH sig=6`; the backtrace runs `mtk_build` → `arch_lookup` → `-[AGXG14GFamilyArchive_mtlnext newRenderPipelineStateWithDescriptor:error:]` → `AGX::ArchivePipelineFactory::lookupRenderPipeline` → `-[MTLMeshRenderPipelineDescriptor validateWithDevice:error:]`. The run that wrote the archive had `Compilation failed due to an interrupted connection: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED` lines and `[RED] pipeline archive: capturing a render pipeline failed after N in its flush period`, and `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports` has `MTLCompilerService-*.ips` reports (SIGBUS in AGX's LLVM backend). → [An archive period written after the compiler service died](../reference/rendering.md#an-archive-period-written-after-the-compiler-service-died)
- the pipeline prebuild (`DM_PSO_PREBUILD=tool`, the NSP script's `[RED] dmhost exit code -11`), or a run that captures pipelines, dies with SIGSEGV and no `CRASH` line of its own. macOS's crash report has `objc_msgSend` ← `MultiLevelAirntBinaryCache::getElementAtDestination` ← `-[MTLCompiler compileFunctionRequestInternal:…]` ← `arch_capture`, and a thread on the `MTLBinaryArchive_Serialize` queue in `airntSerializeToURL` ← `arch_write_file`. → [A pipeline compile crashes while an archive period is written](../reference/rendering.md#a-pipeline-compile-crashes-while-an-archive-period-is-written)
- the pipeline prebuild dies with SIGSEGV (`[RED] dmhost exit code -11`) and no `CRASH` line of its own, sometimes seconds into the run and not on every run. macOS's crash report has `objc_msgSend` ← `MultiLevelAirntBinaryCache::getElementAtDestination` (+60) ← `-[MTLCompiler compileFunctionRequestInternal:…]` ← `AGX::Compiler::compileProgram<AGX::VertexProgramKey…>` ← `arch_capture` ← `pre_pair`, other prebuild workers wait in `-[_MTLBinaryArchive getBinaryDataForKey:reflectionType:]` under the same function, and no thread is in `airntSerializeToURL` (unlike A pipeline compile crashes while an archive period is written). → [Two captures of one shader function crash in Metal's binary cache](../reference/rendering.md#two-captures-of-one-shader-function-crash-in-metals-binary-cache)
- the start after a cold start spends minutes in the prepare step ("Preparing shaders"), with MTLCompilerService at 700-980% CPU, yet the log says no base was compiled: `pipeline prepare: 30838 lines (…) in 761.61 s on 10 workers before the first frame, progress screen shown; bases 0 compiled, 8608 from the archive, 637 reused`, then `pipeline archive compacted: 80 files of bases with a vertex layout gave 8859 bases to the files just written and were removed` (issue #1747: a cold start of 457 s, then a warm one of 309 s with 0 compiled). → [A cold start leaves a file per timer flush, and the next start compacts them all](../reference/rendering.md#a-cold-start-leaves-a-file-per-timer-flush-and-the-next-start-compacts-them-all)
- SIGTERM during startup logs `received SIGTERM: graceful exit`, then `CRASH sig=5` in `_os_object_release` from `mtl_init`. The caller can be the graphics drain or the guest's first NVN memory pool registration. → [SIGTERM during the first Metal initialization](../reference/rendering.md#sigterm-during-the-first-metal-initialization)
- shutdown after classic Metal initialization throws `NSInvalidArgumentException` in `useResidencySet:`. The exception reports `-[NSCountedSet addObject:]: attempt to insert nil`. → [A classic shutdown has no residency sets to attach](../reference/rendering.md#a-classic-shutdown-has-no-residency-sets-to-attach)
- a run without a window (headless, or `DM_PSO_PREPARE=0`) compiles the same few dozen to few hundred bases again at every start, and area loads stall on them (a draw waits in `pso_get` → `arch_capture` → `MTLCompiler`), although each run compiled them before. The run's last lines say `pipeline archive at exit: 425 pipelines not written yet, not all written in time: those left are built again at the next start (968 ms)`, or `[RED] graceful exit not done in 1.5 seconds, exiting at once` with no archive line at all. → [A run that ends in its prewarm loses what it compiled](../reference/rendering.md#a-run-that-ends-in-its-prewarm-loses-what-it-compiled)
- a start after some play spends minutes in the prepare step and the log says `pipeline archive compacted: N files of bases with a vertex layout gave M bases to the files just written and were removed`: Ocarina of Time on an M4, 9 files of play beside a compaction's 5 made the next start capture 8,543 bases again, 713 s before the first frame (PR #1880); after a cold start's 80 files, 762 s (A cold start leaves a file per timer flush, issue #1747). → [Archive lookups asked every file, so a few files made a start compile every base again](../reference/rendering.md#archive-lookups-asked-every-file-so-a-few-files-made-a-start-compile-every-base-again)
- the first start of an app with a big prewarm list sits in the prepare step ("Preparing shaders") for minutes with MTLCompilerService at full CPU: Ocarina of Time (40,154 lines) took 450-475 s on an M5 Max with an empty device archive, and the log says `pipeline prepare: … bases N compiled`. The start after it takes the bases from `pso_archive` in seconds. → [A first start builds every base of the prewarm list before the first frame](../reference/rendering.md#a-first-start-builds-every-base-of-the-prewarm-list-before-the-first-frame)
- the NSP script's `[RED] prewarm pipelines: the host's prebuild tool failed (see …/logs/pso_prewarm_mac.log); the app is packed without them and builds them on its first start`, though the tool's log ends at `pipeline prebuild: N files (… MB) for the app to bundle in …` with 0 failed; the tool's crash report (`EXC_BREAKPOINT`) has the main thread in `mtl_arch_save` under `exit` → `__cxa_finalize_ranges`, and the crashing thread on `dm.nvn.pso-archive` in `localeconv_l` ← `strtod_l` ← `libllvm-flatbuffers` ← `-[_MTL4PipelineDataSetSerializer serializeAsPipelinesScriptWithError:]` ← `prewarm_script` ← `arch_write_file`. Not every build: one of two of the same inputs. → [Captures still queued on the harvest queue left out of a waiting flush](../reference/rendering.md#captures-still-queued-on-the-harvest-queue-left-out-of-a-waiting-flush)
- every start compiles a few prewarm bases again and writes them to a new archive file, though the archive holds them: Cuphead's second and third windowed starts `bases 9 compiled, 19 from the archive` and `bases 8 compiled, 20 from the archive` of its 28 (one new `pso_archive` file each); ŌKAMI HD 2 and 3 of 14. The count varies from start to start and was 0 in some headless runs. → [Bases of byte-identical libraries compiled again at every start](../reference/rendering.md#bases-of-byte-identical-libraries-compiled-again-at-every-start)
- with Metal 4 pipelines on (`DM_PSO_MTL4=1`), the iOS app crashes before its first frame, right after `pipeline prewarm list … lines`: `CRASH sig=11 pc=objc_release+0x10`, `bt: objc_autoreleasePoolPop warm_task __warm_prepare_block_invoke`, on several prepare workers at once. The Mac runs the same pipelines. → [A specialization descriptor iOS 26.0 returns autoreleased](../reference/rendering.md#a-specialization-descriptor-ios-260-returns-autoreleased)
- with Metal's API validation on (`MTL_DEBUG_LAYER=1`), the game stops at a draw in a multisampled pass: `-[MTL4DebugRenderCommandEncoder setRenderPipelineState:]: failed assertion 'Render Command Encoder Set Render Pipeline State Validation The color sample count (4) does not match the renderPipelineState's color sample count (1) The raster sample count (4) does not match the renderPipelineState's raster sample count (1)'`. With `MTL_DEBUG_LAYER_ERROR_MODE=nslog` the game plays on and logs the pair at every such bind. Without validation nothing is wrong on screen. → [A specialized Metal 4 pipeline keeps its base's fixed state in its descriptor](../reference/rendering.md#a-specialized-metal-4-pipeline-keeps-its-bases-fixed-state-in-its-descriptor)
- a Switch 2 game calls `nvnCommandBufferEvaluateDlss` and its builder functions, which the host did not have. → [NVN2's DLSS on MetalFX](../reference/rendering.md#nvn2s-dlss-on-metalfx)
- a later pass that composites an RGBA DLSS output sees opaque alpha, whatever alpha the input colour held. RGB is correct and no validation or compilation error is logged. → [MetalFX's DLSS stand-in preserves alpha](../reference/rendering.md#metalfxs-dlss-stand-in-preserves-alpha)
- `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnDlssNetworkResize (x0=… x1=0x300 x2=0x1b0)` at the title. → [The DLSS network resized](../reference/rendering.md#the-dlss-network-resized)
