Metal System Trace's Shader Timeline shows no samples for task and mesh shaders, so such a pass looks idle there; the encoder's vertex-stage time and an overlay A/B tell. Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh pass took 4.6 ms of vertex-stage time a frame at 683x384, 0.2 ms of fragment. → [128-bit raw global loads read one word at a time](../reference/shaders.md#128-bit-raw-global-loads-read-one-word-at-a-time)
- a 64- or 128-bit load through an address in no pool (a null or stale pointer, a gap between pools) reads 0 in its first word and whatever the invocation's `gmem_vec` held in the others: the words of the previous vector load, or undefined data on the first. Before the change in 128-bit raw global loads read one word at a time, every word of such a load read 0. A shader that tests a loaded record's first word for 0 is unaffected; one that uses the other words (a bounding box, a count, a second pointer) gets garbage. → [A 64- or 128-bit raw global load in no pool reads undefined upper words](../reference/shaders.md#a-64--or-128-bit-raw-global-load-in-no-pool-reads-undefined-upper-words)
- wrong data, no error: of two `LDG.64` / `LDG.128` in one block, a word of the second reads a word of the first load's memory, and the second load's own memory is never read. → [A word of one 128-bit load read from another's vector](../reference/shaders.md#a-word-of-one-128-bit-load-read-from-anothers-vector)
- `no resolved slot number`, the shader does not load; the translation has a 32-entry texture-pool array (`fp_t2d_cb0_40000000`, handle type Direct). → [Bindless handles through a loop phi](../reference/shaders.md#bindless-handles-through-a-loop-phi)
- none in the log: texture reads come back 0. Particle meshes collapse to a point (their scale reads 0), so effects are missing or show as dark patches. The translator's own message (`Failed to find handle source for bindless access of type "textureBuffer"`) used to be dropped, because `dmsh_xlate`'s accessor had no `Log`; its messages now go into the shader's notes, marked `translator: `, and the build summary counts them apart from unanswered queries. → [Bindless reads whose handle phi also carries other values](../reference/shaders.md#bindless-reads-whose-handle-phi-also-carries-other-values)
- the shaders' bindless arrays have no slot and used not to load; the translator falls back to a 32-entry array such as `array<texture_buffer<float>, 32> vp_tb_cb0_40000000`. → [Texture slots picked at run time](../reference/shaders.md#texture-slots-picked-at-run-time)
- Ryujinx's fallback arrays (`fp_t2d_cb0_40000000`) in pixel shaders whose handle comes from the game's own uniform buffers; the shaders do not load and their draws are skipped. → [A texture chosen at run time between two uniform-buffer handles](../reference/shaders.md#a-texture-chosen-at-run-time-between-two-uniform-buffer-handles)
- `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: a texture handle the translator could not trace to one slot (profile key tex_dyn_slots off)`, then `failed to load`, and the draws are skipped: in Mario Party Superstars every character and NPC in a lit scene is a flat grey silhouette (the depth pre-pass draws, the colour pass does not), and the opening scene's pipe is grey. → [Texture handles from game memory index the pools](../reference/shaders.md#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools)
- `[RED] <key>: the translator deleted N bindless accesses (<type> N): their results read 0` for thousands of shaders, and `lost without failing: …` in the build; textures read as black (PRAGMATA: 65,241 reads in 8,630 shaders, its intro a saturated cyan). → [Handles a shader computes index the pools](../reference/shaders.md#handles-a-shader-computes-index-the-pools)
- `reflection conversion failed: B binding … has no resolved slot number` at load, then `the dispatched program has no compute shader` each time the game dispatches it; the build log has `Bindless handle taken from the one constant buffer handle among its phi inputs` for the shader. → [A handle loaded from memory on one path only](../reference/shaders.md#a-handle-loaded-from-memory-on-one-path-only)
- `[metal failed]` in the build, the compiler's message `vector component access exceeds type 'float2'` on a line that samples a `texture2d_array` with a two-component coordinate and `uint(rint(coord.z))` as its layer; the shader and its alpha test entry points do not build. → [One handle read as a texture and as an array](../reference/shaders.md#one-handle-read-as-a-texture-and-as-an-array)
- the host stops at the shader's first load: `unexpected argument buffer layout: encodedLength=640 maxAid=48`. → [An array of handles in a uniform buffer](../reference/shaders.md#an-array-of-handles-in-a-uniform-buffer)
- characters and other moving objects have almost no ambient light: lit only from where the sun reaches, black underneath, while the scenery around them is right. Nothing is red. On Kirby Air Riders a dark red Kirby on a bright course, where the game's own tutorial movie shows him bright pink. → [A table of handles longer than the array](../reference/shaders.md#a-table-of-handles-longer-than-the-array)
- a pass that samples through a table of a uniform buffer's handles takes hundreds of milliseconds on the GPU; nothing is red and the picture is right. In Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave on an M1 Pro, the lighting pass at Alcineus Bridge took 196.7 ms and the scene ran at 4 fps. → [Reads of a handle table marked through a null address](../reference/shaders.md#reads-of-a-handle-table-marked-through-a-null-address)
- a draw or dispatch that reads a table of a uniform buffer's handles samples the texture or sampler of an earlier draw: the one before an inline uniform update (`nvnCommandBufferUpdateUniformBuffer`) in the same pass, or, in a submission with compute work only, the one the previous submission read. Nothing is red. → [A handle table kept past a write to its handles](../reference/shaders.md#a-handle-table-kept-past-a-write-to-its-handles)
- `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t2ds_cb196614_10570000 has no resolved slot number`, then `failed to load`; those draws are skipped, and the scene is dark or black where it is lit by them. → [An array of uniform-buffer handles with its sampler word in c2](../reference/shaders.md#an-array-of-uniform-buffer-handles-with-its-sampler-word-in-c2)
- `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t_cb720898_2000005ASmplr has no resolved slot number`, then `failed to load`; those draws are skipped. In The Witcher 3 245 of 602 shaders failed this way, its domain shaders among them, and the screen stayed black. → [A separate sampler ID in a uniform buffer (handle type 2)](../reference/shaders.md#a-separate-sampler-id-in-a-uniform-buffer-handle-type-2)
- characters shaded black; a shader indexes a table of cube maps, and every element it reads clamps to the array's last one, which holds no handle. → [A table of handles past the array's length](../reference/shaders.md#a-table-of-handles-past-the-arrays-length)
- `shaders with a binding whose slot was not decoded 588`; shadow cascades read through `array<texture2d_array<float>, 32> fp_t2das_cb2_0`. → [An array of handles from the driver table c2](../reference/shaders.md#an-array-of-handles-from-the-driver-table-c2)
- `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t2d_cb196610_10570000 has no resolved slot number`, then, with that resolved, `… reads driver-bank locations at run time that the replay layer cannot fill: c2 dynamic index`; the shader does not load and its draws are skipped. → [Arrays of c2 handles cover the whole table, with a separate sampler word](../reference/shaders.md#arrays-of-c2-handles-cover-the-whole-table-with-a-separate-sampler-word)
- characters and props glowing white, about 10⁴ too bright, in DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined's opening cutscene only, while walls and gameplay look right. → [A texture slot that shares its c2 word with an image slot](../reference/shaders.md#a-texture-slot-that-shares-its-c2-word-with-an-image-slot)
- `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader … declares slot 128 as Metal type 2, texture ID … is type 7 (NVN target 2, …); bound as is`, for a shader whose MSL has a `texture3d` and a `texture2d` on the same register and only calls `get_width` / `get_height` on the `texture2d`. → [A size query on a 3D texture gets a 2D slot](../reference/shaders.md#a-size-query-on-a-3d-texture-gets-a-2d-slot)
- `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: 66 bindings, over the limit 64`, then `failed to load`, and the draws with it are dropped (a black map). → [More than 64 bindings in one shader](../reference/shaders.md#more-than-64-bindings-in-one-shader)
- the host stops at a shader's first load: `FATAL shader … has an unexpected argument buffer layout: encodedLength=88 maxAid=8` (crash in `c1_store` / `pull_load`), with a storage buffer or image missing from its reflection json. → [Argument buffer members with attributes after their id](../reference/shaders.md#argument-buffer-members-with-attributes-after-their-id)
- the replay put a texture resource ID where the shader wanted an address; with the old 64 KB dummy buffer, pointers in the game's objects and in the host's program objects got their upper 4 bytes replaced and the game or the replay crashed, or `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`, after which macOS ignores the process's command buffers (GitHub issue #101). Later, `[RED] image atomics on a block-linear texture … not followed, the atomics go to a scratch buffer`, and compute shaders with atomics on a 3D image did not build (`array subscript is not an integer`). → [Image atomics](../reference/shaders.md#image-atomics)
- `image atomics: unsupported or overflowing IMG extent` at the first dispatch, despite a valid four-byte counter buffer. → [Buffer image reflection keeps its texture type](../reference/shaders.md#buffer-image-reflection-keeps-its-texture-type)
- `[RED] image atomics on a texture Metal cannot run them on`, and whatever the shader computes through them is lost: its writes go to a sink. → [Image atomics on a 32-bit colour texture](../reference/shaders.md#image-atomics-on-a-32-bit-colour-texture)
- Metal shader validation reports invalid image-atomic stores even with an image-sized scratch buffer; without validation, GPU page faults can freeze rendering. → [Bounds of image atomics](../reference/shaders.md#bounds-of-image-atomics)
- `NotSupportedException: Ampere 59f at …: SURED form 0110e400` in the shader build, then the scene's 3D picture black behind a UI that draws: `[RED] shader … (compute) failed to load` and `[RED] the dispatched program has no compute shader`. A texture dump shows the visibility target (RG32Uint) all zero. → [64-bit image max and min (SURED.MAX.U64)](../reference/shaders.md#64-bit-image-max-and-min-suredmaxu64)
- the GPU hangs (Metal command-buffer timeouts, `GPU access revoked`, exit 70; on the Mac WindowServer can lose its watchdog check-in and the session restarts) or a draw reads garbage, where a shader reads a uniform slot further than the game bound it. No `[RED]` line. → [Reads past a uniform binding's size](../reference/shaders.md#reads-past-a-uniform-bindings-size)
- on an M1 Pro (macOS 26), blue, green and white blocks for one or two frames on trees that dither-fade (OCTOPATH TRAVELER's S'warkii, walking behind a tree); a texture dump shows NaN pixels in the scene colour and holes in the normal G-buffer target, which depth of field spreads into blocks. Not seen on an iPhone or on newer Macs. On every GPU, a branch for each uniform word a shader reads. → [Bounded uniform reads written as select](../reference/shaders.md#bounded-uniform-reads-written-as-select)
- a shader reads another resource's bytes where it indexes a storage buffer past the memory the game mapped there. No `[RED]` line. PRAGMATA's R53 hangar (after the tram ride, "Contact Earth from the Comms Tower") washes out in pink, green and white as you enter and on the stairs up to the upper deck, and it fades when the camera stays still; on the console the hangar is dark blue-grey. → [Reads past a storage buffer bound by address](../reference/shaders.md#reads-past-a-storage-buffer-bound-by-address)
- the pipeline prebuild stops at the same line on every start (`pipeline prebuild: 7000 of 20740 pairs, 1987 built, 5013 already archived, 0 left to the draws, 0 failed`), behind *Preparing pipelines* or, with `DM_PSO_PREPARE=0`, as a black screen while the sound plays on; the NSP script's `[7/7] Pipeline prebuild` does not end (`dmhost` at 0% CPU, `MTLCompilerService` at 100%), and `MTLCompilerService` crash reports (`EXC_CRASH`, `llvm::report_bad_alloc_error`) come every 6 to 10 minutes. The bases built since the last file are never written, so the next start builds them again and stops at the same pair. → [A compile that does not return stalls every flush and draw](../reference/shaders.md#a-compile-that-does-not-return-stalls-every-flush-and-draw)
- a self-contained Mac app (`--platform mac-app`) logs `pipeline prebuild: no .../Resources/dm/shader_pairs.tsv, nothing to build` although every shader library is there; it draws no hybrid stand-ins, and each pipeline seen for the first time compiles on the submit thread (frames of 100–550 ms). → [Shader-pair tables in a Mac app](../reference/shaders.md#shader-pair-tables-in-a-mac-app)
- a truncated `.mtl4a` reaches `MTL4ArchiveImpl::loadFromURL`; the bundle reports coverage with `0 of 0 probed bases found`; rebuilt shader libraries reuse an archive of their old binaries; or packing fails with `pso_offline: no base to build` when every eligible pair has function constants, or when the profile's `shader_pairs.tsv` holds only its header (`--platform mac-app`, GitHub issue #338). → [Offline pipeline archive reuse and fallback](../reference/shaders.md#offline-pipeline-archive-reuse-and-fallback)
- a gray screen for several seconds at startup, before the pipeline progress screen, on a Mac whose GPU has an archive in the app: `pipeline archive bundled with the app: .../pso/applegpu_g16s.mtl4a, 4405.6 MB, opened in 7978.8 ms`. The same app starts fast on a Mac whose GPU has none, although that Mac's own `pso_archive` folder holds twice as much (38 files, 8,880 MB, about 1.4 s on an M1 Pro). → [A bundled pipeline archive read a page at a time when it opens](../reference/shaders.md#a-bundled-pipeline-archive-read-a-page-at-a-time-when-it-opens)
- an unchanged NSP spends minutes in `shader split` after a shader-chain update, on both Mac and iOS builds, although the extracted package is identical. → [Shader extraction reruns after translation-only updates](../reference/shaders.md#shader-extraction-reruns-after-translation-only-updates)
- rebuilding an unchanged NSP after a shader-chain update runs `metal-tt` for minutes even when all referenced Metal libraries and pipeline settings remain identical. → [Offline pipeline archive rebuilt after a chain-only update](../reference/shaders.md#offline-pipeline-archive-rebuilt-after-a-chain-only-update)
- the host logs `pipeline archive bundled with the app: made for ... this run needs ...`, naming different depths, and pipeline bases compile during play despite a successful archive build. There need not be a `[RED]` line. → [Offline pipeline archive and profile depth disagree](../reference/shaders.md#offline-pipeline-archive-and-profile-depth-disagree)
- `pipeline archive bundled with the app: this system cannot open …/applegpu_<gpu>.mtl4a (deployment target for architecture … found in archive … is not compatible with current OS …): the bases are built on the device`. On macOS 26.6 with Xcode 27.1, `t_pso_bundle` failed 10 checks, from `bundle: opened` on, and a Mac app packed there compiled every base on its first start, as without the archive. → [Offline pipeline archive made for another major version](../reference/shaders.md#offline-pipeline-archive-made-for-another-major-version)
- after a major macOS update, every start logs `[RED] pipeline archive file …/pso_archive/p00042.mtl4a not usable (deployment target for architecture … is not compatible with current OS …): its bases are built again`, once per file written on the old system, but no `pipeline prebuild:` line; the game then stalls for one to several seconds whenever a scene shows bases it had before (frames of 1–7 s talking to a character or when bombs go off). The red lines and the missing prebuild come back on every later start; a base a draw built is captured again, so the stalls move on to the scenes not yet played since the update. → [Pipeline archive files left behind by a major system update](../reference/shaders.md#pipeline-archive-files-left-behind-by-a-major-system-update)
- after a shader input changes, the Mac app build recompiles the affected library but reports `prewarm pipelines: reused (inputs unchanged)`, retaining pipelines made with the previous shader functions. → [Mac prewarm pipelines reused after a packed shader library changes](../reference/shaders.md#mac-prewarm-pipelines-reused-after-a-packed-shader-library-changes)
- `pso_offline.py` holds about 1.8 GB while `metal-tt` builds an archive of 140,036 bases, and the system's memory view shows the build far larger than that (180.5 GB) before `metal-tt` fails or is stopped. → [Python memory during offline pipeline archive preparation](../reference/shaders.md#python-memory-during-offline-pipeline-archive-preparation)
- the offline archive stops with only `applegpu-nt command failed`, sometimes after `Max Unstruct ID` lines (a note, not the failure); the macOS kernel log reports `no paging space action` and kills `air-nt` as the largest compressed process. → [Bounded groups for the offline pipeline compiler](../reference/shaders.md#bounded-groups-for-the-offline-pipeline-compiler)
- `[RED] pso_offline: metal-tt failed for applegpu_… (exit 1): LLVM ERROR: Failed to encode.`, and the NSP script's `[RED] pipeline archive: pso_offline.py failed …; the app is packed without one and builds its pipeline bases on its first start`. On a run from the build folder: `[RED] pipeline prebuild: the base of … failed: Compilation failed due to an interrupted connection: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED`, a `MTLCompilerService` crash report whose stack ends in a fatal error under `llvm::MCObjectStreamer::emitInstruction` (`Error message SHA256: 01fbda05…`, the hash of `Failed to encode.`), and the compiles in flight when the service ends failing with it. → [A base the GPU compiler cannot encode cost the whole offline archive](../reference/shaders.md#a-base-the-gpu-compiler-cannot-encode-cost-the-whole-offline-archive)
- `--platform mac-app` or the iPhone build stops at its pipeline archive stage, after every earlier stage has finished: `[RED] pso_offline: metal-tt failed for applegpu_<gpu> (exit 1)`, then `pso_offline.py exit code 1`. → [A failed pipeline archive stops the whole build](../reference/shaders.md#a-failed-pipeline-archive-stops-the-whole-build)
- `[RED] pso_offline: metal-tt failed for applegpu_<gpu> (exit 1)` with only `applegpu-nt command failed` in `metal-tt.<arch>.log`. → [Offline pipeline archive on a full disk](../reference/shaders.md#offline-pipeline-archive-on-a-full-disk)
- a game without a recorded prewarm list (a provisional profile) stalls at its first dispatches while Metal compiles their compute pipelines. The Mac app's pipeline archive held render bases only (`archive.tsv`: `bases`), whatever the shader map listed. → [Compute pipelines compiled at first use](../reference/shaders.md#compute-pipelines-compiled-at-first-use)
- a compute pipeline the archive bundled with the app does not hold (no bundle, as in a headless boot or a build without `pso_offline.py`; a shader outside the shader map; an OS update the bundle's binaries do not survive) was compiled again at its first dispatch of every start the system's shader cache had dropped it, and the device's own pipeline archive (`pso_archive`) never held one: `compute pipelines so far: 0 from the archive bundled with the app, N built` start after start. → [Compute pipelines outside the bundled archive built at every start](../reference/shaders.md#compute-pipelines-outside-the-bundled-archive-built-at-every-start)
- a manifest contains native mesh rows, but the game still logs `task / mesh pipeline: … required at frame` for the same pipelines after preparation. → [A learned mesh pipeline is built again after prewarm](../reference/shaders.md#a-learned-mesh-pipeline-is-built-again-after-prewarm)
- the first sight of a scene drawn with task and mesh shaders freezes for seconds on a cold Metal cache, on every start once the system's shader cache has dropped the pipelines, although the prewarm finished: `task / mesh pipeline: task … mesh … fragment …, N ms required at frame F`. Before ea0c98df the draw was left out instead, and the foliage stayed missing or white until the pipeline was ready. → [Native mesh pipelines compiled at their first draw](../reference/shaders.md#native-mesh-pipelines-compiled-at-their-first-draw)
- `[RED] pipeline prewarm: seed list does not exist: …/pso_manifest.tsv` at every start, no `pipeline prewarm list …/pso_user.tsv` line, and the same compile stalls every session in places already visited, while `pso_user.tsv` keeps growing; the pipeline archive gains files every start and is never compacted (Donkey Kong Bananza: 88 files, 7 s to open them). → [The device's prewarm list unread without a seed list](../reference/shaders.md#the-devices-prewarm-list-unread-without-a-seed-list)
- on an iPhone the next start still waits on, or draws with stand-ins for, the pipelines the last session asked for last. Ocarina of Time on an iPhone 18 Pro Max asked for about 400 pipelines in Castle Town and finished about 60 a minute; whatever was still unbuilt when the run ended was not in `pso_user.tsv`, so the next start's prepare step did not build it. HookLink's measurement; we have not reproduced it. → [A pipeline a hybrid draw stood in for listed only once built](../reference/shaders.md#a-pipeline-a-hybrid-draw-stood-in-for-listed-only-once-built)
- the first frame that draws with new programs loads their shader libraries on the submit thread (`[longframe] ... shader libraries N, X ms`): tens of milliseconds with the files cached, seconds when the drive is slow. → [Shader libraries loaded when the program is made](../reference/shaders.md#shader-libraries-loaded-when-the-program-is-made)
- at boot, `CRASH sig=5 … pc=_dispatch_once_wait.cold.1` right after the `pipeline bases so far` line, with `bt: pso_prebuild __shmap_load_block_invoke … mtl_prog_prefetch h_nvnProgramSetShaders`. → [The prepare step inside the shader map's once](../reference/shaders.md#the-prepare-step-inside-the-shader-maps-once)
- after an IPA packed without `_bp.metallib` ran once with `DM_PSO_MTL4=1`, an app with those libraries still stalls on new pipelines: `pipelines up to frame` lines show `draws with a hybrid pipeline 0` and `draws that waited: pulling base not made yet`, and the log has no `pipeline prebuild:` line. The run before it logged `[RED] pulling library of … not usable` and `pipeline prebuild done: … 19838 left to the draws`. → [A prebuild stamp written without the pulling libraries](../reference/shaders.md#a-prebuild-stamp-written-without-the-pulling-libraries)
- the user cache folder (`getconf DARWIN_USER_CACHE_DIR`) fills with `gpuarchiver-*` folders, one per pipeline archive flush: in `com.apple.gpuarchiver/` for a host from a build, in `org.nativenavi.<title ID>/com.apple.gpuarchiver/` for a built Mac app. Each holds an LMDB store (`PersistentState`) and captured binaries, 0.3–5 MB at first (456 folders, 2.5 GB after two days), later up to about 127 MB (an M4 Pro, 2026-10-29: 19 GB in 423 folders after a day of Mac runs and regression checks); why they grew is **open**. A built app's folders were never removed: Ocarina of Time's app had 4,016 of them, about 285 GB, and one Mac's apps 345.8 GiB in all; each start of Ocarina's Castle Town route left 6 more, 560 MB (issue #2680). A later compile can fail with `cannot open '…/gpuarchiver-…/PersistentState': No such file or directory`. → [Metal's build folders left by pipeline archive flushes](../reference/shaders.md#metals-build-folders-left-by-pipeline-archive-flushes)
- a previously working game exits with 139 on every start after pipeline preparation; `dispatch_retain(NULL)` is called by `-[_MTLBinaryArchive materializeAirNTFromOnlineForSlice:entryList:]` under `airntSerializeToURL` and `arch_flush` (issue #342). → [A compaction flush that crashes, then crashes every start](../reference/shaders.md#a-compaction-flush-that-crashes-then-crashes-every-start)
- identical render pipelines compile repeatedly under different keys, lengthening startup prewarm and adding pipeline misses during play. → [Pipeline keys omit unused colour state](../reference/shaders.md#pipeline-keys-omit-unused-colour-state)
- a vertex shader whose pairs changed was restored unpadded from `cas/`. → [Shader cache key covering the pairs](../reference/shaders.md#shader-cache-key-covering-the-pairs)
- an edit to a shader build helper reaches no library: `shader cache: hit` for every shader, and the old MSL is filed under the new chain version. An edit to `dmsh_meshstage.py` did not change the chain version either. → [Shader cache key missing helper modules](../reference/shaders.md#shader-cache-key-missing-helper-modules)
- a full build reports successful shaders, but `metal-tt` rejects its pipeline archive with fragment inputs not written by the vertex shader. Fifteen cached vertices lacked default outputs in DRAGON QUEST I & II. → [Partial vertex builds missing paired pixel sources](../reference/shaders.md#partial-vertex-builds-missing-paired-pixel-sources)
- a repeated iOS-only shader build crashes with `FileNotFoundError` for `<key>_b.json` in the Mac output directory; requesting only Mac libraries with `IOSOUT` set still compiles iOS side libraries. → [Target selection keeps the shader analysis files in the cache](../reference/shaders.md#target-selection-keeps-the-shader-analysis-files-in-the-cache)
- after `scripts/build_app.py <NSP folder> --platform mac`, a plain (iOS) run of the script on the same folder succeeds, but the Mac run command the first build printed then logs hundreds of `[RED] failed to load shader <key>_b: cannot open …/shader/cache/b-<chain>/<key>_b.metallib` lines, until `--platform mac` is run again. → [An iOS build after a Mac-only build kept no Mac libraries](../reference/shaders.md#an-ios-build-after-a-mac-only-build-kept-no-mac-libraries)
- the build folder of a game (`<id>_build/shader/`) gains about 2 GB for Breath of the Wild with every upstream update that changes the shader chain: after two updates `shader/cache` held `b-157e1b96c8da` (2.1 GB) and `b-a78f966a7b38` (2.3 GB) besides the current one, and the next build added a third. → [Old shader library folders grew a game's build folder with each chain change](../reference/shaders.md#old-shader-library-folders-grew-a-games-build-folder-with-each-chain-change)
- `<id>_build/shader/cache/cas/shader/` holds several entries for each shader, and grows by a full set with every upstream update that changes the shader chain. Tears of the Kingdom (seedds/nxnative#1033): 172,014 entries after six builds for 28,669 shaders, 11.85 GB of `.metal` and `.json` that no build read; the test Mac's copy held 934,955, of which the prune (dry run) found 906,286 unused: 70.2 GB of sources and reflection, scanned in 18 minutes on an external SSD. → [Old per-shader cache entries grew a game's build folder with each chain change](../reference/shaders.md#old-per-shader-cache-entries-grew-a-games-build-folder-with-each-chain-change)
- the NSP script's Mac or iOS shader step for a game with very large shader packages runs at one or two shaders a second whatever `J` is. Its Python process sits at one full core, mostly system time, while few `metal` compiles run. Pokémon Pokopia (4.4 GB of packages, 131,426 shaders): players reported ETAs of 20 to 200 hours (seedds/nxnative#1036). → [A shader build that keeps its packages in memory runs one worker at a time](../reference/shaders.md#a-shader-build-that-keeps-its-packages-in-memory-runs-one-worker-at-a-time)
- the Mac or iOS shader step of a game with a large map still takes many hours with every worker busy, and a `metal -c` of a translated shader takes about 0.7 s of CPU, nearly all in the front end. Optimising adds about 0.1 s, and an empty shader takes 0.025 s. Pokémon Pokopia's 131,426 shaders: about 9 hours on an M4 Pro (seedds/nxnative#1036). → [Each Metal compile instantiates Metal's texture classes again](../reference/shaders.md#each-metal-compile-instantiates-metals-texture-classes-again)
- the shader step of a game with large translated sources has fewer Metal compiles running than `J` asks for, and `dmsh_build.py`'s own process stays busy on one core between them. Pokémon Pokopia's sources average 220 KB, and the builder spent about 0.12 s of Python on each shader, all but its system calls under the GIL, which alone would have come to about 4.2 hours of its full build. → [Rewrites that try every character of a large source](../reference/shaders.md#rewrites-that-try-every-character-of-a-large-source)
- a cold shader step leaves about half the cores idle whatever `J` is, and `dmsh_build.py`'s own process stays at one full core from start to end. Ocarina of Time's whole Mac map, 42,848 shaders: 3,506 s on an M3 Max with 16 cores (`J=16`, the build at 104% CPU, 4 to 5 cores busy in all), 3,002 s on an M5 Max with 18 cores. → [A shader build's Python finishes one shader at a time](../reference/shaders.md#a-shader-builds-python-finishes-one-shader-at-a-time)
- the Mac shader step stops a few seconds after it starts, every time, with `concurrent.futures.process.BrokenProcessPool` (since 8b55ebb3; the `[RED] a shader build worker process stopped` line once the build prints it), and `DMSH_PROCS=0` builds. The worker's crash report: `SIGSEGV` in `_os_log_preferences_refresh` ← `os_signpost_enabled` ← `os_signpost_id_make_with_pointer` ← `openDatabase` (`libsqlite3.dylib`) ← Python's `sqlite3`. Only with a Python whose `_sqlite3` module links Apple's `/usr/lib/libsqlite3.dylib` (`otool -L` on `_sqlite3.__file__`): Xcode's, and by the reports some Python 3.12+ builds; Homebrew's Python has its own SQLite and builds. → [A forked shader worker dies opening the compile cache](../reference/shaders.md#a-forked-shader-worker-dies-opening-the-compile-cache)
- two builds of the same shaders give different `_bl` or `_bf` libraries for some keys, of the same size, 46 bytes apart, while their MSL is the same. A byte comparison of two library folders reports them, and `regress.py` can report them as changed variants. → [A library that depends on its source's file name](../reference/shaders.md#a-library-that-depends-on-its-sources-file-name)
- the Mac or iOS shader step of a game with many pixel shaders spends most of its Metal compiles on libraries that few draws load: Pokémon Pokopia's whole map took 366,805 compiles, about 57% of them for `_ba` and `_bl`, and its runs opened none of the 5,775 of each. → [One compile for a pixel shader's side entry points](../reference/shaders.md#one-compile-for-a-pixel-shaders-side-entry-points)
- the first Mac shader build of a title, with an empty output directory and CAS and no keys, takes tens of minutes while `J` Metal workers stay busy. On an Apple M2 MacBook Pro at `J=8`, Mario Kart 8 Deluxe took 22:46 (1366 s) and Super Mario Odyssey 30:02 (1802 s). → [Cold Mac shader builds compile each pixel shader more than once](../reference/shaders.md#cold-mac-shader-builds-compile-each-pixel-shader-more-than-once)
- a cold Mac or iOS shader build spends most of its Metal compiles on vertex shaders' copies of one body. Breath of the Wild's 19,592 shaders took 21,552 compiles, 2,405 of the build's 3,692 CPU seconds (240 s of wall on an M5 Pro at `J=18`): 839 s of that went to pulling libraries and 109 s to shadow-input libraries, each a second and third compile of a body `<key>_b` already holds three times. → [One compile for a vertex shader's libraries](../reference/shaders.md#one-compile-for-a-vertex-shaders-libraries)
- startup tasks can prepare the same shader more than once, even though only one object enters the warm dictionary. A measured Tears of the Kingdom startup avoided 320 duplicate preparations; this is not a steady-frame-rate claim. → [Concurrent warm tasks prepare each shader once](../reference/shaders.md#concurrent-warm-tasks-prepare-each-shader-once)
- the Mac's pipeline prebuild (`DM_PSO_PREBUILD=tool`, the NSP script's `pso_prebuild.log`) runs on far fewer workers than the Mac has: `1 workers` on an M6, where Metroid Prime Remastered's 20,233 pairs took 1,725 s and Link's Awakening's 17,742 took 667 s, and 4 on an M5 Pro, where Donkey Kong Bananza's 21,225 took 6.5 to 7.5 minutes. → [A prebuild pool sized from the Super cores](../reference/shaders.md#a-prebuild-pool-sized-from-the-super-cores)
- `fragmentFunction main0 cannot be used to build a pipeline state` (a Metal validation assertion). → [Function constants specialized at load](../reference/shaders.md#function-constants-specialized-at-load)
- with Metal 4 pipelines on (the Mac; on iOS `pso_mtl4 on` or `DM_PSO_MTL4=1`), Apple's performance HUD says `Compiled 2 shaders. Total compilation time is 102.70ms. Consider adopting MTL4Compiler for more fine grained compilations` during play, when a shader with function constants loads. → [Functions with function constants specialized by the pipeline, not the library](../reference/shaders.md#functions-with-function-constants-specialized-by-the-pipeline-not-the-library)
- the picture bright green and white from some scene on, in runs with pipelines compiled on demand (prewarm lists off or short) and in none with `DM_NVN_PREDICT=0`. → [The precompile look-ahead restores the scissor](../reference/shaders.md#the-precompile-look-ahead-restores-the-scissor)
- a crash in `-[AGXG16XFamilyRenderContext_mtlnext setRenderPipelineState:]` from `draw_` with direct Metal encoding. The pass dump shows a pipeline miss and an end in `gpu_read_wait` before the draw reaches its pipeline bind. → [Pipeline look-ahead must leave future attachments unresolved](../reference/shaders.md#pipeline-look-ahead-must-leave-future-attachments-unresolved)
- on entering a new area or opening a menu for the first time, with no archive or prewarm list covering it, frames of 0.2–2 s whose `[longframe]` line is nearly all pipeline work, and `draws that waited: pulling base not made yet` in the `pipelines up to frame` lines. → [The look-ahead hands every later draw's pipeline to the background](../reference/shaders.md#the-look-ahead-hands-every-later-draws-pipeline-to-the-background)
- a GPU frame capture (`DM_GPU_CAPTURE`, or Xcode with `MTL_CAPTURE_ENABLED=1`) fails with `Capturing Render pipeline specialization is not supported`, or the capture layer crashes on the pipeline archive; a capture of the `MTLDevice` is refused. Before the fix below asked Metal, a run that did not capture but had `MTL_CAPTURE_ENABLED=0` or `DM_GPU_CAPTURE=0` left in its environment logged `hybrid pipelines off`, and every pipeline it had not built yet stalled its frame (an M1 Ocarina of Time session: 467 stalls of about 170 ms, one 84 s freeze with 255 pipelines in a submission). → [GPU frame captures need pipelines built through the device](../reference/shaders.md#gpu-frame-captures-need-pipelines-built-through-the-device)
- the first boot sat on the loading clock for about a minute, the CPU idle, with every file read done after 12 s. → [A cold Metal cache looks like a long load](../reference/shaders.md#a-cold-metal-cache-looks-like-a-long-load)
- missing geometry or bright patches just after loading, which disappear as background pipeline compilation finishes. → [Native mesh draws omitted while their pipeline compiles](../reference/shaders.md#native-mesh-draws-omitted-while-their-pipeline-compiles)
- Apple's performance HUD (`DM_METAL_HUD=1`) says `Compiled 2 shaders. Total compilation time is 102.70ms. Consider adopting MTL4Compiler for more fine grained compilations` during play, with a dip in the frame-rate graph at that moment, for example when a new room first issues a scissored clear. → [The host's own shaders compiled from source at run time](../reference/shaders.md#the-hosts-own-shaders-compiled-from-source-at-run-time)
- a raw global memory read of a memory pool made after the shader's arguments were encoded gets 0, though the pool exists when the GPU runs it; a task shader that reads both ends of a range this way loops `(0 - 1) >> 5` times and hangs the GPU. The memory report's `raw pool table words at commit` line counts the words that were out of date. → [Raw pool table words that predate the pools](../reference/shaders.md#raw-pool-table-words-that-predate-the-pools)
- integer divisions by a constant cost GPU time that the compiler should have folded away. The MSL holds calls such as `(spvMulExtended<_2, uint>(uint(_5), 16u))._m1`, and spirv-cross defines `spvMulExtended` as `[[clang::optnone]]`, so each product stays a call to an unoptimized function. → [Extended products behind spirv-cross's optnone helper](../reference/shaders.md#extended-products-behind-spirv-crosss-optnone-helper)
- integer results in a shader come out wrong where the game's code relies on signed arithmetic wrapping: a packed field unpacked by hand, an overflow test, a sign extension. In Ocarina of Time, one section of an otherwise dark wall in the Deku Tree (Gohma's room) was lit brightly and shimmered while the camera moved. Only models drawn with task / mesh shaders were affected; the vertex-shader models beside them were lit correctly. → [Signed integer overflow in translated shaders](../reference/shaders.md#signed-integer-overflow-in-translated-shaders)
- building a render pipeline with one pixel shader ends Apple's compiler service: `[RED] pipeline prebuild: the base of … failed: Compilation failed due to an interrupted connection: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED. This error occurred after multiple retries.`, the pair in `prebuild_failed.tsv`, and a `MTLCompilerService` crash report (`EXC_CRASH`, `SIGABRT`) whose stack is `fatalErrorHandler` ← `llvm::report_fatal_error` ← `llvm::MCObjectStreamer::emitInstruction` ← `AGCLLVMUserObject::compile` ← `AGCModule<AGX::Impl::FragmentProgramKey>::compileLLVMObject`. The shader translates and its library builds; every vertex shader paired with it fails the same way, and the compiles in flight when the service ends fail with it. Offline, the toolchain's `applegpu-nt` says `LLVM ERROR: Failed to encode.` for the same library. → [A jump Apple's GPU compiler cannot encode (a tested bit 0's mask)](../reference/shaders.md#a-jump-apples-gpu-compiler-cannot-encode-a-tested-bit-0s-mask)
- after a first start's prepare step built the shader pairs' bases into `pso_archive/p*.mtl4a`, the same run still has draws that wait with `pulling base not made yet` and builds those bases again in the background (`hy_base_kick`); the next start has none of it. → [Prebuilt pipeline archives not opened during the first run](../reference/shaders.md#prebuilt-pipeline-archives-not-opened-during-the-first-run)
- the app stops at a pipeline build with SIGTRAP ("unbalanced call to dispatch_group_leave") or SIGSEGV at 0x30 in the block of `createVertexProgramVariant` (Apple's G16X driver), a few seconds into a cold start or while the prewarm runs. Three Ocarina of Time runs, the first at 18 s. → [Apple's driver crashes when threads build pipelines of one vertex function at once](../reference/shaders.md#apples-driver-crashes-when-threads-build-pipelines-of-one-vertex-function-at-once)

## Game state

- "stuck" at a screen with one draw per frame. → [Most draws are in command buffers replayed later](../reference/game-state.md#most-draws-are-in-command-buffers-replayed-later)
